# THE COMPANIES ACT, 2013

> **NOTICE: AI-written reconstruction, NOT the official text.** Each section is restated in plain words from general knowledge (written 2026-10-09), covering amendments up to about 2020 plus some later changes. Wording, figures, penalty amounts and anything marked [verify] must be checked against the official Act on indiacode.nic.in before relying on it. Chapter XXIA (Producer Companies, ss.378M-378ZU) is the least reliable part. This is legal information, not legal advice.


> Restated summary for reference. This is not the statutory text. Penalty provisions are shown as they stand after the amendments of 2015, 2017, 2019 and 2020 to the extent known. Items marked `[verify]` should be checked against the official text.

## CHAPTER I — PRELIMINARY

### Section 1. Short title, extent, commencement and application

- **(1) Short title:** the Companies Act, 2013.
- **(2) Extent:** the whole of India.
- **(3) Commencement:** Section 1 came into force on the date of enactment (29 August 2013) [verify]. The remaining provisions come into force on dates the Central Government notifies in the Official Gazette, and different dates may be set for different provisions. A reference in any provision to "the commencement of this Act" means the commencement of that provision.
- **(4) Application:** the Act applies to:
  - (a) companies incorporated under this Act or under any previous company law;
  - (b) insurance companies, except where the Act is inconsistent with the Insurance Act, 1938 or the Insurance Regulatory and Development Authority Act, 1999;
  - (c) banking companies, except where the Act is inconsistent with the Banking Regulation Act, 1949;
  - (d) companies engaged in generating or supplying electricity, except where the Act is inconsistent with the Electricity Act, 2003;
  - (e) any other company governed by a special Act, except where the Act is inconsistent with that special Act;
  - (f) any body corporate incorporated by any Act in force, as the Central Government notifies, subject to the exceptions, modifications or adaptations in the notification.

### Section 2. Definitions

Unless the context requires otherwise:

- **(1) abridged prospectus** — a memorandum with the key features of a prospectus, in the form SEBI specifies by regulations.
- **(2) accounting standards** — the accounting standards for companies, or a class of companies, referred to in section 133.
- **(3) alter / alteration** — includes making additions, omissions and substitutions.
- **(4) Appellate Tribunal** — the National Company Law Appellate Tribunal set up under section 410.
- **(5) articles** — the company's articles of association as originally framed or as altered, or as applied under any previous company law or this Act.
- **(6) associate company** — a company over which another company has significant influence, but which is not its subsidiary. This includes a joint venture company.
  - Explanation (a): "significant influence" means control of at least 20% of the total voting power, or control of or participation in business decisions under an agreement. (The 2017 amendment replaced "share capital" with "voting power".)
  - Explanation (b): "joint venture" means a joint arrangement in which the parties with joint control have rights to the net assets of the arrangement.
- **(7) auditing standards** — the standards on auditing, or any addendum to them, referred to in section 143(10).
- **(8) authorised capital / nominal capital** — the maximum share capital the memorandum allows the company to have.
- **(9) banking company** — a banking company as defined in section 5(c) of the Banking Regulation Act, 1949.
- **(10) Board of Directors / Board** — the directors of a company acting together as a body.
- **(11) body corporate / corporation** — includes a company incorporated outside India. It excludes (i) a co-operative society registered under any co-operative societies law and (ii) any other body corporate (not a company under this Act) that the Central Government notifies.
- **(12) book and paper / book or paper** — includes books of account, deeds, vouchers, writings, documents, minutes and registers, whether kept on paper or electronically.
- **(13) books of account** — records of: all money received and spent, and what it was for; all sales and purchases of goods and services; the company's assets and liabilities; and, for companies covered by section 148, the prescribed cost items.
- **(14) branch office** — any establishment described as a branch by the company.
- **(15) called-up capital** — the part of the share capital that has been called for payment.
- **(16) charge** — an interest or lien created on the property or assets of a company, or any of its undertakings, or both, as security. It includes a mortgage.
- **(17) chartered accountant** — a member of the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India who holds a certificate of practice under section 6(1) of the Chartered Accountants Act, 1949.
- **(18) Chief Executive Officer** — an officer the company has appointed as its CEO.
- **(19) Chief Financial Officer** — a person the company has appointed as its CFO.
- **(20) company** — a company incorporated under this Act or under any previous company law.
- **(21) company limited by guarantee** — a company whose memorandum limits each member's liability to the amount the member undertakes to contribute to the company's assets if it is wound up.
- **(22) company limited by shares** — a company whose memorandum limits each member's liability to any amount still unpaid on the shares the member holds.
- **(23) company liquidator** — the person appointed by the Tribunal as company liquidator in a winding up under this Act (section 275) [verify].
- **(24) company secretary / secretary** — a company secretary as defined in section 2(1)(c) of the Company Secretaries Act, 1980, appointed by a company to perform the functions of a company secretary under this Act.
- **(25) company secretary in practice** — a company secretary treated as being in practice under section 2(2) of the Company Secretaries Act, 1980.
- **(26) contributory** — a person liable to contribute to the company's assets if it is wound up. Explanation: a holder of fully paid-up shares counts as a contributory but has no liability to contribute; they keep only their rights as a contributory.
- **(27) control** — includes the right to appoint a majority of the directors, or to control management or policy decisions. This right may be exercised by one person, or by several persons acting individually or together, directly or indirectly, including through shareholding, management rights, shareholders' agreements, voting agreements or in any other way.
- **(28) Cost Accountant** — a cost accountant as defined in section 2(1)(b) of the Cost and Works Accountants Act, 1959, holding a valid certificate of practice under section 6(1) of that Act.
- **(29) court** — means, as applicable: the High Court with jurisdiction over the registered office; a district court empowered by the Central Government with jurisdiction over the registered office; a Sessions Court; a Special Court set up under section 435; or a Metropolitan Magistrate or Judicial Magistrate of the First Class.
- **(30) debenture** — includes debenture stock, bonds and any other instrument evidencing a debt, whether or not it creates a charge on the company's assets. Proviso (2017): this excludes the instruments in Chapter III-D of the RBI Act, 1934 and any other instrument the Central Government prescribes in consultation with the RBI.
- **(31) deposit** — any money received by a company as a deposit, a loan or in any other form. It excludes the categories prescribed in consultation with the RBI.
- **(32) depository** — a depository as defined in section 2(1)(e) of the Depositories Act, 1996.
- **(33) derivative** — a derivative as defined in section 2(ac) of the Securities Contracts (Regulation) Act, 1956.
- **(34) director** — a director appointed to the Board of a company.
- **(35) dividend** — includes any interim dividend.
- **(36) document** — includes summons, notices, requisitions, orders, declarations, forms and registers, whether issued, sent or kept on paper or electronically.
- **(37) employees' stock option** — an option given to the directors, officers or employees of a company, its holding company or its subsidiaries. It gives them the benefit of, or the right to buy or subscribe for, the company's shares at a later date at a price fixed in advance.
- **(38) expert** — includes an engineer, a valuer, a chartered accountant, a company secretary, a cost accountant and any other person legally authorised to issue a certificate.
- **(39) financial institution** — includes a scheduled bank and any other financial institution defined or notified under the RBI Act, 1934.
- **(40) financial statement** — includes: (i) the balance sheet as at the end of the financial year; (ii) the profit and loss account, or for a non-profit company the income and expenditure account; (iii) the cash flow statement; (iv) the statement of changes in equity, if applicable; and (v) any explanatory note to these. Proviso: a One Person Company, a small company, a dormant company and an exempted private company need not include a cash flow statement.
- **(41) financial year** — the period ending on 31 March each year. For a company incorporated on or after 1 January, the first financial year ends on 31 March of the following year.
  - Proviso: a company that is the holding company or subsidiary or associate of a company incorporated outside India, and needs a different financial year to consolidate its accounts outside India, may apply for one. Since the 2019 amendment, the Central Government approves this (previously the Tribunal). Existing companies had to align their financial year within 2 years of commencement.
- **(42) foreign company** — a company or body corporate incorporated outside India that (a) has a place of business in India, whether by itself, through an agent, physically or electronically, and (b) carries on any business activity in India in any other way.
- **(43) free reserves** — reserves that may be used to pay dividends according to the latest audited balance sheet. They do not include (i) unrealised gains, notional gains or revaluation of assets, whether shown as a reserve or otherwise, or (ii) changes in the carrying amount of an asset or liability recognised in equity, including surplus in the profit and loss account, on measuring the asset or liability at fair value.
- **(44) Global Depository Receipt** — a depository receipt created by an overseas depository bank outside India and authorised by the issuing company.
- **(45) Government company** — a company in which at least 51% of the paid-up share capital is held by the Central Government, one or more State Governments, or both together. A subsidiary of a Government company is also a Government company.
- **(46) holding company** — a company of which one or more other companies are subsidiaries. Explanation (2017): "company" here includes any body corporate.
- **(47) independent director** — an independent director as referred to in section 149(6).
- **(48) Indian Depository Receipt** — a depository receipt created by a domestic depository in India against the underlying equity shares of an issuing company incorporated outside India.
- **(49) interested director** — omitted by the Companies (Amendment) Act, 2017.
- **(50) issued capital** — the share capital the company issues for subscription from time to time.
- **(51) key managerial personnel** — (i) the CEO, managing director or manager; (ii) the company secretary; (iii) the whole-time director; (iv) the CFO; (v) any other officer, not more than one level below the directors, who is in whole-time employment and is designated as KMP by the Board (added in 2017); and (vi) any other officer prescribed.
- **(52) listed company** — a company any of whose securities are listed on a recognised stock exchange. Proviso (2020): the Central Government, in consultation with SEBI, may exclude classes of companies that list or intend to list prescribed classes of securities. Under the 2021 rules, examples include certain issuers of privately placed listed non-convertible debt securities or non-convertible redeemable preference shares [verify].
- **(53) manager** — an individual who manages all or substantially all of the company's affairs under the superintendence, control and direction of the Board. This includes a director or other person in that position, whatever their title and whether or not under a contract of service.
- **(54) managing director** — a director who is given substantial powers of management by the articles, an agreement, a resolution of the company or the Board, or the memorandum or articles, and includes a director occupying that position by any name. Explanation: routine administrative powers (such as affixing the common seal, drawing and endorsing cheques, signing share certificates, or registering share transfers) are not "substantial powers of management".
- **(55) member** — (i) a subscriber to the memorandum, who is treated as having agreed to become a member and whose name is entered in the register of members on registration; (ii) every other person who agrees in writing to become a member and whose name is entered in the register of members; and (iii) every person holding shares whose name is entered as a beneficial owner in a depository's records.
- **(56) memorandum** — the memorandum of association as originally framed or as altered under any previous company law or this Act.
- **(57) net worth** — the total of paid-up share capital, all reserves created out of profits, securities premium, and the debit or credit balance of the profit and loss account, minus accumulated losses, deferred expenditure and miscellaneous expenditure not written off, as per the audited balance sheet. It excludes reserves created from revaluation of assets, write-back of depreciation, and amalgamation.
- **(58) notification** — a notification published in the Official Gazette, and "notify" has the matching meaning.
- **(59) officer** — includes any director, manager or key managerial personnel, and any person in accordance with whose directions or instructions the Board, or any one or more directors, is accustomed to act.
- **(60) officer who is in default** — the officers liable for a default where the Act refers to this term:
  - (i) a whole-time director;
  - (ii) key managerial personnel;
  - (iii) if there is no KMP, the director(s) named by the Board for this purpose who have given written consent, or else all the directors;
  - (iv) any person who, under the authority of the Board or a KMP, is responsible for maintaining, processing or filing accounts or records, and who authorises, actively takes part in, knowingly permits, or knowingly fails to take active steps to prevent, any default;
  - (v) any person on whose advice, directions or instructions the Board is accustomed to act, other than a person giving advice in a professional capacity;
  - (vi) every director who knows of a contravention because they received or took part in Board proceedings without objecting, or with whose consent or connivance the contravention happened;
  - (vii) for the issue or transfer of shares, the share transfer agents, registrars and merchant bankers to the issue or transfer.
- **(61) Official Liquidator** — an Official Liquidator appointed under section 359.
- **(62) One Person Company** — a company with only one person as its member.
- **(63) ordinary or special resolution** — an ordinary or special resolution as referred to in section 114.
- **(64) paid-up share capital** — the amount of money paid up or credited as paid up on shares issued, and the amount credited as paid up on shares. It does not include any other amount received for those shares, whatever it is called.
- **(65) postal ballot** — voting by post or through any electronic mode.
- **(66) prescribed** — prescribed by rules made under this Act.
- **(67) previous company law** — any of the earlier company laws: the Acts before the Indian Companies Act, 1866; the Indian Companies Acts of 1866, 1882 and 1913; the Registration of Transferred Companies Ordinance, 1942; the Companies Act, 1956; and the corresponding laws in force in the merged territories, Part B States, Jammu and Kashmir, and the Portuguese Civil Code in Goa, Daman and Diu [verify the full list].
- **(68) private company** — a company with the minimum paid-up share capital (if any) prescribed, whose articles:
  - (i) restrict the right to transfer its shares;
  - (ii) except for a One Person Company, limit the number of members to 200; and
  - (iii) prohibit any invitation to the public to subscribe for its securities.
  - Provisos: joint holders count as one member. Present employee-members, and former employees who became members while employed and remain members, are not counted. (The ₹1 lakh minimum paid-up capital requirement was removed in 2015.)
- **(69) promoter** — a person who (a) is named as a promoter in a prospectus or in the annual return under section 92; (b) has direct or indirect control over the company's affairs, as a shareholder, director or otherwise; or (c) is a person on whose advice, directions or instructions the Board is accustomed to act. Clause (c) does not apply to a person acting only in a professional capacity.
- **(70) prospectus** — any document described or issued as a prospectus, including a red herring prospectus (section 32), a shelf prospectus (section 31), or any notice, circular, advertisement or other document inviting offers from the public to subscribe for or buy a company's securities.
- **(71) public company** — a company that (a) is not a private company and (b) has the minimum paid-up share capital (if any) prescribed. Proviso: a subsidiary of a public company is treated as a public company even if its articles say it is a private company. (The ₹5 lakh minimum paid-up capital requirement was removed in 2015.)
- **(72) public financial institution** — (i) the Life Insurance Corporation of India; (ii) the Infrastructure Development Finance Company Limited; (iii) the specified company under the Unit Trust of India (Transfer of Undertaking and Repeal) Act, 2002; (iv) institutions notified under section 4A(2) of the Companies Act, 1956; and (v) any other institution the Central Government notifies in consultation with the RBI. Under (v), the institution must be set up by a Central or State Act, or at least 51% of its paid-up capital must be held by the Central Government, a State Government or both, or by a public financial institution [verify].
- **(73) recognised stock exchange** — a recognised stock exchange as defined in section 2(f) of the Securities Contracts (Regulation) Act, 1956.
- **(74) register of companies** — the register of companies kept by the Registrar on paper or electronically.
- **(75) Registrar** — a Registrar, Additional Registrar, Joint Registrar, Deputy Registrar or Assistant Registrar who has the duty of registering companies and performing functions under this Act.
- **(76) related party** — in relation to a company:
  - (i) a director or KMP, or their relative;
  - (ii) a firm in which a director, manager or their relative is a partner;
  - (iii) a private company in which a director or manager, or their relative, is a member or director;
  - (iv) a public company in which a director or manager is a director, and holds more than 2% of its paid-up share capital along with relatives;
  - (v) a body corporate whose Board, managing director or manager is accustomed to act on the advice of a director or manager of the company;
  - (vi) a person on whose advice the company's director or manager is accustomed to act;
  - Exception for (v) and (vi): advice given in a professional capacity does not count.
  - (vii) the company's holding, subsidiary or associate company, or a subsidiary of its holding company;
  - (viii) a director (other than an independent director) or KMP of the holding company, or their relative;
  - (ix) any other person prescribed.
- **(77) relative** — in relation to another person: (i) members of a Hindu Undivided Family; (ii) husband and wife; or (iii) any other prescribed relationship. The rules list father (including step-father), mother (including step-mother), son (including step-son), son's wife, daughter, daughter's husband, brother (including step-brother) and sister (including step-sister).
- **(78) remuneration** — any money, or its equivalent, given or passed to a person for services rendered. It includes perquisites as defined under the Income-tax Act, 1961.
- **(79) Schedule** — a Schedule annexed to this Act.
- **(80) scheduled bank** — a bank included in the Second Schedule to the RBI Act, 1934.
- **(81) securities** — securities as defined in section 2(h) of the Securities Contracts (Regulation) Act, 1956.
- **(82) Securities and Exchange Board** — the Securities and Exchange Board of India (SEBI) set up under section 3 of the SEBI Act, 1992.
- **(83) Serious Fraud Investigation Office** — the office referred to in section 211.
- **(84) share** — a share in the share capital of a company. It includes stock.
- **(85) small company** — a company, other than a public company, whose:
  - (i) paid-up share capital does not exceed ₹50 lakh or any higher prescribed amount (statutory ceiling ₹10 crore); and
  - (ii) turnover, as per the profit and loss account for the immediately preceding financial year, does not exceed ₹2 crore or any higher prescribed amount (statutory ceiling ₹100 crore).
  - The 2017 amendment raised the ceilings. The prescribed limits were raised to ₹2 crore / ₹20 crore (2021), then ₹4 crore / ₹40 crore (2022), and then to ₹10 crore paid-up capital / ₹100 crore turnover (2025 rules) [verify].
  - Exclusions: (A) a holding or subsidiary company; (B) a company registered under section 8; (C) a company or body corporate governed by a special Act.
- **(86) subscribed capital** — the part of the capital for the time being subscribed by the company's members.
- **(87) subsidiary company / subsidiary** — a company in which the holding company:
  - (i) controls the composition of the Board of Directors; or
  - (ii) exercises or controls more than half of the total voting power, either alone or together with one or more of its subsidiaries or holding companies. (The 2017 amendment replaced "total share capital" with "total voting power".)
  - Proviso: prescribed classes of holding companies may not have more than the prescribed number of layers of subsidiaries. The rules allow two layers, with exceptions [verify].
  - Explanations: (a) a company is a subsidiary even if the control in (i) or (ii) is held through another subsidiary; (b) control of the Board's composition means the power to appoint or remove all or a majority of the directors at its discretion; (c) "company" includes a body corporate; (d) "layer" means a subsidiary or subsidiaries of the holding company.
- **(88) sweat equity shares** — equity shares issued to directors or employees at a discount or for a non-cash consideration, in return for providing know-how, making intellectual property rights available, or adding value, whatever it is called.
- **(89) total voting power** — the total of the votes that may be cast on the question by all members present and entitled to vote, if they all voted.
- **(90) Tribunal** — the National Company Law Tribunal set up under section 408.
- **(91) turnover** — the gross amount of revenue recognised in the profit and loss account from the sale, supply or distribution of goods, from services rendered, or from both, during a financial year. (This definition was substituted in 2017.)
- **(92) unlimited company** — a company whose members' liability is not limited.
- **(93) voting right** — a member's right to vote on any matter at company meetings, by show of hands or by poll.
- **(94) whole-time director** — includes a director in the whole-time employment of the company.
- **(94A) winding up** — inserted by the Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code, 2016. It means winding up under this Act, or liquidation under the IBC, as applicable.
- **(95) other words and expressions** — words and expressions used but not defined in this Act, and defined in the SCRA, 1956, the SEBI Act, 1992 or the Depositories Act, 1996, have the meanings given in those Acts.

## CHAPTER II — INCORPORATION OF COMPANY AND MATTERS INCIDENTAL THERETO

### Section 3. Formation of company

- **(1)** A company may be formed for any lawful purpose by:
  - (a) **seven or more persons**, for a public company;
  - (b) **two or more persons**, for a private company; or
  - (c) **one person**, for a One Person Company (a private company).
  - The founders must subscribe their names to a memorandum and register the company under this Act.
  - **OPC proviso:** the memorandum of an OPC must name another person, with that person's prior written consent in the prescribed form. That person becomes the member if the subscriber dies or becomes incapable of contracting. The written consent is filed with the Registrar at incorporation, together with the memorandum and articles. The nominee may withdraw consent, and the member may change the nominee at any time, by giving notice as prescribed. The company must inform the Registrar of any such change.
- **(2)** A company formed under (1) may be:
  - (a) a company limited by shares;
  - (b) a company limited by guarantee; or
  - (c) an unlimited company.

### Section 3A. Members severally liable in certain cases

- *(Inserted by the Companies (Amendment) Act, 2017/2019 [verify year].)*
- This applies if a company carries on business for **more than six months** after its membership falls below **seven** (public company) or **two** (private company).
- In that case, every person who is a member during the time the business continues after those six months, and who knows about the shortfall, is **severally liable** for all debts the company contracts during that time. Each such person may be sued separately.

### Section 4. Memorandum

- **(1)** The memorandum must state:
  - (a) **the name** of the company, ending with "Limited" for a public limited company or "Private Limited" for a private limited company. This does not apply to a section 8 company.
  - (b) **the State** where the registered office will be located;
  - (c) **the objects** for which the company is incorporated, and any matters needed to further them;
  - (d) **the liability of members**, whether limited or unlimited, and:
    - (i) for a company limited by shares, that each member's liability is limited to any amount unpaid on their shares;
    - (ii) for a company limited by guarantee, the amount each member undertakes to contribute to the company's assets in a winding up while a member, or within one year after ceasing to be a member, for debts and liabilities contracted while they were a member, the costs of winding up, and adjusting the contributories' rights among themselves;
  - (e) **for a company with share capital:**
    - (i) the amount of authorised capital, divided into shares of a fixed amount;
    - (ii) the number of shares each subscriber agrees to take, which must be at least one share;
    - (iii) the number of shares each subscriber takes, shown against their name;
  - (f) **for an OPC**, the name of the person who becomes the member if the subscriber dies.
- **(2)** The name must not be identical with, or too close to, the name of an existing company. It must also not be a name whose use would be an offence under any law, or that the Central Government considers undesirable.
- **(3)** A name, or a name containing prescribed words, that suggests a connection with the Central Government, a State Government, a local authority, or any corporation or body set up by the Government, needs the Central Government's prior approval.
- **(4)** A person may apply to the Registrar in the prescribed form to reserve a name, either for a new company or for a change of name of an existing company.
- **(5)(i)** After the application, the Registrar may reserve the name for **20 days** from the date of approval, or for any other prescribed period. (The rules allow 60 days for a name reserved for a change of name [verify].)
- **(5)(ii)** If, after reservation, it is found that the name was obtained by giving wrong or incorrect information:
  - (a) **if the company is not yet incorporated**, the reservation is cancelled and the applicant is liable to a penalty of up to **₹1 lakh**;
  - (b) **if the company is already incorporated**, the Registrar, after giving the company a hearing, may:
    - (i) direct it to change its name within **three months**, after passing an ordinary resolution;
    - (ii) take action to strike off its name from the register; or
    - (iii) petition for its winding up.
- **(6)** The memorandum must follow whichever of **Tables A, B, C, D and E in Schedule I** applies to the company.
- **(7)** For a company limited by guarantee without share capital, any provision in the memorandum or articles that gives anyone a right to share in divisible profits other than as a member is void.

### Section 5. Articles

- **(1)** The articles contain the regulations for managing the company.
- **(2)** The articles must also contain the matters prescribed under the rules. The company may include any additional matters it considers necessary for its management.
- **(3)** The articles may contain **entrenchment** provisions. These allow specified provisions to be changed only if conditions or procedures more restrictive than a special resolution are met or followed.
- **(4)** Entrenchment provisions may be made only:
  - on formation of the company; or
  - by amending the articles, agreed by **all members** in a private company or by **special resolution** in a public company.
- **(5)** If the articles contain entrenchment provisions, made on formation or by amendment, the company must notify the Registrar in the prescribed form and manner.
- **(6)** The articles must follow whichever of **Tables F, G, H, I and J in Schedule I** applies to the company.
- **(7)** A company may adopt all or any of the regulations in the applicable model articles.
- **(8)** For a company registered after commencement of this section, the applicable model articles apply automatically, as if they were in the registered articles, to the extent the registered articles do not exclude or modify them.
- **(9)** This section does not affect the articles of a company registered under any previous company law unless those articles are amended under this Act.

### Section 6. Act to override memorandum, articles, etc.

- Unless the Act expressly provides otherwise:
  - (a) the Act's provisions apply even if anything to the contrary is contained in a company's memorandum or articles, in any agreement the company makes, or in any resolution passed by the company in general meeting or by its Board, whether registered, made or passed before or after commencement;
  - (b) any provision in the memorandum, articles, agreement or resolution is void to the extent it conflicts with the Act.

### Section 7. Incorporation of company

- **(1)** The following documents must be filed with the Registrar with jurisdiction over the proposed registered office:
  - (a) the memorandum and articles, duly signed by all subscribers as prescribed;
  - (b) a **declaration** in the prescribed form by an advocate, chartered accountant, cost accountant or company secretary in practice who is engaged in forming the company, and by a person named in the articles as a director, manager or secretary, that all requirements of the Act and rules for registration have been met;
  - (c) a **declaration** from each subscriber and each first director named in the articles stating that:
    - they have not been convicted of any offence connected with the promotion, formation or management of any company;
    - they have not been found guilty of fraud, misfeasance or breach of duty to any company under this Act or any previous company law during the **preceding five years**; and
    - all documents filed are correct, complete and true to the best of their knowledge and belief.
    - (The 2017 amendment replaced the earlier "affidavit" with a "declaration".)
  - (d) the address for correspondence until the registered office is established;
  - (e) for each subscriber: name (including surname or family name), residential address, nationality and other prescribed particulars, with proof of identity. For a body corporate subscriber, the prescribed particulars.
  - (f) for each person named as a first director in the articles: the same particulars, plus their Director Identification Number (DIN) and other prescribed particulars;
  - (g) the particulars of the interests of the first directors in other firms or bodies corporate, along with their consent to act as directors, in the prescribed form and manner.
- **(2)** Based on the documents filed, the Registrar registers the documents and information in the register and issues a **certificate of incorporation** in the prescribed form.
- **(3)** From the date in the certificate, the Registrar allots the company a **corporate identity number (CIN)**, which is a distinct identity for the company and is also stated in the certificate.
- **(4)** The company must keep copies of all documents and information originally filed under (1) at its registered office until it is dissolved under this Act.
- **(5)** A person who, in any document filed for incorporation, gives false or incorrect particulars, or suppresses any material information they know of, is liable under **section 447** (fraud).
- **(6)** If, at any time after incorporation, it is proved that the company was incorporated by giving false or incorrect information or representation, or by suppressing a material fact or information, in any document or declaration filed or made for incorporating the company, the promoters, the first directors and the persons making declarations under (1)(b) are each liable under **section 447**.
- **(7)** If the Tribunal is satisfied, on an application, that the situation in (6) applies, it may:
  - (a) make any order it thinks fit for regulating the company's management, including changes to its memorandum and articles, in the public interest or in the interest of the company and its members and creditors;
  - (b) direct that the members' liability is unlimited;
  - (c) direct removal of the company's name from the register of companies;
  - (d) order the winding up of the company; or
  - (e) make any other order it thinks fit.
  - Before passing an order, the Tribunal must give the company a reasonable opportunity to be heard. It must also consider the transactions the company entered into, including obligations it incurred and payments of any liability.

### Section 8. Formation of companies with charitable objects, etc.

- **(1)** This applies where the Central Government is satisfied that a person or association of persons, proposed to be registered as a limited company:
  - (a) has as its objects the promotion of commerce, art, science, sports, education, research, social welfare, religion, charity, protection of the environment, or any similar object;
  - (b) intends to apply its profits, if any, or other income in promoting its objects; and
  - (c) intends to prohibit the payment of any dividend to its members.
  - In that case, the Central Government may, by licence, allow it to be registered as a limited company **without adding "Limited" or "Private Limited"** to its name. The company is then registered accordingly.
- **(2)** A company registered under this section has all the privileges, and is subject to all the obligations, of a limited company.
- **(3)** A firm may be a member of a section 8 company. Its membership ends when the firm is dissolved.
- **(4)(i)** A section 8 company may not change the provisions of its memorandum or articles without the Central Government's prior approval.
- **(4)(ii)** A section 8 company that wants to convert into a company of any other kind must comply with the prescribed conditions.
- **(5)** An existing limited company that meets the requirements of (1) may apply to the Central Government for a licence under this section. It may then be granted a licence and its name changed to remove "Limited" or "Private Limited". The Registrar then makes the necessary entries.
- **(6)** The Central Government may revoke the licence if the company:
  - contravenes any requirement of this section or any condition of the licence; or
  - conducts its affairs fraudulently, or in breach of its objects, or against the public interest.
  - After revocation, the Registrar adds "Limited" or "Private Limited" to the company's name. The Central Government must give the company a reasonable opportunity to be heard first, by written notice.
- **(7)** After revoking the licence, the Central Government may, if it is satisfied it is necessary in the public interest, direct that the company be wound up under this Act, or be amalgamated with another section 8 company having similar objects.
- **(8)** If the Central Government orders amalgamation, it may provide for the constitution, property, powers, rights, interests, authorities, privileges, liabilities, duties and obligations of the single amalgamated company.
- **(9)** If a section 8 company is wound up or dissolved, any surplus assets left after all debts and liabilities are met may be given or transferred to another section 8 company with similar objects, as decided by the Tribunal. Alternatively, they may be sold and the proceeds credited to the Insolvency and Bankruptcy Fund under section 224 of the IBC [verify].
- **(10)** A company registered under this section must amalgamate only with another section 8 company having similar objects.
- **(11) Penalty** (as substituted in 2020 [verify]):
  - The company: fine of **₹10 lakh to ₹1 crore**.
  - Its directors and every officer in default: fine of **₹25,000 to ₹25 lakh**.
  - If the affairs were conducted fraudulently, every officer in default is liable under **section 447**.
  - These apply without affecting any other action under this section.

### Section 9. Effect of registration

- From the date of incorporation in the certificate, the subscribers to the memorandum and all other persons who later become members form a **body corporate** under the registered name.
- This body corporate:
  - can exercise all the functions of an incorporated company under this Act;
  - has **perpetual succession**; and
  - has power to acquire, hold and dispose of movable and immovable, tangible and intangible property, to contract, and to sue and be sued in its own name.
- (The reference to a mandatory common seal was removed by the 2015 amendment.)

### Section 10. Effect of memorandum and articles

- **(1)** Once registered, the memorandum and articles bind the company and its members as if each member had signed them and agreed to observe all their provisions.
- **(2)** Any money a member must pay to the company under the memorandum or articles is a **debt due** from that member to the company.

### Section 10A. Commencement of business, etc.

- *(Inserted by the Companies (Amendment) Act, 2019, with effect from 2 November 2018.)*
- **(1)** A company incorporated after the Companies (Amendment) Ordinance, 2018 that has share capital must not commence business or exercise any borrowing powers unless:
  - (a) a director files a **declaration** with the Registrar within **180 days of incorporation**, in the prescribed form and verified as prescribed, that every subscriber has paid the value of the shares they agreed to take on the date of the declaration; and
  - (b) the company has filed with the Registrar verification of its registered office under section 12(2).
- **(2) Penalty for default:**
  - The company: **₹50,000**.
  - Every officer in default: **₹1,000 per day** while the default continues, up to a maximum of **₹1 lakh**.
- **(3)** If no declaration is filed within 180 days, and the Registrar has reasonable cause to believe the company is not carrying on any business or operations, the Registrar may start action to remove the company's name from the register under **Chapter XVIII**. This does not affect the penalty under (2).

### Section 11. Commencement of business, etc.

- **Omitted** by the Companies (Amendment) Act, 2015. The original section required a declaration before commencement of business. A similar requirement was later brought back as section 10A.

### Section 12. Registered office of company

- **(1)** From the **15th day of its incorporation**, and at all times after that, a company must have a registered office that can receive and acknowledge all communications and notices addressed to it.
- **(2)** The company must give the Registrar verification of its registered office within **30 days of incorporation**, in the prescribed manner.
- **(3)** Every company must:
  - (a) paint or affix its name and the address of its registered office, and keep them painted or affixed, on the outside of every office or place where it carries on business. These must be in a conspicuous position, in legible letters, and, if the characters used are not those of the local language, also in the local language. An OPC must also add "One Person Company" in brackets below its name wherever its name is printed, affixed or engraved.
  - (b) have its name engraved in legible characters on its seal, if it has one;
  - (c) have its name, registered office address, CIN, telephone number, fax number (if any), email and website address (if any) printed on all its business letters, billheads, letter papers, and all its notices and other official publications;
  - (d) have its name printed on hundis, promissory notes, bills of exchange and other prescribed documents.
  - If the company has changed its name in the **last two years**, it must also print or affix its former name(s) next to its current name.
- **(4)** Notice of any change in the situation of the registered office must be given to the Registrar within **15 days** of the change, verified as prescribed. (The 2017 amendment reduced this from 30 days [verify].) The Registrar records it.
- **(5)** A **special resolution** is needed to move the registered office outside the local limits of the city, town or village where it is located. Moving it from the jurisdiction of one Registrar to another within the **same State** also needs confirmation from the **Regional Director**, on the company's application.
- **(6)** The Regional Director communicates the confirmation within **30 days** of receiving the application. The company then files it with the Registrar within **60 days** of the confirmation, and the Registrar certifies the registration within **30 days**.
- **(7)** That certificate is **conclusive evidence** that all requirements of the Act for the change of registered office have been met. The change takes effect from the date of the certificate.
- **(8) Penalty for default:** the company and every officer in default are each liable to a penalty of **₹1,000 per day** of default, up to a maximum of **₹1 lakh**.
- **(9)** (Inserted in 2019.) If the Registrar has reasonable cause to believe the company is not carrying on any business or operations, the Registrar may arrange a **physical verification** of the registered office as prescribed. If the company fails to comply with (1), the Registrar may start action to remove its name from the register under **Chapter XVIII**. This does not affect the penalty under (8).

### Section 13. Alteration of memorandum

- **(1)** Except as provided in section 61, a company may change its memorandum by **special resolution**, after following the prescribed procedure.
- **(2)** A change of name requires the **Central Government's written approval**. No approval is needed for adding or deleting "Private" when a company converts from one class to another.
- **(3)** When the name is changed, the Registrar enters the new name in the register and issues a **fresh certificate of incorporation**.
- **(4)** Moving the registered office from one **State to another** needs the Central Government's approval (exercised by the Regional Director), on the company's application in the prescribed form.
- **(5)** The Central Government must decide an application under (4) within **60 days**. Before approving, it must be satisfied that:
  - the creditors, debenture-holders and other persons concerned consent to the change; or
  - enough provision has been made to discharge all the company's debts and obligations, or adequate security has been provided.
- **(6)** The company must file with the Registrar:
  - (a) the special resolution passed under (1), within 30 days, as required by section 117; and
  - (b) the Central Government's approval under (2), if the change concerns the name.
- **(7)** For a change under (4), the company files the Central Government's approval order with the Registrar within the prescribed time. The Registrar registers it within **30 days** and certifies the registration.
- **(8)** A company that raised money from the public through a prospectus and still has any of that money unused must not change its objects for that money unless:
  - a special resolution is passed through **postal ballot**;
  - details of the resolution, with the justification for the change, are published in two newspapers (one English and one vernacular) in the city of the registered office, and placed on the company's website if it has one; and
  - dissenting shareholders are given an opportunity to **exit** by the promoters and controlling shareholders, under SEBI regulations.
- **(9)** The Registrar registers any change to the object clause and certifies the registration within **30 days** of the filing.
- **(10)** No change to the memorandum takes effect until it is registered under this section.
- **(11)** For a company limited by guarantee without share capital, any change that would give anyone a right to share in divisible profits other than as a member is **void**.

### Section 14. Alteration of articles

- **(1)** Subject to the Act and to any conditions in its memorandum, a company may change its articles by **special resolution**, including changes that:
  - (a) convert a private company into a public company; or
  - (b) convert a public company into a private company.
  - Proviso: a private company whose amended articles no longer contain the restrictions in section 2(68) stops being a private company from the date of the change.
  - Proviso (2019): converting a public company into a private company takes effect only when approved by an order of the **Central Government** (previously the Tribunal), on the company's application in the prescribed form.
- **(2)** Every change to the articles, and any approval order, must be filed with the Registrar within **15 days**, together with a printed copy of the amended articles. The Registrar then registers it.
- **(3)** A change registered under this section is valid as if it had been in the original articles.

### Section 15. Alteration of memorandum or articles to be noted in every copy

- **(1)** Every change made to the memorandum or articles must be noted in every copy of them issued after the change.
- **(2) Penalty:** if the company does not comply, the company and every officer in default are each liable to a penalty of **₹1,000 for every copy** issued without the change. (This was converted from a fine in 2019.)

### Section 16. Rectification of name of company

- **(1)** If a company is registered, by mistake or otherwise, with a name (on first registration or on a later change) that:
  - (a) is identical with, or too nearly resembles, the name of an **existing company** registered earlier, the Central Government may, on its own motion or on an application, direct it to change its name. The company must change its name within **three months** of the direction, after passing an **ordinary resolution**.
  - (b) is identical with, or too nearly resembles, a **registered trade mark** of a proprietor, the proprietor may apply to the Central Government within **three years** of incorporation, registration or change of name. If the Government directs a change, the company must make it within **three months** of the direction, after passing an ordinary resolution [verify — the period was six months before 2017/2019].
  - (The Central Government's power here is exercised by the Regional Director.)
- **(2)** When a company changes its name under (1), it must notify the Registrar within **15 days**, together with the Central Government's order. The Registrar then makes the change in the register and issues a fresh certificate of incorporation.
- **(3)** (As substituted in 2019/2020.) If the company does not comply with a direction under (1), the Central Government allots it a **new name** in the prescribed manner. The Registrar enters the new name in place of the old one and issues a fresh certificate, and the company must use the new name from then on. The company may later change its name under section 13.

### Section 17. Copies of memorandum, articles, etc., to be given to members

- **(1)** If a member asks, and pays the prescribed fee, the company must send them copies of the following within **7 days** of the request:
  - (a) the memorandum;
  - (b) the articles; and
  - (c) every agreement and every resolution referred to in section 117(1), if they are not included in the memorandum or articles.
- **(2) Penalty:** for each default, the company and every officer in default are liable to a penalty of **₹1,000 per day** of default or **₹1 lakh**, whichever is less.

### Section 18. Conversion of companies already registered

- **(1)** A company of any class registered under this Act may convert into a company of another class by changing its memorandum and articles in line with this Chapter.
- **(2)** On such conversion, the Registrar, on the company's application, closes the former registration. After registering the documents, the Registrar issues a certificate of incorporation in the same way as for a first registration.
- **(3)** Registration under this section does not affect any debts, liabilities, obligations or contracts incurred or entered into by or for the company before conversion. These may be enforced as if the registration had not happened.

### Section 19. Subsidiary company not to hold shares in its holding company

- **(1)** A company must not hold shares in its holding company, either itself or through nominees. A holding company must not allot or transfer its shares to any of its subsidiaries. Any such allotment or transfer is **void**.
  - Exceptions: this does not apply where the subsidiary:
    - (a) holds the shares as the legal representative of a deceased member of the holding company;
    - (b) holds the shares as a trustee, unless the holding company or the subsidiary has a beneficial interest under the trust; or
    - (c) was a shareholder before it became a subsidiary. In this case, the subsidiary has **voting rights only** in the circumstances allowed [verify — the proviso says it has a right to vote only on such resolutions as may be prescribed, or has no voting rights].
- **(2)** References to shares in (1) include the interest of members where the holding company is a company limited by guarantee or an unlimited company without share capital.

### Section 20. Service of documents

- **(1)** A document may be served on a company or any of its officers by sending it to the company or officer at the registered office by registered post, speed post, courier service, by leaving it at the registered office, or by electronic or other prescribed means.
  - Proviso: where securities are held with a depository, the depository may serve records of beneficial ownership on the company by electronic or other means.
- **(2)** Except as otherwise provided, a document may be served on the Registrar or any member by sending it by post, registered post, speed post or courier, by delivering it to their office or address, or by electronic or other prescribed means.
  - Proviso: a member may ask for a document to be delivered by a particular mode. The member must pay any fee the company fixes for this in its annual general meeting.
  - Explanation: "courier" means a person or agency that delivers documents and provides proof of delivery.

### Section 21. Authentication of documents, proceedings and contracts

- Except as otherwise provided in the Act:
  - (a) any document or proceeding that needs to be authenticated by a company; and
  - (b) any contract made by or for a company
  - may be signed by any **key managerial personnel**, or by an officer or **employee** of the company authorised by the Board for this purpose. (Employees were added in 2017.)

### Section 22. Execution of bills of exchange, etc.

- **(1)** A bill of exchange, hundi or promissory note is treated as made, accepted, drawn or endorsed by or for a company if it is made, accepted, drawn or endorsed in the company's name, or for its account, by any person acting under the company's express or implied authority.
- **(2)** A company may, in writing under its **common seal (if any)**, authorise any person, generally or for specific matters, as its attorney to execute other deeds for it in any place inside or outside India.
  - Proviso (2015): if the company has no common seal, the authorisation must be made by **two directors**, or by **one director and the company secretary** if a company secretary has been appointed.
- **(3)** A deed signed by such an attorney for the company, and under the attorney's seal where sealing is required, binds the company.

## CHAPTER III — PROSPECTUS AND ALLOTMENT OF SECURITIES

### PART I — PUBLIC OFFER

### Section 23. Public offer and private placement

- **(1)** A **public company** may issue securities:
  - (a) to the public through a prospectus (a "public offer"), following Part I of this Chapter;
  - (b) through private placement, following Part II of this Chapter; or
  - (c) through a rights issue or bonus issue, following this Act and, for a listed company or one intending to list, also the SEBI Act and its regulations.
- **(2)** A **private company** may issue securities:
  - (a) through a rights issue or bonus issue, following this Act; or
  - (b) through private placement, following Part II of this Chapter.
- Explanation: a "public offer" includes an initial public offer, a further public offer, and an offer for sale of securities to the public by an existing shareholder through a prospectus.
- **(3)** (Inserted in 2020.) The Central Government may allow prescribed classes of public companies to issue prescribed classes of securities in order to list them on stock exchanges in **permissible foreign jurisdictions** or other prescribed jurisdictions.
- **(4)** The Central Government may exempt any class of public companies covered by (3) from any of the provisions of Chapter III, Chapter IV, section 89, section 90 or section 127. A copy of every such notification must be laid before both Houses of Parliament. (The Companies (Listing of Equity Shares in Permissible Jurisdictions) Rules, 2024 provide for listing on IFSC exchanges [verify].)

### Section 24. Power of Securities and Exchange Board to regulate issue and transfer of securities, etc.

- **(1)** The provisions of this Chapter, Chapter IV and section 127 are administered as follows:
  - (a) for the issue and transfer of securities, and non-payment of dividend, by **listed companies** or companies that intend to list their securities on a recognised stock exchange in India: by **SEBI**, through regulations;
  - (b) in any other case: by the **Central Government**.
  - Explanation: all powers relating to other matters, including prospectus matters, return of allotment, redemption of preference shares and any other matter specifically provided in this Act, are exercised by the Central Government, the Tribunal or the Registrar, as applicable.
- **(2)** For the purposes of (1), SEBI has the powers it holds under sections 11(1), 11(2A), 11(4), 11A, 11B and 11D of the SEBI Act, 1992 over listed companies and companies intending to list.

### Section 25. Document containing offer of securities for sale to be deemed prospectus

- **(1)** If a company allots or agrees to allot securities with a view to all or any of them being offered for sale to the public, the document offering them is treated as a **prospectus issued by the company**. All the rules on prospectus contents and on liability for mis-statements, false statements and omissions apply to it, as if the securities were offered to the public for subscription.
- **(2)** Unless the contrary is proved, the allotment is presumed to have been made with a view to public sale if:
  - (a) the offer for sale to the public was made within **six months** after the allotment or agreement to allot; or
  - (b) when the offer was made, the company had not received the whole consideration for the securities.
- **(3)** Section 26 applies to such a document with modifications. In addition to the usual contents, it must state:
  - (a) the net amount of consideration the company has received or will receive for the securities; and
  - (b) the time and place where the contract for the allotment can be inspected.
  - The persons making the offer are treated as the persons named in the prospectus as directors [verify].
- **(4)** If the person making the offer is a company or a firm, it is enough for the document to be signed for the company by **two directors**, or for the firm by **at least half of the partners**. The signatory may sign through an agent authorised in writing.

### Section 26. Matters to be stated in prospectus

- **(1)** Every prospectus issued by or for a public company, whether about an existing or an intended company, must be dated and signed. As substituted in 2017, it must:
  - (a) state the information, and (b) set out the reports on financial information, that **SEBI specifies** in consultation with the Central Government. Until SEBI specifies these, its existing issue regulations apply.
  - (c) contain a declaration that the Act, the SEBI Act and the rules and regulations under them have been complied with, and that nothing in the prospectus contradicts them; and
  - (d) state any other matters and set out any other reports that are prescribed.
- **(2)** Sub-section (1) does not apply:
  - (a) when a prospectus or application form is issued to existing members or debenture-holders, whether or not the applicant may renounce in favour of others; or
  - (b) when a prospectus or application form relates to shares or debentures that are in all respects uniform with ones previously issued and already dealt in or quoted on a recognised stock exchange.
- **(3)** Subject to (2), sub-section (1) applies whether the prospectus or form is issued on or with reference to the company's formation, or later.
  - Explanation: the date shown in the prospectus is treated as its date of publication.
- **(4)** No prospectus may be issued by or for a company, or about an intended company, unless a copy has been delivered to the Registrar for registration on or before its date of publication. The copy must be signed by every person named in it as a director or proposed director, or by their attorney authorised in writing.
- **(5)** A prospectus must not include a statement claimed to be made by an **expert** unless:
  - the expert is not, and has not been, engaged or interested in the formation, promotion or management of the company; and
  - the expert has given written consent to the prospectus being issued with the statement, and has not withdrawn that consent before the copy was delivered for registration.
  - A statement to that effect must be included in the prospectus.
- **(6)** Every prospectus must state on its face:
  - (a) that a copy has been delivered to the Registrar for registration under (4); and
  - (b) any documents required by this section to be filed with the Registrar along with it, or refer to statements in the prospectus that specify those documents.
- **(7)** The Registrar must not register a prospectus unless it meets this section and is accompanied by the written consent of all persons named in it.
- **(8)** A prospectus is **not valid** if it is issued more than **90 days** after the date a copy was delivered to the Registrar.
- **(9) Penalty** (as amended in 2020 [verify]):
  - The company: liable to a penalty of **₹50,000**.
  - Every person who is knowingly a party to issuing the prospectus in contravention: liable to a penalty of **₹50,000**.
  - (The earlier text provided for a fine of ₹50,000 to ₹3 lakh, and imprisonment of up to 3 years for knowing parties.)

### Section 27. Variation in terms of contract or objects in prospectus

- **(1)** A company may change the terms of a contract referred to in its prospectus, or the objects for which the prospectus was issued, only with the approval of the company in general meeting by **special resolution**.
  - The notice of the resolution must be published in two newspapers (one English and one vernacular) in the city of the registered office, as prescribed, and must clearly state the justification for the change.
  - The company must not use money raised through the prospectus to buy, trade in or otherwise deal in the equity shares of any other listed company.
- **(2)** Dissenting shareholders must be given an opportunity to **exit**. The promoters or controlling shareholders must offer this exit at the exit price, and in the manner and on the conditions, specified by SEBI regulations.

### Section 28. Offer of sale of shares by certain members of company

- **(1)** If certain members of a company propose, in consultation with the Board, to offer all or part of their holding of shares to the public, they may do so following the procedure prescribed.
- **(2)** The document by which the offer is made is treated as a **prospectus issued by the company**. All laws and rules on prospectus contents, and on liability for mis-statements and omissions, apply as if the offer were made by the company.
- **(3)** The members, whether individuals or bodies corporate, who want to make the offer must collectively authorise the company, by written agreement or otherwise, to take all actions needed for the offer for sale. They must also reimburse the company for all expenses it incurs on the matter.

### Section 29. Public offer of securities to be in dematerialised form

- **(1)** Despite anything in any other law:
  - (a) every company making a public offer; and
  - (b) any other class or classes of public companies that are prescribed,
  - must issue securities only in **dematerialised form**, in line with the Depositories Act, 1996 and the regulations under it.
- **(1A)** (Inserted in 2019.) For prescribed classes of unlisted companies, securities may be held or transferred only in dematerialised form, as provided in the Depositories Act, 1996 and its regulations.
  - The rules made under this provision apply to unlisted public companies (rule 9A, from 2 October 2018) and to private companies other than small companies (rule 9B, inserted in 2023, with a compliance timeline that was later extended) [verify].
- **(2)** Any other company may convert its securities into dematerialised form, or issue them in physical or dematerialised form, under the Depositories Act, 1996 and its regulations.

### Section 30. Advertisement of prospectus

- If an advertisement of a prospectus is published in any way, it must state:
  - the contents of the memorandum about the company's objects;
  - the liability of members;
  - the amount of share capital;
  - the names of the signatories to the memorandum and the number of shares each subscribed for; and
  - the company's capital structure.

### Section 31. Shelf prospectus

- **(1)** Any class or classes of companies that SEBI specifies by regulations may file a **shelf prospectus** with the Registrar at the stage of the first offer of securities. (Before 2017, this was limited to public financial institutions, public sector banks and scheduled banks.)
  - The shelf prospectus must state a validity period of up to **one year**, starting from the date the first offer opens.
  - No further prospectus is needed for a second or later offer during that period.
- **(2)** Before each later offer, the company must file with the Registrar, within the prescribed time, an **information memorandum**. It must contain all material facts about new charges created, changes in the company's financial position since the first or previous offer, and any other prescribed changes.
  - Proviso: if the company or any other person has received applications with advance payments before the changes were notified, the company must tell the applicants about the changes. If an applicant wants to withdraw, the money must be refunded within **15 days**.
- **(3)** When an information memorandum is filed, every time an offer is made under (2), the memorandum and the shelf prospectus together are treated as the prospectus.
- Explanation: a "shelf prospectus" is a prospectus under which securities, or a class of securities, are issued for subscription in one or more issues over a period, without issuing a fresh prospectus each time.

### Section 32. Red herring prospectus

- **(1)** A company proposing to make an offer of securities may issue a **red herring prospectus** before issuing the prospectus.
- **(2)** The company must file the red herring prospectus with the Registrar at least **three days** before the subscription list and the offer open.
- **(3)** A red herring prospectus carries the same obligations as a prospectus. Any differences between it and the final prospectus must be highlighted as variations in the prospectus.
- **(4)** When the offer closes, a prospectus must be filed with both the Registrar and SEBI. It must state the total capital raised, whether by debt or share capital, the closing price of the securities, and any other details not included in the red herring prospectus.
- Explanation: a "red herring prospectus" is a prospectus that does not include full details of the quantity or price of the securities.

### Section 33. Issue of application forms for securities

- **(1)** No application form for buying a company's securities may be issued unless it comes with an **abridged prospectus**, which is a memorandum containing the key features of the prospectus in the prescribed form.
  - Exceptions: an application form may be issued without it:
    - (a) in connection with a genuine invitation to a person to enter into an underwriting agreement for the securities; or
    - (b) for securities that are not offered to the public.
- **(2)** On request, a copy of the prospectus must be given to any person before the subscription list and the offer close.
- **(3) Penalty:** a company that defaults is liable to a penalty of **₹50,000** for each default [verify — whether this is a "fine" or a "penalty" after 2019/2020].

### Section 34. Criminal liability for mis-statements in prospectus

- This applies where a prospectus that is issued, circulated or distributed:
  - contains any statement that is untrue or misleading in form or context; or
  - includes or leaves out any matter in a way likely to mislead.
- In that case, every person who **authorised the issue** of the prospectus is liable under **section 447** (fraud).
- Defence: this does not apply to a person who proves that the statement or omission was **immaterial**, or that they had reasonable grounds to believe, and did believe up to the time of issue, that the statement was true or that the inclusion or omission was necessary.

### Section 35. Civil liability for mis-statements in prospectus

- **(1)** If a person subscribes for securities relying on a prospectus that contains a misleading statement, or an inclusion or omission, and suffers loss or damage as a result, the following are liable to **pay compensation** to every such person:
  - the company; and
  - every person who:
    - (a) was a director of the company when the prospectus was issued;
    - (b) agreed to be named, and is named, in the prospectus as a director, or as having agreed to become a director, now or after an interval;
    - (c) is a promoter of the company;
    - (d) authorised the issue of the prospectus; or
    - (e) is an expert referred to in section 26(5).
  - This is in addition to any punishment under section 36.
- **(2)** A person is not liable under (1) if they prove that:
  - (a) after agreeing to become a director, they withdrew their consent before the prospectus was issued, and it was issued without their authority or consent; or
  - (b) the prospectus was issued without their knowledge or consent, and once they became aware of it, they promptly gave reasonable public notice that it was issued without their knowledge or consent.
- **(3)** Despite the above, if it is proved that the prospectus was issued with intent to defraud applicants or any other person, or for any fraudulent purpose, every person referred to in (1) is **personally responsible, without any limit of liability**, for all or any of the losses or damages suffered by any person who subscribed relying on the prospectus.

### Section 36. Punishment for fraudulently inducing persons to invest money

- A person is liable under **section 447** if they do any of the following to induce another person to enter into, or offer to enter into, certain agreements:
  - knowingly or recklessly make any statement, promise or forecast that is false, deceptive or misleading; or
  - deliberately conceal any material facts.
- The agreements covered are:
  - (a) any agreement to acquire, dispose of, subscribe for or underwrite securities;
  - (b) any agreement whose purpose, or claimed purpose, is to secure a profit for any party from the yield of securities, or from changes in their value; or
  - (c) any agreement to obtain credit facilities from any bank or financial institution.

### Section 37. Action by affected persons

- A suit may be filed, or any other action taken, under **section 34, 35 or 36** by:
  - any person;
  - any group of persons; or
  - any association of persons
  - who are affected by a misleading statement, or by the inclusion or omission of any matter, in the prospectus.

### Section 38. Punishment for personation for acquisition, etc., of securities

- **(1)** Any person who does any of the following is liable under **section 447**:
  - (a) makes, or abets the making of, an application to a company in a **fictitious name** to acquire or subscribe for its securities;
  - (b) makes, or abets the making of, **multiple applications** to a company in different names, or in different combinations of their name or surname, to acquire or subscribe for its securities; or
  - (c) otherwise induces a company, directly or indirectly, to allot securities to them or any other person in a fictitious name, or to register a transfer of securities to them or any other person in a fictitious name.
- **(2)** The provisions of (1) must be prominently reproduced in every prospectus issued by a company and in every form of application for securities.
- **(3)** If a person is convicted under this section, the Court may also order:
  - **disgorgement** of any gain the person made in this way; and
  - **seizure and disposal** of the securities in the person's possession.
- **(4)** Amounts received through disgorgement or disposal under (3) are credited to the **Investor Education and Protection Fund**.

### Section 39. Allotment of securities by company

- **(1)** Securities offered to the public for subscription may be allotted only if:
  - the **minimum amount** stated in the prospectus has been subscribed; and
  - the sums payable on application for that amount have been paid to and received by the company, by cheque or other instrument.
- **(2)** The amount payable on application for each security must be at least **5% of its nominal amount**, or any other percentage or amount that SEBI specifies by regulations.
- **(3)** If the stated minimum amount is not subscribed, and the application money is not received, within **30 days** from the date of issue of the prospectus (or any other period SEBI specifies), the amount received must be **returned** within the prescribed time and manner. The rules require return within 15 days of the issue closing, with liability for interest if this is not done [verify].
- **(4)** Whenever a company with share capital makes any allotment of securities, it must file a **return of allotment** with the Registrar in the prescribed manner.
- **(5) Penalty:** for a default under (3) or (4), the company and every officer in default are each liable to a penalty of **₹1,000 per day** of default or **₹1 lakh**, whichever is less.

### Section 40. Securities to be dealt with in stock exchanges

- **(1)** Before making a public offer, every company must apply to one or more **recognised stock exchanges** for permission for its securities to be dealt with on that exchange or those exchanges, and must obtain that permission.
- **(2)** If permission has been applied for under (1), the prospectus must state the name or names of the stock exchanges where the securities will be dealt with.
- **(3)** All money received on application from the public must be kept in a **separate bank account in a scheduled bank**. It may be used only for:
  - (a) adjustment against allotment of securities, if the securities have been permitted to be dealt with on the stock exchange(s); or
  - (b) repayment of money received from applicants, within the time SEBI specifies, if the company cannot allot securities for any reason.
- **(4)** Any condition that requires or binds an applicant to waive compliance with this section is **void**.
- **(5) Punishment** (as amended in 2020 [verify]):
  - The company: fine of **₹5 lakh to ₹50 lakh**.
  - Every officer in default: fine of **₹50,000 to ₹3 lakh**.
  - (The original text also allowed imprisonment of up to 1 year for officers.)
- **(6)** A company may pay **commission** to any person in connection with subscription to its securities, on the prescribed conditions.

### Section 41. Global depository receipt

- A company may issue **depository receipts** in any foreign country after passing a **special resolution** in its general meeting.
- The issue must be made in the prescribed manner and on the prescribed conditions.

### PART II — PRIVATE PLACEMENT

### Section 42. Issue of shares on private placement basis

- *(Substituted by the Companies (Amendment) Act, 2017. The earlier heading was "Offer or invitation for subscription of securities on private placement" [verify current heading].)*
- **(1)** A company may make a private placement of securities, subject to this section.
- **(2)** A private placement may be made only to a select group of persons identified by the Board (the **"identified persons"**).
  - The number of identified persons in a financial year must not exceed **50**, or any higher number prescribed. The rules set the limit at **200** per financial year.
  - The count excludes qualified institutional buyers, and employees offered securities under an employees' stock option scheme under section 62(1)(b).
  - The placement must meet the prescribed conditions, including the form and manner of the offer.
- **(3)** A private placement must be made using a **private placement offer and application** in the prescribed form. It must be serially numbered and addressed specifically to the identified person to whom the offer is made. It must be sent to that person within **30 days** of their names being recorded [verify]. Only the identified persons may apply.
- **(4)** An identified person who wants to subscribe must pay the subscription money by cheque, demand draft or other banking channel, and **not in cash**. The company must not use the money raised until the allotment is made and the return of allotment is filed with the Registrar.
- **(5)** A company must not make a fresh private placement offer unless earlier allotments have been completed, withdrawn or abandoned. Subject to the maximum number of identified persons, a company may make more than one issue of securities to different classes of identified persons at any time.
- **(6)** Allotment timeline and refunds:
  - Securities must be allotted within **60 days** of receiving the application money.
  - If they are not allotted in that time, the money must be repaid within **15 days** after the 60 days end.
  - If the money is not repaid in time, it must be repaid with interest at **12% per year** from the 60th day.
  - Application money must be kept in a **separate bank account in a scheduled bank**. It may be used only for adjustment against allotment, or for repayment if allotment cannot be made.
- **(7)** A company making a private placement must not release any public advertisement, or use any media, marketing, distribution channels or agents to inform the public at large about the issue.
- **(8)** The company must file a **return of allotment** with the Registrar within **15 days** of allotment. It must include the full list of allottees, their names, addresses, the number of securities allotted, and other prescribed details.
- **(9) Penalty for late return:** the company, its promoters and directors are liable to a penalty of **₹1,000 per day** while the default continues, up to a maximum of **₹25 lakh**.
- **(10) Penalty for contravention:** subject to (11), if a company makes an offer or accepts money in breach of this section:
  - the company, its promoters and directors are liable to a penalty of up to the **amount raised** through the private placement, or **₹2 crore**, whichever is lower; and
  - the company must refund all money, with interest, to the subscribers within **30 days** of the penalty order.
- **(11)** Any private placement issue that does not comply with this section is treated as a **public offer**, and all provisions of this Act, the SCRA, 1956 and the SEBI Act, 1992 apply to it.
- Explanation I:
  - "**private placement**" means any offer or invitation to subscribe, or issue of securities, by a company to a select group of persons (other than through a public offer), using a private placement offer-cum-application that meets the conditions of this section.
  - "**qualified institutional buyer**" means a QIB as defined in the SEBI (Issue of Capital and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations.
- Explanation II: if a company, whether listed or unlisted, offers or invites subscription to securities, or allots or agrees to allot them, to more than the prescribed number of persons, this is treated as a public offer. This applies whether or not payment was received, and whether or not the company intends to list the securities [verify placement within section].

## CHAPTER IV — SHARE CAPITAL AND DEBENTURES

### Section 43. Kinds of share capital

- The share capital of a company limited by shares is of two kinds:
  - **(a) equity share capital:**
    - (i) with voting rights; or
    - (ii) with **differential rights** as to dividend, voting or otherwise, as prescribed. The rules cap shares with differential voting rights at **74% of total voting power** (amended in 2019) and set other conditions [verify].
  - **(b) preference share capital.**
- Explanation:
