  - (i) "**equity share capital**" means all share capital that is not preference share capital.
  - (ii) "**preference share capital**" means the part of the issued share capital that carries, or will carry, a preferential right to:
    - (a) payment of dividend, either as a fixed amount or at a fixed rate, which may be free of or subject to income-tax; and
    - (b) repayment of the paid-up (or deemed paid-up) capital on a winding up or repayment of capital, whether or not there is also a preferential right to a fixed premium or a premium on a fixed scale specified in the memorandum or articles.
  - (iii) Capital is still preference capital even if it also carries a right to share, fully or in a limited way, with equity capital in surplus on winding up or in further dividends after the preferential dividend.
- (Private companies are exempted from this section where their memorandum or articles so provide, under the 2015 exemption notification [verify].)

### Section 44. Nature of shares or debentures

- The shares, debentures or other interest of any member in a company are **movable property**.
- They are transferable in the manner provided by the company's articles.

### Section 45. Numbering of shares

- Every share in a company with share capital must be identified by a **distinctive number**.
- This does not apply to shares held with a depository.

### Section 46. Certificate of shares

- **(1)** A share certificate is **prima facie evidence** of a person's title to the shares it specifies if it is issued:
  - under the company's common seal, if any; or
  - signed by **two directors**, or by **one director and the company secretary** if one has been appointed. (The 2015 amendment made the seal optional.)
- **(2)** A **duplicate** share certificate may be issued if the original:
  - (a) is proved to have been lost or destroyed; or
  - (b) has been defaced, mutilated or torn and is surrendered to the company.
- **(3)** Despite anything in the articles, the manner of issuing a certificate or duplicate, its form, its particulars, and the authority under which it is issued are as prescribed.
- **(4)** If a share is held in dematerialised form, the depository's record is **prima facie evidence** of the beneficial owner's interest.
- **(5) Punishment for fraudulent duplicates:** if a company issues a duplicate certificate with intent to defraud:
  - the company is liable to a fine of at least **five times** the face value of the shares involved, up to **ten times** that face value or **₹10 crore**, whichever is higher; and
  - every officer in default is liable under **section 447**.

### Section 47. Voting rights

- **(1)** Subject to section 43(a)(ii) (differential rights) and section 50(2), every member of a company limited by shares who holds equity shares:
  - (a) has a right to vote on every resolution placed before the company; and
  - (b) on a poll, has voting rights in proportion to their share of the paid-up equity share capital.
- **(2)** Every member holding **preference shares**, for that capital:
  - may vote only on resolutions that **directly affect the rights** attached to their preference shares; and
  - may vote on any resolution for the **winding up** of the company, or for the **repayment or reduction** of its equity or preference share capital.
  - On a poll, their voting rights are in proportion to their share of the paid-up preference share capital.
  - Proviso: the proportion of voting rights of equity shareholders to preference shareholders is the same as the proportion of paid-up equity capital to paid-up preference capital.
  - Proviso: if the dividend on a class of preference shares has **not been paid for two years or more**, holders of that class may vote on **all resolutions** placed before the company.

### Section 48. Variation of shareholders' rights

- **(1)** If share capital is divided into different classes, the rights attached to a class may be varied with:
  - the **written consent** of holders of at least **three-fourths** of the issued shares of that class; or
  - a **special resolution** passed at a separate meeting of the holders of that class.
  - This is allowed:
    - (a) if the memorandum or articles provide for it; or
    - (b) if they are silent, so long as the terms of issue of that class do not prohibit it.
  - Proviso: if varying one class's rights affects the rights of another class, the consent of three-fourths of that other class must also be obtained, and this section applies to that variation as well.
- **(2)** Holders of at least **10%** of the issued shares of a class who did not consent or vote in favour may apply to the **Tribunal** to cancel the variation.
  - Once such an application is made, the variation has no effect unless and until the Tribunal confirms it.
  - The application must be made within **21 days** after the consent was given or the resolution was passed. It may be made on behalf of the entitled shareholders by one or more of them authorised in writing.
- **(3)** The Tribunal's decision on any such application is **binding** on the shareholders.
- **(4)** The company must file a copy of the Tribunal's order with the Registrar within **30 days** of the order.
- **(5) Punishment:** the original text provides a fine of **₹25,000 to ₹5 lakh** for the company, and for every officer in default imprisonment of up to **6 months**, or a fine of **₹25,000 to ₹5 lakh**, or both. This was amended by the 2020 amendment (the imprisonment was removed) [verify current wording].

### Section 49. Calls on shares of same class to be made on uniform basis

- When a company makes a call for further share capital on shares of a class, the call must be made on a **uniform basis** on all shares of that class.
- Explanation: shares of the same nominal value on which different amounts have been paid up are not treated as the same class for this purpose.

### Section 50. Company to accept unpaid share capital, although not called up

- **(1)** If its articles allow, a company may accept from a member all or part of the amount unpaid on their shares, even though none of it has been called up.
- **(2)** A member does not get voting rights for an amount paid under (1) until that amount has been called up.

### Section 51. Payment of dividend in proportion to amount paid-up

- If its articles allow, a company may pay dividends in proportion to the amount paid up on each share.

### Section 52. Application of premiums received on issue of shares

- **(1)** If a company issues shares at a premium, whether for cash or otherwise, the total premium must be transferred to a **"securities premium account"**. The rules on reducing share capital apply to this account as if it were paid-up share capital, except as provided in this section.
- **(2)** Despite (1), the securities premium account may be used:
  - (a) to issue unissued shares to members as fully paid **bonus shares**;
  - (b) to write off the company's **preliminary expenses**;
  - (c) to write off the expenses of, or the commission paid or discount allowed on, any issue of shares or debentures;
  - (d) to provide for the **premium payable on redemption** of redeemable preference shares or debentures; or
  - (e) to **buy back** its own shares or other securities under section 68.
- **(3)** For prescribed classes of companies whose financial statements comply with the accounting standards under section 133 (Ind AS), the securities premium account may be used only:
  - (a) to pay up unissued equity shares to be issued to members as fully paid bonus shares;
  - (b) to write off the expenses of, or commission paid or discount allowed on, any issue of equity shares; or
  - (c) to buy back its own shares or other securities under section 68.

### Section 53. Prohibition on issue of shares at discount

- **(1)** Except as provided in section 54, a company must not issue shares at a **discount**.
- **(2)** Any share issued at a discounted price is **void**.
- **(2A)** (Inserted in 2017.) A company may issue shares at a discount to its **creditors** when debt is converted into shares under a statutory resolution plan, or a debt restructuring scheme, made under RBI guidelines, directions or regulations under the RBI Act, 1934 or the Banking Regulation Act, 1949.
- **(3) Penalty (as amended in 2019):**
  - The company and every officer in default are each liable to a penalty of up to the amount raised through the discounted issue, or **₹5 lakh**, whichever is less.
  - The company must also refund all money received, with interest at **12% per year** from the date of issue until the date of refund.

### Section 54. Issue of sweat equity shares

- **(1)** Despite section 53, a company may issue **sweat equity shares** of a class of shares already issued, if all these conditions are met:
  - (a) the issue is authorised by a **special resolution** of the company;
  - (b) the resolution specifies the number of shares, their current market price, the consideration (if any), and the class or classes of directors or employees to whom they will be issued;
  - (c) *(omitted in 2017; it previously required at least one year to have passed since the company commenced business)*; and
  - (d) if the company's equity shares are listed on a recognised stock exchange, the issue follows **SEBI regulations**. If they are not listed, the issue follows the prescribed rules.
- **(2)** The rights, limitations, restrictions and provisions that apply to equity shares also apply to sweat equity shares. Holders of sweat equity shares rank equally (**pari passu**) with other equity shareholders.

### Section 55. Issue and redemption of preference shares

- **(1)** A company limited by shares must not issue preference shares that are **irredeemable**.
- **(2)** If its articles allow, a company limited by shares may issue preference shares that are to be redeemed within **20 years** from the date of issue, on the prescribed conditions.
  - Proviso: a company engaged in setting up and dealing with **infrastructure projects** (as listed in Schedule VI) may issue preference shares for more than 20 years. It must redeem a prescribed percentage of them each year at the option of the shareholders. The rules allow up to 30 years, with at least 10% redeemed each year from the 21st year [verify].
  - Further conditions:
    - (a) preference shares may be redeemed only out of the company's **profits available for dividend**, or out of the proceeds of a **fresh issue** of shares made for the purpose of redemption;
    - (b) preference shares may be redeemed only if they are **fully paid**;
    - (c) if shares are redeemed out of profits, an amount equal to the nominal value of the shares redeemed must be transferred from profits available for dividend to a **capital redemption reserve account**. The rules on reducing share capital apply to this reserve as if it were paid-up share capital, except as provided in this section;
    - (d)(i) for prescribed classes of companies whose financial statements follow the accounting standards under section 133, any premium payable on redemption must be provided **out of profits** before the shares are redeemed. Premium on preference shares issued before the Act began may be provided out of the securities premium account [verify];
    - (d)(ii) for other companies, any premium payable on redemption must be provided out of profits or out of the securities premium account before the shares are redeemed.
  - The capital redemption reserve account may be used to pay up unissued shares to be issued to members as fully paid **bonus shares**.
- **(3)** If a company cannot redeem preference shares, or pay dividend on them, under their terms of issue, it may issue **further redeemable preference shares** equal to the amount due, including dividend. This requires the consent of holders of **three-fourths in value** of those preference shares and the **Tribunal's** approval on a petition. On the new issue, the unredeemed preference shares are treated as redeemed.
  - Proviso: when approving, the Tribunal must order the immediate redemption of the preference shares held by those who did not consent.
  - Explanation: an issue under this sub-section is not treated as an increase in share capital.
- **(4)** Redeeming preference shares under this section is **not** treated as a reduction of authorised share capital.
- Explanation: "infrastructure projects" means the projects listed in **Schedule VI**.

### Section 56. Transfer and transmission of securities

- **(1)** A company must not register a transfer of securities (other than between persons both shown as beneficial owners in a depository's records) unless:
  - a proper instrument of transfer, in the prescribed form, duly stamped, dated and executed by or for both the transferor and the transferee, and stating the transferee's name, address and occupation (if any),
  - is delivered to the company by the transferor or transferee within **60 days** of its execution,
  - together with the share certificate or debenture certificate, or the letter of allotment if no certificate exists.
  - Proviso: if the instrument has been lost, or was not delivered within the prescribed period, the company may register the transfer on terms of indemnity that the Board thinks fit.
- **(2)** Sub-section (1) does not affect the company's power to register as a shareholder or debenture-holder any person to whom securities have passed by **operation of law** (transmission).
- **(3)** If the **transferor alone** applies to transfer **partly paid** shares, the transfer is registered only if the company has given notice to the transferee and the transferee raises **no objection within two weeks** of receiving the notice.
- **(4)** Unless a law or an order of a court, Tribunal or other authority prohibits it, every company must deliver the certificates of all securities allotted, transferred or transmitted:
  - (a) within **2 months** of incorporation, for subscribers to the memorandum;
  - (b) within **2 months** of allotment, for any allotment of shares;
  - (c) within **1 month** of receiving the instrument of transfer under (1), or the intimation of transmission under (2); and
  - (d) within **6 months** of allotment, for any allotment of debentures.
  - Proviso: if the securities are held with a depository, the company must inform the depository of the allotment details immediately after allotment.
- **(5)** A transfer of the securities or interest of a deceased member, made by their legal representative, is valid as if the legal representative had been a holder at the time of execution, even though they are not a holder themselves.
- **(6) Penalty** (as amended in 2020 [verify]): for a default under (1) to (5), the company and every officer in default are each liable to a penalty of **₹50,000**. (The earlier text provided for a fine of ₹25,000 to ₹5 lakh.)
- **(7)** Without affecting the SEBI Act, 1992 or the Depositories Act, 1996, a depository or depository participant that transfers shares with intent to defraud a person is liable under **section 447**.

### Section 57. Punishment for personation of shareholder

- This applies to a person who deceitfully personates the owner of any security or interest in a company, or of any share warrant or coupon issued under the Act, and by doing so:
  - gets or tries to get that security or interest; or
  - receives or tries to receive any money due to the owner.
- **Punishment:** imprisonment of **1 to 3 years**, and a fine of **₹1 lakh to ₹5 lakh**.

### Section 58. Refusal of registration and appeal against refusal

- **(1)** If a **private company limited by shares** refuses to register a transfer or transmission of securities, whether under its articles or otherwise, it must send notice of the refusal, with reasons, within **30 days** of receiving the instrument of transfer or intimation of transmission. The notice goes to the transferor and transferee, or to the person who gave the intimation of transmission.
- **(2)** Without affecting (1), the securities or other interest of any member in a **public company** are **freely transferable**.
  - Proviso: any contract or arrangement between two or more persons about the transfer of securities is enforceable as a contract.
- **(3)** For a private company, the transferee may appeal to the **Tribunal** against the refusal:
  - within **30 days** of receiving the notice of refusal; or
  - if no notice was sent, within **60 days** of the date the instrument of transfer or intimation of transmission was delivered to the company.
- **(4)** If a **public company** refuses to register a transfer within **30 days** of receiving the instrument or intimation without sufficient cause, the transferee may appeal to the Tribunal:
  - within **60 days** of the refusal; or
  - if the company sent no intimation, within **90 days** of the date the instrument of transfer or intimation of transmission was delivered.
- **(5)** After hearing the parties on an appeal under (3) or (4), the Tribunal may:
  - (a) dismiss the appeal; or
  - (b) by order:
    - (i) direct the company to register the transfer or transmission, which the company must do within **10 days** of receiving the order; or
    - (ii) direct rectification of the register and order the company to pay damages, if any, to the aggrieved party.
- **(6) Punishment for contravening the Tribunal's order:** imprisonment of **1 to 3 years**, and a fine of **₹1 lakh to ₹5 lakh**.

### Section 59. Rectification of register of members

- **(1)** The following may appeal to the **Tribunal** for rectification of the register of members:
  - the person aggrieved;
  - any member of the company; or
  - the company itself.
  - An appeal may be filed if, without sufficient cause:
    - a person's name is entered in the register; or
    - a person's name is omitted from the register after having been entered; or
    - there is a default or unnecessary delay in recording that a person has become, or stopped being, a member.
  - For foreign members or debenture-holders residing outside India, the appeal may instead be filed before a competent court outside India specified by the Central Government by notification.
- **(2)** After hearing the parties, the Tribunal may:
  - dismiss the appeal; or
  - order the company to register the transfer or transmission within **10 days** of receiving the order; or
  - direct rectification of the depository's records or the register, and order the company to pay damages, if any, to the aggrieved party.
- **(3)** This section does not restrict a holder's right to transfer their securities. A person who acquires securities is entitled to voting rights unless the Tribunal has suspended them by order.
- **(4)** If securities are transferred in breach of the SCRA, 1956, the SEBI Act, 1992, this Act or any other law, the Tribunal may, on an application, direct any company or depository to correct the breach and rectify its register or records. The application may be made by the depository, the company, the depository participant, the holder of the securities, or SEBI.
- **(5) Punishment for default in complying with the Tribunal's order** (as amended in 2020 [verify]):
  - The company: fine of **₹1 lakh to ₹5 lakh**.
  - Every officer in default: fine of **₹1 lakh to ₹3 lakh**.
  - (The original text also allowed imprisonment of up to 1 year for officers.)

### Section 60. Publication of authorised, subscribed and paid-up capital

- **(1)** If any notice, advertisement, other official publication, business letter, billhead or letter paper of a company states its **authorised capital**, it must also state its **subscribed** and **paid-up** capital, in an equally prominent position and in equally conspicuous characters.
- **(2) Penalty:**
  - The company: **₹10,000**.
  - Every officer in default: **₹5,000** for each default.
  - [verify — the amounts and daily element as converted to penalties by the 2019/2020 amendments.]

### Section 61. Power of limited company to alter its share capital

- **(1)** If its articles allow, a limited company with share capital may change the capital clause of its memorandum in **general meeting** to:
  - (a) **increase** its authorised share capital by any amount it thinks fit;
  - (b) **consolidate and divide** all or any of its share capital into shares of a larger amount than its existing shares.
    - Proviso: a consolidation and division that changes the voting percentage of shareholders needs the **Tribunal's** approval, on an application made in the prescribed manner.
  - (c) **convert** all or any of its fully paid-up shares into **stock**, and convert stock back into fully paid-up shares of any denomination;
  - (d) **sub-divide** its shares, or any of them, into shares of a smaller amount than the memorandum fixes. The proportion of paid and unpaid amounts on each reduced share must stay the same as on the share from which it is derived;
  - (e) **cancel** shares that, at the date of the resolution, have not been taken or agreed to be taken by any person, and reduce its share capital by the amount of the shares cancelled.
- **(2)** A cancellation under (1)(e) is **not** treated as a reduction of share capital.

### Section 62. Further issue of share capital

- **(1)** If a company with share capital proposes to increase its subscribed capital by issuing further shares, the shares must be offered:
  - **(a) Rights issue** to the existing equity shareholders as at the date of the offer, in proportion, as nearly as possible, to the paid-up share capital on their shares. This is done by letter of offer, on the following conditions:
    - (i) the offer is made by notice stating the number of shares offered. It must give a period of at least **15 days** (or a shorter period prescribed, as allowed by the 2020 amendment [verify]) and at most **30 days** from the date of the offer. If the offer is not accepted within that period, it is treated as declined.
    - (ii) unless the articles say otherwise, the offer includes a right for the shareholder to **renounce** all or any of the offered shares in favour of any other person. The notice must say this.
    - (iii) after the period ends, or earlier if the shareholder says they decline, the Board may dispose of the shares in a manner that is not disadvantageous to the shareholders and the company.
  - **(b) ESOP** to employees under an **employees' stock option scheme**, subject to a **special resolution** and the prescribed conditions; or
  - **(c) Preferential issue** to any persons, if authorised by **special resolution**, whether or not they include the persons in (a) or (b). The shares may be issued for cash or for consideration other than cash. The price must be determined by the **valuation report of a registered valuer**, subject to the prescribed conditions.
- **(2)** The notice under (1)(a)(i) must be sent to all existing shareholders by registered post, speed post, electronic mode or courier, or any other mode with proof of delivery, at least **three days** before the issue opens.
- **(3)** This section does not apply to an increase in subscribed capital caused by exercising an option, attached to debentures issued or loans raised, to convert them into shares.
  - Proviso: the terms of such debentures or loans must have been approved by a **special resolution** in general meeting before they were issued or raised.
- **(4)** Despite (3), if the **Government** has given a loan or subscribed to debentures, the Central Government may, in the public interest, order the debentures or loans to be converted into shares of the company on terms it considers reasonable.
  - If the company does not accept the terms, it may appeal to the **Tribunal** within **60 days** of receiving the order. The Tribunal's decision is final.
- **(5)** In setting the terms of conversion under (4), the Government must consider the company's financial position, the terms of issue of the debentures or loans, the interest rate, and other relevant matters.
- **(6)** If the Government orders conversion under (4), the company's authorised capital is treated as increased by the amount of the conversion to the extent needed, and the memorandum is treated as changed accordingly.

### Section 63. Issue of bonus shares

- **(1)** A company may issue fully paid-up **bonus shares** to its members, in any manner, out of:
  - (i) its **free reserves**;
  - (ii) its **securities premium account**; or
  - (iii) its **capital redemption reserve account**.
  - Proviso: bonus shares must not be issued by capitalising reserves created by **revaluation of assets**.
- **(2)** A company may capitalise its profits or reserves to issue fully paid-up bonus shares under (1) only if:
  - (a) its **articles** authorise it;
  - (b) the issue is authorised in **general meeting** on the Board's recommendation;
  - (c) it has **not defaulted** in paying interest or principal on fixed deposits or debt securities it has issued;
  - (d) it has **not defaulted** in paying statutory dues of employees, such as provident fund contributions, gratuity and bonus;
  - (e) any **partly paid-up** shares outstanding on the date of allotment are made fully paid up; and
  - (f) it meets any other prescribed conditions.
- **(3)** Bonus shares must not be issued **in lieu of dividend**.

### Section 64. Notice to be given to Registrar for alteration of share capital

- **(1)** The company must file a notice with the Registrar in the prescribed form within **30 days** of the alteration, order or redemption, together with the altered memorandum, in each of these cases:
  - (a) it alters its share capital in any way described in section 61(1);
  - (b) a Government order under section 62(4) increases its authorised capital; or
  - (c) it redeems any redeemable preference shares.
- **(2) Penalty (as amended in 2019):** the company and every officer in default are each liable to a penalty of **₹500 per day** of default. The maximum is **₹5 lakh** for the company and **₹1 lakh** for each officer in default [verify].

### Section 65. Unlimited company to provide for reserve share capital on conversion into limited company

- When an unlimited company with share capital passes a resolution to register as a limited company, the resolution may also do either or both of the following:
  - (a) **increase the nominal amount** of its share capital by increasing the nominal amount of each share. No part of the increase may be called up except if, and for the purposes of, the company being wound up.
  - (b) **provide** that a specified part of its uncalled share capital may not be called up except if, and for the purposes of, the company being wound up.

### Section 66. Reduction of share capital

- **(1)** A company limited by shares, or limited by guarantee and having share capital, may reduce its share capital in any manner by **special resolution**, subject to **confirmation by the Tribunal** on an application. In particular, it may:
  - (a) extinguish or reduce the liability on any of its shares for share capital not paid up; or
  - (b) whether or not it also extinguishes or reduces liability on any shares:
    - (i) **cancel** any paid-up share capital that is lost or not represented by available assets; or
    - (ii) **pay off** any paid-up share capital that is more than the company needs,
  - and change its memorandum by reducing the amount of its share capital and of its shares accordingly.
  - Proviso: no reduction may be made if the company is **in arrears** in repaying any deposits it accepted, whether before or after the Act began, or the interest on them.
- **(2)** The Tribunal must give notice of every application to the **Central Government**, the **Registrar**, **SEBI** (for listed companies) and the company's **creditors**. It must consider any representations made within **three months** of their receiving the notice. If no representation is received in that time, it is presumed that they have no objection.
- **(3)** The Tribunal may confirm the reduction on the terms it thinks fit if it is satisfied that the debt or claim of every creditor has been:
  - discharged or determined; or
  - secured; or
  - consented to.
  - Proviso: no application will be sanctioned unless the company's **auditor** certifies, in a certificate filed with the Tribunal, that the proposed accounting treatment conforms to the accounting standards under section 133 or any other provision of the Act.
- **(4)** The order confirming the reduction must be published in the manner the Tribunal directs.
- **(5)** The company must deliver to the Registrar, within **30 days** of receiving the order, a certified copy of the order and of a minute approved by the Tribunal. The minute must show:
  - (a) the amount of share capital;
  - (b) the number of shares into which it is to be divided;
  - (c) the amount of each share; and
  - (d) the amount, if any, treated as paid up on each share at the date of registration.
  - The Registrar registers these and issues a certificate.
- **(6)** This section does not apply to a **buy-back** of a company's own securities under section 68.
- **(7)** A past or present member is not liable to any call or contribution on a share beyond the difference, if any, between the amount paid on the share (or the reduced amount treated as paid) and the amount of the share as fixed by the order.
- **(8)** This applies where a creditor entitled to object is not entered on the list of creditors because they did not know of the proceedings, or of their nature and effect on the claim, and the company later cannot pay that creditor's debt or claim. In that case, every person who was a member at the date the order was registered is liable to contribute towards that debt. The amount is limited to what they would have had to contribute if the company had started winding up the day before that date.
- **(9)** Sub-section (8) does not affect the rights of the contributories among themselves.
- **(10)** An officer of the company is liable under **section 447** if they:
  - (a) knowingly conceal the name of any creditor entitled to object to the reduction;
  - (b) knowingly misrepresent the nature or amount of a creditor's debt or claim; or
  - (c) abet or are privy to any such concealment or misrepresentation.
- **(11)** *(Omitted by the Companies (Amendment) Act, 2020 [verify]. It previously provided a fine of ₹5 lakh to ₹25 lakh for failing to comply with sub-section (4).)*

### Section 67. Restrictions on purchase by company or giving of loans by it for purchase of its shares

- **(1)** A company limited by shares, or limited by guarantee and having share capital, may not buy its own shares unless the resulting reduction of share capital is made under this Act.
- **(2)** A **public company** must not give any **financial assistance**, directly or indirectly, for the purpose of, or in connection with, any person buying or subscribing for shares in the company or in its holding company. This covers loans, guarantees, provision of security and any other form of assistance.
- **(3)** Sub-section (2) does not apply to:
  - (a) a **banking company** lending money in the ordinary course of its business;
  - (b) a company providing money, under a scheme approved by **special resolution** and in the prescribed manner, to buy or subscribe for fully paid-up shares in the company or its holding company. The purchase or subscription must be made by **trustees for the benefit of employees**, or the shares must be held by or for the benefit of employees;
  - (c) a company giving **loans to its employees**, other than directors or KMP, to enable them to buy or subscribe for fully paid-up shares in the company or its holding company, to be held by them as beneficial owners. The loan must not exceed their salary or wages for **six months**.
  - Proviso: where employees do not exercise voting rights on shares under (b) directly, the company must disclose this in its Board's report as prescribed.
- **(4)** This section does not affect a company's right to **redeem** preference shares issued under this Act or any previous company law.
- **(5) Punishment:**
  - The company: fine of **₹1 lakh to ₹25 lakh**.
  - Every officer in default: imprisonment of up to **3 years**, and a fine of **₹1 lakh to ₹25 lakh**.
- (Private companies meeting certain conditions are exempted from this section by the 2015 exemption notification [verify].)

### Section 68. Power of company to purchase its own securities

- **(1)** Despite section 67, a company may buy back its own shares or other specified securities out of:
  - (a) its **free reserves**;
  - (b) its **securities premium account**; or
  - (c) the **proceeds of an issue** of any shares or other specified securities.
  - Proviso: a buy-back of a kind of shares or securities may not be made out of the proceeds of an earlier issue of the **same kind**.
- **(2)** A company may buy back only if all these conditions are met:
  - (a) its **articles** authorise the buy-back;
  - (b) a **special resolution** authorising it has been passed in general meeting.
    - Exception: no special resolution is needed if the buy-back is **10% or less** of the total paid-up equity capital and free reserves, and it is authorised by a **Board resolution** passed at a meeting.
    - Proviso: no offer under this exception may be made within **one year** from the date the previous buy-back offer, if any, closed.
  - (c) the buy-back is **25% or less** of the total paid-up capital and free reserves.
    - Proviso: for a buy-back of equity shares in a financial year, the 25% is calculated on the total paid-up **equity** capital in that financial year.
  - (d) after the buy-back, the company's total secured and unsecured debt is not more than **twice** its paid-up capital and free reserves. The Central Government may prescribe a higher ratio for a class of companies. (The 2017 amendment clarified that the ratio is tested on both standalone and consolidated financial statements [verify].)
  - (e) all shares or other specified securities to be bought back are **fully paid up**;
  - (f) a buy-back of **listed** securities follows SEBI regulations; and
  - (g) a buy-back of **unlisted** securities follows the prescribed rules.
- **(3)** The notice of the meeting for the special resolution must include an explanatory statement covering:
  - (a) a full and complete disclosure of all material facts;
  - (b) why the buy-back is needed;
  - (c) the class of shares or securities to be bought back;
  - (d) the amount to be invested in the buy-back; and
  - (e) the time limit for completing it.
- **(4)** Every buy-back must be completed within **one year** of the special resolution or Board resolution.
- **(5)** A buy-back may be made:
  - (a) from existing shareholders or security holders, on a **proportionate** basis;
  - (b) from the **open market**;
  - (c) by buying securities issued to **employees** under an employees' stock option scheme or as sweat equity.
- **(6)** After passing the resolution and before making the buy-back, the company must file a **declaration of solvency** with the Registrar and SEBI, in the prescribed form and verified by affidavit.
  - It must be signed by at least **two directors**, one of whom must be the managing director, if there is one.
  - It must state that the Board has made a full inquiry into the company's affairs and has formed the opinion that the company can meet its liabilities and will not become insolvent within **one year** from the date the Board adopts the declaration.
  - Proviso: an unlisted company need not file the declaration with SEBI.
- **(7)** Securities bought back must be **extinguished and physically destroyed** within **seven days** after the buy-back is completed.
- **(8)** After completing a buy-back, the company must not make a further issue of the same kind of shares or other securities, including through a rights issue under section 62(1)(a), within **six months**.
  - Exceptions: a bonus issue, or the discharge of existing obligations such as conversion of warrants, stock option schemes, sweat equity, or conversion of preference shares or debentures into equity shares.
- **(9)** The company must keep a **register** of the securities bought back. It must show the consideration paid, the dates of cancellation, extinguishing and physical destruction, and other prescribed particulars.
- **(10)** The company must file a **return** with the Registrar and SEBI within **30 days** of completing the buy-back, containing the prescribed particulars.
  - Proviso: an unlisted company need not file the return with SEBI.
- **(11) Punishment** for default in complying with this section, or with SEBI regulations under (2)(f) (as amended in 2020 [verify]):
  - The company: fine of **₹1 lakh to ₹3 lakh**.
  - Every officer in default: fine of **₹1 lakh to ₹3 lakh**.
  - (The original text also allowed imprisonment of up to 3 years for officers.)
- Explanation:
  - "**specified securities**" includes employees' stock options and any other securities the Central Government notifies.
  - "**free reserves**" includes the securities premium account.

### Section 69. Transfer of certain sums to capital redemption reserve account

- **(1)** If a company buys back its own shares out of **free reserves** or the **securities premium account**, it must transfer an amount equal to the nominal value of the shares bought back to the **capital redemption reserve account**. The details of the transfer must be disclosed in the balance sheet.
- **(2)** The capital redemption reserve account may be used to pay up unissued shares to be allotted to members as fully paid **bonus shares**.

### Section 70. Prohibition for buy-back in certain circumstances

- **(1)** A company must not buy back its own shares or other specified securities, directly or indirectly:
  - (a) through any **subsidiary**, including its own subsidiary companies;
  - (b) through any **investment company** or group of investment companies; or
  - (c) while any of the following **defaults** continue:
    - repayment of deposits (accepted before or after the Act began), or interest on them;
    - redemption of debentures or preference shares;
    - payment of dividend to any shareholder; or
    - repayment of any term loan, or interest on it, to any financial institution or banking company.
  - Proviso: the prohibition in (c) ends once the default has been remedied and **three years** have passed since it stopped.
- **(2)** A company must not buy back its own shares or other specified securities, directly or indirectly, if it has not complied with sections **92** (annual return), **123** (declaration of dividend), **127** (failure to distribute dividend) and **129** (financial statements).

### Section 71. Debentures

- **(1)** A company may issue debentures with an option to convert them into shares, wholly or partly, at the time of redemption.
  - Proviso: an issue of convertible debentures must be approved by a **special resolution** in general meeting.
- **(2)** A company must not issue debentures with **voting rights**.
- **(3)** **Secured** debentures may be issued on the prescribed terms and conditions.
- **(4)** If debentures are issued by a prescribed class of companies, the company must create a **debenture redemption reserve account** out of its profits available for dividend. The amount in this account may be used only to redeem debentures. (The 2019 amendment limited this to prescribed classes of companies, and the rules exempt several categories [verify].)
- **(5)** A company must appoint one or more **debenture trustees** before issuing a prospectus, or before making an offer or invitation to the public or to more than **500** of its members, to subscribe for its debentures. The appointment must meet the prescribed conditions.
- **(6)** A debenture trustee must take steps to protect the interests of debenture-holders and resolve their grievances, as prescribed.
- **(7)** Any provision in a trust deed, or in a contract with debenture-holders secured by a trust deed, is **void** to the extent it would exempt a trustee from liability for breach of trust, or indemnify the trustee against it, where the trustee fails to show the care and diligence required of a trustee. This is judged with regard to the powers, authority and discretion the trust deed gives the trustee.
  - Proviso: the trustee's liability is subject to any exemptions agreed by a majority of debenture-holders holding at least **three-fourths in value** of the total debentures, at a meeting held for that purpose.
- **(8)** A company must pay interest on, and redeem, its debentures according to their terms and conditions of issue.
- **(9)** If a debenture trustee concludes at any time that the company's assets are, or are likely to become, insufficient to pay the principal when due, the trustee may file a petition with the **Tribunal**. After hearing the company and any other interested person, the Tribunal may restrict the company from incurring further liabilities as it considers necessary to protect the debenture-holders.
- **(10)** If a company fails to redeem debentures on maturity, or to pay interest when due, the Tribunal may, on the application of any or all debenture-holders or the debenture trustee, and after hearing the parties, order the company to redeem the debentures immediately by paying the principal and interest due.
- **(11) Punishment** for failing to comply with the Tribunal's order under (10): every officer in default is liable to imprisonment of up to **3 years**, or a fine of **₹2 lakh to ₹5 lakh**, or both [verify whether amended in 2020].
- **(12)** A contract with a company to take up and pay for any of its debentures may be enforced by a decree for **specific performance**.
- **(13)** The Central Government may prescribe:
  - the procedure for securing the issue of debentures;
  - the form of the debenture trust deed;
  - the procedure for debenture-holders to inspect the trust deed and get copies;
  - the amount of the debenture redemption reserve; and
  - other related matters.

### Section 72. Power to nominate

- **(1)** Every holder of securities may, at any time, nominate any person in the prescribed manner to receive their securities if they die.
- **(2)** If securities are held **jointly**, the joint holders may together nominate any person to receive all the rights in the securities if all of them die.
- **(3)** Despite anything in any other law, or in any will or other disposition, a nomination made in the prescribed manner gives the nominee all the rights in the securities on the death of the holder, or of all the joint holders. The nominee takes these rights to the **exclusion of all other persons**, unless the nomination is varied or cancelled in the prescribed manner.
- **(4)** If the nominee is a **minor**, the holder may nominate a person to receive the securities if the holder dies while the nominee is a minor.

## CHAPTER V — ACCEPTANCE OF DEPOSITS BY COMPANIES

### Section 73. Prohibition on acceptance of deposits from public

- **(1)** From the commencement of this Act, no company may invite, accept or renew **deposits** from the public except as provided in this Chapter.
  - Proviso: this does not apply to:
    - a **banking company**;
    - a **non-banking financial company** as defined in the RBI Act, 1934; or
    - any other company that the Central Government specifies, after consulting the RBI.
- **(2)** A company may accept deposits from its **members** if it passes a resolution in general meeting and follows the rules made in consultation with the RBI. It must also meet these conditions:
  - (a) issue a **circular** to its members. The circular must include a statement of the company's financial position, its credit rating, the total number of depositors, the amount due on any earlier deposits, and other prescribed particulars, in the prescribed form and manner;
  - (b) file a copy of the circular with the **Registrar** within **30 days** before the date it is issued;
  - (c) deposit at least **20%** of the deposits maturing in the next financial year in a separate account with a scheduled bank, called the **"deposit repayment reserve account"**, and keep it there. (This was 15% before the 2017 amendment [verify].)
  - (d) *(omitted by the 2017 amendment; it previously required deposit insurance)*;
  - (e) certify that it has **not defaulted** in repaying deposits, or paying interest on them, whether the deposits were accepted before or after the Act began; and
  - (f) provide **security**, if any, for repaying the deposit and interest, including by creating a charge on its property or assets.
  - Proviso (2017): a company that has defaulted in repaying deposits or paying interest on them may accept deposits only after making good the default and after **five years** have passed since it did so [verify].
  - Proviso: if a company does not secure its deposits, or secures them only partly, they must be called **"unsecured deposits"** and described that way in every circular, form, advertisement or document relating to them.
- **(3)** Every deposit accepted under (2) must be **repaid with interest** according to the terms of the agreement.
- **(4)** If a company fails to repay a deposit, or interest on it, within the time in (3) or any further time the Tribunal allows, the depositor may apply to the **Tribunal**. The Tribunal may order the company to pay the amount due, and any loss or damage the depositor has suffered because of the non-payment, and may make any other order it thinks fit.

### Section 74. Repayment of deposits, etc., accepted before commencement of this Act

- **(1)** This applies to any deposit accepted before this Act began where the deposit, any part of it, or any interest on it was unpaid at commencement or becomes due afterwards. In that case, the company must:
  - (a) file with the Registrar, within **three months** of commencement or of the date the payment becomes due, a statement of all such deposits, the amounts still unpaid with interest, and its arrangements for repaying them; and
  - (b) repay the deposits within **one year** of commencement or of the date they become due, whichever is earlier. (As amended in 2015.)
- **(2)** On the company's application, the Tribunal may allow further time for repayment that it considers reasonable. It must consider the company's financial condition, the amount of the deposits and interest due, and any other relevant matters.
- **(3) Punishment** for failing to repay within the time in (1), or any further time allowed under (2):
  - The company: fine of **₹1 crore to ₹10 crore**, in addition to paying the deposit and interest.
  - Every officer in default: imprisonment of up to **7 years**, or a fine of **₹25 lakh to ₹2 crore**, or both.

### Section 75. Damages for fraud

- **(1)** This applies where a company fails to repay a deposit, any part of it, or any interest on it, within the time specified in **section 74(1)** or the further time allowed by the Tribunal under **section 74(2)**, and it is proved that the deposits were accepted with intent to **defraud** depositors or for any fraudulent purpose. (The 2015 amendment extended this to deposits accepted under sections 73 and 76 [verify].)
  - Every officer of the company who was responsible for accepting the deposit is **personally responsible, without any limit of liability**, for all or any losses or damages the depositors suffer.
  - This applies in addition to the punishment under section 74(3), and to any other liability.
- **(2)** A suit or other action may be brought by any person, group of persons or association of persons who suffered loss because the company failed to repay the deposits or interest.

### Section 76. Acceptance of deposits from public by certain companies

- **(1)** Despite section 73, a **public company** may accept deposits from persons other than its members (an **"eligible company"**) if it meets all these conditions:
  - it has a **net worth of at least ₹100 crore** or a **turnover of at least ₹500 crore**;
  - it has obtained the prior consent of the company in general meeting by **special resolution**, and filed the resolution with the Registrar before inviting deposits from the public;
  - it complies with the requirements of section 73(2); and
  - it follows the rules made in consultation with the RBI.
  - Proviso: if the deposits are within the limit under section 180(1)(c), an **ordinary resolution** is enough.
  - Proviso: the company must get a **credit rating**, including its net worth rating, from a recognised credit rating agency, so that the public knows the rating when deposits are invited. It must get the rating renewed **every year** while the deposits are outstanding.
  - Proviso: a company accepting **secured deposits** from the public must, within **30 days** of accepting them, create a charge on its assets in favour of the depositors. The charge must be for at least the amount of the deposits accepted, as prescribed.
- **(2)** The provisions of this Chapter apply, with necessary changes, to deposits accepted from the public under this section.

### Section 76A. Punishment for contravention of section 73 or section 76

- *(Inserted by the Companies (Amendment) Act, 2015.)*
- This applies if a company:
  - accepts or invites deposits, or allows or causes any other person to accept or invite deposits, in breach of the manner or conditions in **section 73 or 76** or the rules under them; or
  - fails to repay a deposit, any part of it, or any interest on it, within the time specified under section 73 or 76, or any further time allowed by the Tribunal under section 73.
- **Punishment:**
  - **(a) The company:** must repay the deposit and the interest due, and in addition is liable to a fine of **₹1 crore to ₹10 crore**.
  - **(b) Every officer in default:** imprisonment of up to **7 years**, and a fine of **₹25 lakh to ₹2 crore**.
  - Proviso: if it is proved that the officer acted **knowingly or wilfully** with intent to deceive the company, its shareholders, depositors, creditors or tax authorities, the officer is liable under **section 447**.

## CHAPTER VI — REGISTRATION OF CHARGES

### Section 77. Duty to register charges, etc.

- **(1)** Every company that creates a **charge** must register its particulars with the Registrar. This applies to any charge created in or outside India on its property or assets, or any of its undertakings, whether tangible or not, and whether situated in or outside India.
  - The particulars must be signed by the company and the charge-holder, filed with the instruments creating or modifying the charge, in the prescribed form, and with the prescribed fee.
  - The filing must be made within **30 days** of the charge being created.
  - First proviso (as substituted in 2019): on the company's application, the Registrar may allow registration:
    - (a) for charges created **before** the 2019 amendment: within **300 days** of creation, on payment of the prescribed additional fee;
    - (b) for charges created **on or after** the 2019 amendment: within **60 days** of creation, on payment of the prescribed additional fee.
  - Second proviso: if registration is not made within the time in the first proviso:
    - (a) for charges under clause (a): registration must be made within **six months** from the commencement of the 2019 amendment, on payment of the prescribed additional fee. Different fees may apply to different classes of companies.
    - (b) for charges under clause (b): the Registrar may, on application, allow registration within a **further 60 days**, on payment of the prescribed **ad valorem** fee.
  - Third proviso: registering a charge late does not affect any rights in the property acquired before the charge is actually registered.
- **(2)** Once a charge is registered under (1), the Registrar issues a **certificate of registration** in the prescribed form to the company and to the person in whose favour the charge is created.
- **(3)** Despite anything in any other law, a charge created by a company is **not taken into account** by the liquidator appointed under this Act or the Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code, 2016, or by any other creditor, unless it has been registered under (1) and a certificate issued under (2).
- **(4)** Sub-section (3) does not affect any contract or obligation to repay the money secured by the charge.

### Section 78. Application for registration of charge

- If a company fails to register a charge within the time in section 77, the person in whose favour the charge is created may apply to the Registrar to register it, along with the instrument creating the charge. This does not affect the company's own liability.
- After giving notice to the company in the prescribed manner, the Registrar may allow the registration within **14 days** of the notice [verify], unless the company itself registers the charge or shows sufficient cause why it should not be registered.
- If the charge is registered on the charge-holder's application, that person may **recover** from the company any fees or additional fees they paid for the registration.

### Section 79. Section 77 to apply in certain matters

- The provisions of section 77 on registering charges also apply, so far as possible, to:
  - (a) a company **acquiring any property** that is subject to a charge under that section; and
  - (b) any **modification** of the terms, conditions, extent or operation of a charge registered under that section.

### Section 80. Date of notice of charge

- If a charge on any property, assets or undertaking of a company is registered under section 77, any person who acquires that property, those assets or that undertaking, or any part of it or any share or interest in it, is treated as having **notice of the charge** from the date of registration.

### Section 81. Register of charges to be kept by Registrar

- **(1)** For every company, the Registrar keeps a **register** of the particulars of charges registered under this Chapter, in the prescribed form and manner.
- **(2)** Any person may inspect the register on payment of the prescribed fee for each inspection.

### Section 82. Company to report satisfaction of charge

- **(1)** A company must inform the Registrar, in the prescribed form, when a registered charge is **paid or satisfied in full**. It must do so within **30 days** of the payment or satisfaction.
  - Proviso (as amended in 2019 [verify]): the first proviso to section 77(1) (extended time on additional fees) applies, so far as possible, to this intimation. (The original proviso allowed intimation within 300 days on payment of additional fees.)
- **(2)** On receiving the intimation, the Registrar sends a notice to the charge-holder asking them to show cause, within the time stated in the notice (at most **14 days**), why the payment or satisfaction should not be recorded as reported.
  - If the charge-holder shows no cause, the Registrar orders a **memorandum of satisfaction** to be entered in the register of charges and informs the company.
  - If the charge-holder shows cause, the Registrar records a note to that effect in the register and informs the company.
- **(3)** Sub-section (2) does not apply where the intimation in the prescribed form is **signed by the charge-holder**.
- **(4)** If the Registrar enters a memorandum of satisfaction in full, the Registrar must give the company a copy of it [verify sub-section structure].

### Section 83. Power of Registrar to make entries of satisfaction and release in absence of intimation from company

- **(1)** The Registrar may enter a memorandum of satisfaction, or of release, in the register of charges even without any intimation from the company, if satisfied on evidence that:
  - (a) the debt secured by the charge has been paid or satisfied, in whole or in part; or
  - (b) part of the charged property or undertaking has been released from the charge, or has stopped being part of the company's property or undertaking.
  - The entry records satisfaction in whole or part, or the release, as the case may be.
- **(2)** The Registrar must inform the affected parties within **30 days** of making the entry.

### Section 84. Intimation of appointment of receiver or manager

- **(1)** If any person obtains an order appointing a **receiver** of, or a person to **manage**, property subject to a charge of a company, or appoints one under a power in an instrument, they must notify the company and the Registrar. The notice must be given within **30 days** of the order or appointment, with a copy of the order or instrument. The Registrar records the particulars in the register of charges on payment of the prescribed fee.
- **(2)** When the person appointed stops holding the appointment, they must notify the company and the Registrar, with the prescribed particulars. The Registrar records the notice in the register of charges.

### Section 85. Company's register of charges

- **(1)** Every company must keep a **register of charges** at its registered office, in the prescribed form. It must list all charges and floating charges affecting any of its property, assets or undertakings, with the prescribed particulars, and copies of the instruments creating them.
  - Proviso: entries must be made immediately after any charge is created, modified or satisfied.
- **(2)** The register and the copies of instruments may be inspected during business hours, subject to any reasonable restrictions in the articles:
  - (a) by any **member or creditor**, free of charge; and
  - (b) by any **other person**, on payment of the prescribed fee.
  - The rules require the register to be kept permanently, and instruments to be kept for **8 years** after the charge is satisfied [verify].

### Section 86. Punishment for contravention

- **(1) Penalty (as amended in 2019):** if a company defaults in complying with any provision of this Chapter:
  - the company is liable to a penalty of **₹5 lakh**; and
  - every officer in default is liable to a penalty of **₹50,000**.
- **(2)** Any person who **wilfully** gives false or incorrect information, or **knowingly** suppresses any material information, that must be registered under section 77 is liable under **section 447**.

### Section 87. Rectification by Central Government in register of charges

- **(1)** The Central Government (power exercised by the Regional Director) may act under this section in either of these situations:
  - (a) it is satisfied that:
    - (i) the failure to inform the Registrar of the payment or satisfaction of a charge within the time required by this Chapter; or
    - (ii) the omission or mis-statement of any particulars of a charge, a modification, or a memorandum of satisfaction or other entry under section 82 or 83,
    - was accidental, due to inadvertence or some other sufficient cause, or is not of a nature to prejudice the position of creditors or shareholders; or
  - (b) it is satisfied that it is just and equitable on any other ground to grant relief.
  - In that case, on the application of the company or any interested person, and on terms it thinks just and expedient, it may direct that:
    - the time for giving the intimation of payment or satisfaction be **extended**; or
    - the omission or mis-statement be **rectified**.
  - (Before 2019, this power also covered extending the time for registering a charge or its modification [verify].)
- **(2)** If the Central Government extends the time for registering a charge, its order does not affect any rights in the property concerned acquired before the charge is actually registered.


> Plain-language restatement (not statutory text). Reflects amendments by the Companies (Amendment) Acts of 2015, 2017, 2019 and 2020 to the extent known. Items marked `[verify]` should be checked against the official text and the relevant Rules.

## CHAPTER VII — MANAGEMENT AND ADMINISTRATION

### Section 88. Register of members, etc.
(1) Every company must keep and maintain, in the prescribed form, the following registers:
  - (a) a register of members, showing separately each class of equity and preference shares held by each member, whether residing in or outside India;
  - (b) a register of debenture-holders; and
  - (c) a register of any other security holders.
(2) Each register maintained under sub-section (1) must include an index of names in it.
(3) The register and index of beneficial owners kept by a depository under section 11 of the Depositories Act, 1996 is treated as the corresponding register and index for this Act.
(4) If its articles authorise it, a company may keep, in the prescribed manner, part of any register under sub-section (1) (a foreign register) for members, debenture-holders, other security holders or beneficial owners resident outside India.
(5) Default in maintaining registers as required (post-2020 amendment): the company and every officer in default are liable to a penalty of ₹50,000, plus ₹200 per day for a continuing failure after the first day, up to a maximum of ₹3 lakh for the company and ₹1 lakh for an officer in default.

### Section 89. Declaration in respect of beneficial interest in any share
(1) A person whose name is entered in the register of members as holder of shares, but who does not hold the beneficial interest in them, must declare to the company the name and other particulars of the person who holds the beneficial interest, in the prescribed form and within the prescribed time.
(2) Every person who holds or acquires a beneficial interest in a share must declare to the company the nature of that interest, particulars of the person in whose name the shares are registered, and other prescribed particulars.
(3) If the beneficial interest changes, both the registered holder under (1) and the beneficial owner under (2) must make a declaration to the company in the prescribed form within 30 days of the change.
(4) The Central Government may make rules on the manner of holding and disclosing beneficial interest and beneficial ownership under this section.
(5) A person who fails to make a declaration under (1), (2) or (3) is liable to a penalty of ₹50,000, plus ₹200 per day for continuing failure after the first day, subject to a maximum of ₹5 lakh (2019 amendment).
(6) On receiving a declaration, the company must note it in the relevant register and file a return in the prescribed form with the Registrar within 30 days of receipt, with the prescribed fees.
(7) If the company fails to file the return within time, the company and every officer in default are liable to a penalty of ₹1,000 per day of continuing failure, up to a maximum of ₹5 lakh for the company and ₹2 lakh for an officer in default (2019 amendment).
(8) No right in respect of a share for which a required declaration has not been made by the beneficial owner is enforceable by that owner or anyone claiming through him.
(9) This section does not affect the company's obligation to pay dividend to its members; payment discharges that obligation.
(10) For this section and section 90, "beneficial interest" in a share includes any right or entitlement, held directly or indirectly, through any contract, arrangement or otherwise, alone or with others, to:
  - (i) exercise, or cause to be exercised, any or all rights attached to the share; or
  - (ii) receive or participate in any dividend or other distribution on the share.

### Section 90. Register of significant beneficial owners in a company
(1) Every individual who, acting alone or together, or through one or more persons or a trust (including a trust and persons resident outside India), holds beneficial interest of not less than 25% (or such other percentage as prescribed — the Rules prescribe 10%) in shares of a company, or has the right to exercise, or actually exercises, significant influence or control as defined in section 2(27) over the company (a "significant beneficial owner"), must declare to the company the nature of his interest and other particulars, in the prescribed manner and time. The Central Government may prescribe classes of persons not required to make the declaration.
(2) The company must maintain a register of the interest so declared, containing the prescribed particulars.
(3) The register under (2) is open to inspection by any member on payment of prescribed fees.
(4) The company must file a return of significant beneficial owners and changes in them with the Registrar, in the prescribed form, manner and time.
(4A) Every company must take the prescribed steps to identify any individual who is a significant beneficial owner and require him to comply with this section (2017 amendment).
(5) The company must give notice, in the prescribed manner, to any person (whether or not a member) whom it knows or has reasonable cause to believe:
  - (a) to be a significant beneficial owner;
  - (b) to have knowledge of the identity of a significant beneficial owner or of another person likely to have such knowledge; or
  - (c) to have been a significant beneficial owner at any time during the three years immediately before the notice,
  and who is not registered as such.
(6) The person receiving the notice must give the information required within 30 days of the notice.
(7) If the person fails to give the information within the time, or the information is not satisfactory, the company must apply to the Tribunal for an order directing that the shares in question be subject to restrictions, including restrictions on transfer, suspension of right to receive dividend or other distribution, and suspension of voting rights.
(8) After giving those concerned an opportunity of being heard, the Tribunal may make such order restricting the rights attached to the shares as it thinks fit, within the prescribed period.
(9) The company or the aggrieved person may apply to the Tribunal for relaxation or lifting of the restrictions within one year of the order; if no such application is made within one year, the shares are to be transferred, without restrictions, to the Investor Education and Protection Fund in the prescribed manner. [verify]
(9A) The Central Government may make rules for the purposes of this section.
(10) A person who fails to make a declaration under (1) is liable to a penalty of ₹50,000, plus ₹1,000 per day for continuing failure, up to a maximum of ₹2 lakh (2020 amendment). [verify]
(11) If a company fails to maintain the register, file the return, or allow inspection as required, the company is liable to a penalty of ₹1 lakh plus ₹500 per day for continuing failure (maximum ₹5 lakh), and every officer in default to ₹25,000 plus ₹200 per day (maximum ₹1 lakh) (2020 amendment). [verify]
(12) A person who wilfully furnishes false or incorrect information, or suppresses material information, in a declaration under this section is liable to action under section 447.

### Section 91. Power to close register of members or debenture-holders or other security holders
(1) A company may close the register of members, debenture-holders or other security holders for any period(s) not exceeding 45 days in aggregate in a year, but not more than 30 days at any one time, after giving at least 7 days' prior notice (or such lesser period as SEBI may specify for listed companies or those intending to list) in the prescribed manner.
(2) If the register is closed without notice, or after shorter notice, or for a continuous or aggregate period exceeding the limits in (1), the company and every officer in default are liable to a penalty of ₹5,000 for every day of closure, up to ₹1 lakh (2019 amendment).

### Section 92. Annual return
(1) Every company must prepare an annual return in the prescribed form, with particulars as at the close of the financial year concerning:
  - (a) its registered office, principal business activities, and particulars of its holding, subsidiary and associate companies;
  - (b) its shares, debentures and other securities, and its shareholding pattern;
  - (c) [Omitted by the Companies (Amendment) Act, 2017 — formerly its indebtedness] [verify]
  - (d) its members and debenture-holders, with changes since the close of the previous financial year;
  - (e) its promoters, directors and key managerial personnel, with changes since the close of the previous financial year;
  - (f) meetings of members or a class of members, the Board and its committees, with attendance details;
  - (g) remuneration of directors and key managerial personnel;
  - (h) penalties or punishments imposed on the company, its directors or officers, and details of compounding of offences and appeals against penalties/punishments;
  - (i) matters relating to certification of compliances and disclosures as prescribed;
  - (j) details, as prescribed, of shares held by or on behalf of Foreign Institutional Investors [verify whether omitted by 2017 amendment]; and
  - (k) such other matters as prescribed.
  The return is signed by a director and the company secretary, or, if there is no company secretary, by a company secretary in practice. For a One Person Company and a small company, it is signed by the company secretary, or where there is none, by the director. The Central Government may prescribe an abridged form of annual return for One Person Companies, small companies and other prescribed classes (2017 amendment).
(2) The annual return of a listed company, or of a company with paid-up capital and turnover above the prescribed levels, must be certified by a company secretary in practice in the prescribed form, stating that it discloses the facts correctly and adequately and that the company has complied with the Act.
(3) Every company must place a copy of its annual return on its website, if any, and disclose the web-link in the Board's report (substituted by the 2017 amendment in place of attaching an extract).
(4) Every company must file a copy of the annual return with the Registrar within 60 days of the date of its annual general meeting, or, where no AGM is held in a year, within 60 days of the date by which it should have been held, together with a statement of the reasons for not holding it, with the prescribed fees or additional fees within the time specified in section 403.
(5) If a company fails to file the annual return under (4) before the expiry of the period specified, the company and every officer in default are liable to a penalty of ₹10,000, plus ₹100 per day for continuing failure, up to a maximum of ₹2 lakh for the company and ₹50,000 for an officer in default (2020 amendment).
(6) A company secretary in practice who certifies an annual return otherwise than in conformity with this section or the rules is liable to a penalty of ₹2 lakh (2020 amendment). [verify]

### Section 93. [Omitted]
Formerly "Return to be filed with Registrar in case promoters' stake changes"; omitted by the Companies (Amendment) Act, 2017.

### Section 94. Place of keeping and inspection of registers, returns, etc.
(1) The registers under section 88 and copies of annual returns under section 92 must be kept at the registered office. They may instead be kept at another place in India where more than one-tenth of the total members on the register of members reside, if a special resolution so approves and a copy of the proposed special resolution has been given to the Registrar in advance.
(2) The registers and their indices (except when closed under section 91) and the copies of returns must be open for inspection during business hours, subject to reasonable restrictions under the articles:
  - (a) by any member, debenture-holder, other security holder or beneficial owner, without fee; and
  - (b) by any other person, on payment of the prescribed fee.
(3) Any such person may take extracts without fee or extra charge, and may require a copy of any register, index or return on payment of the prescribed fee.
(4) If inspection is refused, or a requested copy is not sent within the prescribed time, the company and every officer in default are liable to a penalty of ₹1,000 for every day of default, up to ₹1 lakh (2019 amendment); and the Central Government may by order direct immediate inspection or supply of the copy.

### Section 95. Registers, etc., to be evidence
The registers, their indices and the copies of annual returns maintained under sections 88 and 92 are prima facie evidence of any matter that the Act directs or authorises to be recorded in them.

### Section 96. Annual general meeting
(1) Every company other than a One Person Company must hold an annual general meeting (AGM) each year, in addition to any other meetings, and must specify it as such in the notice calling it. Not more than 15 months may elapse between two AGMs.
  - The first AGM must be held within 9 months from the close of the first financial year; subsequent AGMs within 6 months from the close of the financial year.
  - If the first AGM is held as above, no AGM need be held in the year of incorporation.
  - The Registrar may, for any special reason, extend the time for holding any AGM (other than the first AGM) by up to 3 months.
(2) Every AGM must be called during business hours (between 9 a.m. and 6 p.m.) on a day that is not a national holiday, and held at the registered office or at some other place in the city, town or village where the registered office is situated.
  - The Central Government may exempt any company or class of companies from this sub-section, subject to conditions.
  - An unlisted company's AGM may be held anywhere in India if all members consent in advance, in writing or electronically (2017 amendment).
  - Explanation: "national holiday" means a day declared as such by the Central Government.

### Section 97. Power of Tribunal to call annual general meeting
(1) If a company defaults in holding an AGM under section 96, the Tribunal may, on application by any member, notwithstanding anything in the Act or the articles, call or direct the calling of the AGM and give ancillary or consequential directions, including a direction that one member present in person or by proxy shall be deemed to constitute a meeting.
(2) A meeting held under such order is deemed to be an AGM of the company under this Act.

### Section 98. Power of Tribunal to call meetings of members, etc.
(1) If, for any reason, it is impracticable to call a meeting of the company (other than an AGM) in any manner in which meetings may be called, or to hold or conduct it in the manner prescribed by the Act or articles, the Tribunal may, on its own motion or on application by any director or member entitled to vote, order the meeting to be called, held and conducted as it thinks fit, and give ancillary or consequential directions, including that one member present in person or by proxy shall be deemed to constitute a meeting.
(2) A meeting so called, held and conducted is deemed for all purposes a meeting of the company duly called, held and conducted.

### Section 99. Punishment for default in complying with provisions of sections 96 to 98
If default is made in holding a meeting under section 96, or in complying with a Tribunal direction under section 97 or 98, the company and every officer in default are punishable with fine up to ₹1 lakh, and, for a continuing default, a further fine up to ₹5,000 for each day the default continues.

### Section 100. Calling of extraordinary general meeting
(1) The Board may call an extraordinary general meeting (EGM) whenever it thinks fit. An EGM of a company, other than a wholly owned subsidiary of a company incorporated outside India, must be held at a place within India (2017 amendment).
(2) The Board must call an EGM on the requisition of:
  - (a) in a company with share capital, members holding at least one-tenth of the paid-up share capital that carries voting rights on the date of requisition;
  - (b) in a company without share capital, members having at least one-tenth of the total voting power on that date.
(3) The requisition must set out the matters for consideration, be signed by the requisitionists, and be sent to the registered office.
(4) If the Board does not, within 21 days of receiving a valid requisition, proceed to call a meeting for a day not later than 45 days from receipt, the requisitionists may themselves call and hold the meeting within 3 months from the date of the requisition.
(5) A meeting called by requisitionists under (4) must be called in the same manner as a Board-called meeting; the company must reimburse the requisitionists' reasonable expenses and recover them from fees or remuneration of the defaulting directors.

### Section 101. Notice of meeting
(1) A general meeting may be called by giving at least 21 clear days' notice in writing or by electronic mode, in the prescribed manner. A meeting may be called at shorter notice if at least 95% of the members entitled to vote at it consent in writing or electronically (2017 amendment). Where some members are entitled to vote only on some resolutions, they are counted for those resolutions only.
(2) The notice must state the place, date, day and hour of the meeting and contain a statement of the business to be transacted.
(3) Notice must be given to:
  - (a) every member, the legal representative of any deceased member, and the assignee of an insolvent member;
  - (b) the auditor(s) of the company; and
  - (c) every director.
(4) Accidental omission to give notice to, or non-receipt by, any member or other entitled person does not invalidate the proceedings.

### Section 102. Statement to be annexed to notice
(1) A statement setting out the following material facts concerning each item of special business must be annexed to the notice of a general meeting:
  - (a) the nature of concern or interest, financial or otherwise, if any, in each item of:
    - (i) every director and manager, if any;
    - (ii) every other key managerial personnel; and
    - (iii) relatives of the persons in (i) and (ii); and
  - (b) any other information and facts that may enable members to understand the meaning, scope and implications of the item and take a decision.
(2) For this section:
  - (a) at an AGM, all business is special business except: (i) consideration of financial statements and reports of the Board and auditors; (ii) declaration of dividend; (iii) appointment of directors in place of those retiring; and (iv) appointment of auditors and fixing their remuneration;
  - (b) at any other meeting, all business is special business.
  Where any item of special business relates to or affects another company, the shareholding interest in that other company of every promoter, director, manager and key managerial personnel of the first company must also be stated, if it is not less than 2% of that other company's paid-up share capital.
(3) Where an item of business refers to a document to be considered, the time and place for its inspection must be specified in the statement.
(4) If non-disclosure or insufficient disclosure in the statement results in any benefit to a promoter, director, manager or KMP or their relatives, that person holds the benefit in trust for the company and must compensate the company to the extent of the benefit received, without prejudice to other action.
(5) If default is made in complying with this section, every promoter, director, manager or other KMP in default is liable to a penalty of ₹50,000 or five times the amount of benefit accruing to them or their relatives, whichever is more (2019 amendment).

### Section 103. Quorum for meetings
(1) Unless the articles provide for a larger number:
  - (a) for a public company, the quorum is:
