(5) The qualifications, disqualifications, rights, duties and obligations of auditors under this Chapter apply, so far as may be, to a cost auditor; the company must give the cost auditor all assistance and facilities. The cost audit report is submitted by the cost accountant in practice to the Board.
(6) The company must, within 30 days of receiving the cost audit report, furnish the Central Government with the report along with full information and explanation on every reservation or qualification in it.
(7) If, after considering the report and the company's information and explanations, the Central Government considers further information or explanation necessary, it may call for it, and the company must furnish it within the time specified.
(8) If default is made in complying with this section:
  - (a) the company and every officer in default are punishable/liable in the manner provided in section 147(1); and
  - (b) the cost auditor in default is punishable in the manner provided in section 147(2) to (4). [verify]

## CHAPTER XI — APPOINTMENT AND QUALIFICATIONS OF DIRECTORS

### Section 149. Company to have Board of Directors
(1) Every company must have a Board of Directors consisting of individuals as directors, with:
  - (a) a minimum of 3 directors for a public company, 2 for a private company, and 1 for a One Person Company; and
  - (b) a maximum of 15 directors.
  A company may appoint more than 15 directors after passing a special resolution. Prescribed classes of companies must have at least one woman director (Rules: every listed company and every other public company with paid-up share capital of ₹100 crore or more or turnover of ₹300 crore or more [verify]).
(2) Companies existing on commencement of the Act had one year to comply with (1).
(3) Every company must have at least one director who stays in India for a total of not less than 182 days during the financial year. For a newly incorporated company, the requirement applies proportionately at the end of the financial year of incorporation (2017 amendment).
(4) Every listed public company must have at least one-third of its total directors as independent directors, and the Central Government may prescribe a minimum number of independent directors for other classes of public companies (Rules: at least 2 independent directors for public companies with paid-up capital of ₹10 crore or more, turnover of ₹100 crore or more, or aggregate outstanding loans, debentures and deposits exceeding ₹50 crore [verify]). Explanation: any fraction in one-third is rounded off as one.
(5) Companies existing on commencement of the Act had one year to comply with (4).
(6) An "independent director" is a director other than a managing director, whole-time director or nominee director:
  - (a) who, in the Board's opinion, is a person of integrity with relevant expertise and experience;
  - (b) (i) who is not and was not a promoter of the company or its holding, subsidiary or associate company; and (ii) who is not related to promoters or directors of the company or its holding, subsidiary or associate company;
  - (c) who has or had no pecuniary relationship (other than remuneration as director or transactions not exceeding 10% of his total income or such amount as prescribed) with the company, its holding, subsidiary or associate company, or their promoters or directors, during the two immediately preceding financial years or the current financial year (2017 amendment);
  - (d) none of whose relatives:
    - (i) holds any security of or interest in the company, its holding, subsidiary or associate company during the two immediately preceding financial years or the current year — but a relative may hold securities or interest of face value up to ₹50 lakh or 2% of the paid-up capital of the company, its holding, subsidiary or associate company, or such higher sum as prescribed;
    - (ii) is indebted to the company, its holding, subsidiary or associate company, or their promoters or directors, beyond the prescribed amount during the two immediately preceding financial years or the current year;
    - (iii) has given a guarantee or security for the indebtedness of any third person to the company, its holding, subsidiary or associate company, or their promoters or directors of such holding company, for the prescribed amount during the two immediately preceding financial years or the current year; or
    - (iv) has any other pecuniary transaction or relationship with the company, its subsidiary, holding or associate company amounting to 2% or more of its gross turnover or total income, singly or combined with transactions in (i) to (iii) (2017 amendment);
  - (e) who, neither himself nor any of his relatives:
    - (i) holds or has held the position of key managerial personnel, or is or has been an employee, of the company or its holding, subsidiary or associate company in any of the three financial years immediately preceding the year of proposed appointment — but for a relative who is an employee, this restriction does not apply to his employment during those years (2017 amendment);
    - (ii) is or has been an employee, proprietor or partner, in any of those three preceding financial years, of — (A) a firm of auditors, company secretaries in practice or cost auditors of the company or its holding, subsidiary or associate company; or (B) any legal or consulting firm that has or had transactions with the company, its holding, subsidiary or associate company amounting to 10% or more of that firm's gross turnover;
    - (iii) holds, together with his relatives, 2% or more of the total voting power of the company; or
    - (iv) is a Chief Executive or director, by whatever name called, of any non-profit organisation that receives 25% or more of its receipts from the company, its promoters, directors, or its holding, subsidiary or associate company, or that holds 2% or more of the company's total voting power; or
  - (f) who possesses such other qualifications as may be prescribed.
(7) Every independent director must, at the first Board meeting in which he participates as director, thereafter at the first Board meeting of every financial year, and whenever there is a change in circumstances affecting his independence, declare that he meets the criteria in (6).
(8) The company and independent directors must abide by Schedule IV (Code for Independent Directors).
(9) Notwithstanding other provisions but subject to sections 197 and 198, an independent director is not entitled to any stock option, and may receive remuneration only by way of sitting fees under section 197(5), reimbursement of expenses for attending Board and other meetings, and profit-related commission approved by members. If the company has no or inadequate profits, an independent director may receive remuneration (excluding sitting fees) in accordance with Schedule V (2020 amendment).
(10) Subject to section 152, an independent director holds office for a term of up to 5 consecutive years, and is eligible for re-appointment by special resolution, with disclosure of the re-appointment in the Board's report.
(11) Notwithstanding (10), no independent director may hold office for more than two consecutive terms, but he becomes eligible for appointment again after 3 years of ceasing to be an independent director; during those 3 years he may not be appointed in or associated with the company in any other capacity, directly or indirectly. Explanation: tenure held on commencement of the Act is not counted as a term under (10) and (11).
(12) Notwithstanding anything in the Act, an independent director, and a non-executive director who is not a promoter or KMP, is liable only for acts of omission or commission by the company that occurred with his knowledge, attributable through Board processes, and with his consent or connivance, or where he did not act diligently.
(13) The provisions on retirement by rotation in section 152(6) and (7) do not apply to independent directors.

### Section 150. Manner of selection of independent directors and maintenance of databank of independent directors
(1) Subject to section 149, an independent director may be selected from a data bank of names, addresses and qualifications of persons eligible and willing to act as independent directors, maintained by a body, institute or association notified by the Central Government with expertise in creating and maintaining such data banks, and placed on its website for use by companies. The appointing company remains responsible for exercising due diligence before selecting a person from the data bank.
(2) The appointment of an independent director must be approved by the company in general meeting as provided in section 152(2), and the explanatory statement annexed to the notice must give the justification for choosing the appointee.
(3) The body maintaining the data bank must create and maintain it in accordance with the prescribed rules.
(4) The Central Government may prescribe the manner and procedure of selection of independent directors who fulfil the qualifications and requirements of section 149. (Under the Rules, individuals must register with the data bank maintained by the Indian Institute of Corporate Affairs and, unless exempt, pass an online proficiency self-assessment test [verify].)

### Section 151. Appointment of director elected by small shareholders
A listed company may have one director elected by small shareholders, in the prescribed manner and on prescribed terms and conditions. Explanation: "small shareholders" means shareholders holding shares of nominal value of not more than ₹20,000 or such other sum as may be prescribed.

### Section 152. Appointment of directors
(1) Where the articles do not provide for appointment of the first directors, the subscribers to the memorandum who are individuals are deemed to be the first directors until directors are duly appointed; for a One Person Company, the individual member is deemed its first director until the member duly appoints a director or directors.
(2) Save as otherwise expressly provided in the Act, every director is appointed by the company in general meeting.
(3) No person may be appointed a director unless he has been allotted a Director Identification Number under section 154, or any other number prescribed under section 153 (2020 amendment) [verify].
(4) Every person proposed to be appointed as director, in general meeting or otherwise, must furnish his DIN (or other prescribed number) and a declaration that he is not disqualified under the Act.
(5) A person appointed as director may not act as such unless he gives his consent to hold office and that consent is filed with the Registrar within 30 days of appointment, as prescribed. For appointment of an independent director in general meeting, the explanatory statement must include a statement that, in the Board's opinion, he fulfils the conditions specified in the Act.
(6) In a public company:
  - (a) unless the articles provide for retirement of all directors at every AGM, at least two-thirds of the total directors must — (i) be persons whose office is liable to determination by retirement by rotation; and (ii) save as otherwise expressly provided, be appointed by the company in general meeting;
  - (b) the remaining directors are also appointed in general meeting, in default of and subject to any regulations in the articles;
  - (c) at the first AGM after the general meeting at which the first directors are appointed under (a) and (b), and at every subsequent AGM, one-third of the directors liable to retire by rotation (or, if their number is not three or a multiple of three, the number nearest to one-third) must retire;
  - (d) those retiring are the directors longest in office since their last appointment; between those appointed on the same day, it is decided by lot, unless they agree otherwise among themselves;
  - (e) at the AGM where a director retires, the company may fill the vacancy by re-appointing him or appointing another person.
  Explanation: "total number of directors" excludes independent directors, whether appointed under this Act or any other law.
(7) (a) If the vacancy of the retiring director is not filled and the meeting has not expressly resolved not to fill it, the meeting stands adjourned to the same day next week at the same time and place, or, if that day is a national holiday, the next working day at the same time and place.
  (b) If at the adjourned meeting the vacancy is again not filled and there is no express resolution not to fill it, the retiring director is deemed re-appointed at the adjourned meeting, unless:
    - (i) a resolution for his re-appointment was put to that or the previous meeting and lost;
    - (ii) he has given the company or its Board written notice of his unwillingness to be re-appointed;
    - (iii) he is not qualified or is disqualified for appointment;
    - (iv) a resolution (special or ordinary) is required for his appointment or re-appointment under any provision of the Act; or
    - (v) section 162 applies.
  Explanation: "retiring director" means a director retiring by rotation.

### Section 153. Application for allotment of Director Identification Number
Every individual intending to be appointed as director of a company must apply to the Central Government for allotment of a Director Identification Number, in the prescribed form and manner, with prescribed fees. The Central Government may prescribe any other identification number to be treated as the DIN for purposes of the Act, in which case section 154 does not apply (2020 amendment) [verify].

### Section 154. Allotment of Director Identification Number
The Central Government must, within one month of receiving an application under section 153, allot a Director Identification Number to the applicant in the prescribed manner.

### Section 155. Prohibition to obtain more than one Director Identification Number
No individual who has already been allotted a Director Identification Number under section 154 may apply for, obtain or possess another Director Identification Number.

### Section 156. Director to intimate Director Identification Number
Every existing director must, within one month of receiving his Director Identification Number from the Central Government, intimate it to the company or all companies in which he is a director.

### Section 157. Company to inform Director Identification Number to Registrar
(1) Every company must, within 15 days of receiving the intimation under section 156, furnish the Director Identification Numbers of all its directors to the Registrar or other officer or authority specified by the Central Government, with prescribed fees (or additional fees within the time specified in section 403), in the prescribed form and manner.
(2) If a company fails to furnish the DIN under (1) before the expiry of the specified period, the company is liable to a penalty of ₹25,000 and every officer in default to a penalty of ₹25,000; for continuing failure, a further penalty of ₹100 per day applies, subject to a maximum of ₹1 lakh each (2020 amendment) [verify].

### Section 158. Obligation to indicate Director Identification Number
Every person or company furnishing any return, information or particulars required under the Act must mention the Director Identification Number in it where the return, information or particulars relate to a director or contain any reference to a director.

### Section 159. Punishment for contravention
If any individual or director of a company defaults in complying with section 152, 155 or 156, he is liable to a penalty of up to ₹50,000, and for a continuing default, a further penalty of up to ₹500 for each day after the first during which the default continues (2019 amendment).

### Section 160. Right of persons other than retiring directors to stand for directorship
(1) A person who is not a retiring director under section 152 is, subject to the Act, eligible for appointment as director at any general meeting if he, or a member intending to propose him, has left at the registered office, at least 14 days before the meeting, a signed written notice of his candidature or of the member's intention to propose him, together with a deposit of ₹1 lakh or such higher amount as prescribed. The deposit is refunded to the person or member if the candidate is elected or obtains more than 25% of the total valid votes cast on the resolution, on show of hands or poll (or electronically). The deposit is not required for appointment of an independent director, or a director recommended by the Nomination and Remuneration Committee constituted under section 178(1), or, where no such committee is required, a director recommended by the Board (2017 amendment).
(2) The company must inform its members of the candidature in the prescribed manner. (Under the Rules, by individual notice — electronic or physical — at least 7 days before the meeting, or by advertisement in at least two newspapers [verify].)

### Section 161. Appointment of additional director, alternate director and nominee director
(1) The articles may empower the Board to appoint any person as an additional director at any time, other than a person who failed to get appointed as director at a general meeting. The additional director holds office until the next AGM or the last date by which the AGM should have been held, whichever is earlier.
(2) If authorised by the articles or by a resolution in general meeting, the Board may appoint a person to act as alternate director for a director during his absence from India for not less than 3 months. The person must not already hold an alternate directorship for another director, or a directorship, in the same company. Provisos:
  - no person may be appointed alternate director for an independent director unless he is qualified to be appointed as an independent director;
  - the alternate director may not hold office longer than the original director could, and must vacate office when the original director returns to India;
  - if the original director's term ends before he returns, any provision for automatic re-appointment of retiring directors in default of another appointment applies to the original director and not the alternate.
(3) Subject to the articles, the Board may appoint any person as a director nominated by an institution under any law or agreement, or by the Central or a State Government by virtue of its shareholding in a Government company.
(4) In a public company, if the office of a director appointed by the company in general meeting is vacated before his term expires in the normal course, the resulting casual vacancy may, in default of and subject to the articles, be filled by the Board at a Board meeting, subject to approval by members at the immediately next general meeting (2017 amendment). The person so appointed holds office only until the date the original director would have held office had it not been vacated.

### Section 162. Appointment of directors to be voted individually
(1) At a general meeting, a motion to appoint two or more persons as directors by a single resolution may not be moved unless a proposal to move it has first been agreed to by the meeting without any vote being cast against it.
(2) A resolution moved in contravention of (1) is void, whether or not objection was taken when it was moved.
(3) A motion approving a person for appointment, or nominating a person for appointment, as director is treated as a motion for his appointment.

### Section 163. Option to adopt principle of proportional representation for appointment of directors
Notwithstanding anything in the Act, the articles may provide for the appointment of not less than two-thirds of the total number of directors according to the principle of proportional representation, whether by single transferable vote, cumulative voting or otherwise. Such appointments may be made once every 3 years, and casual vacancies among such directors are filled as provided in section 161(4).

### Section 164. Disqualifications for appointment of director
(1) A person is not eligible for appointment as director of a company if:
  - (a) he is of unsound mind and so declared by a competent court;
  - (b) he is an undischarged insolvent;
  - (c) he has applied to be adjudicated an insolvent and the application is pending;
  - (d) he has been convicted by a court of any offence, whether or not involving moral turpitude, and sentenced to imprisonment for not less than 6 months, and 5 years have not elapsed from the expiry of the sentence; if sentenced to imprisonment for 7 years or more, he is not eligible to be appointed director of any company at all;
  - (e) an order disqualifying him for appointment as director has been passed by a court or Tribunal and is in force;
  - (f) he has not paid any calls on shares of the company held by him, alone or jointly, and 6 months have elapsed from the last day fixed for payment;
  - (g) he has been convicted of an offence dealing with related party transactions under section 188 at any time during the last preceding 5 years;
  - (h) he has not complied with section 152(3) (Director Identification Number); or
  - (i) he has not complied with section 165(1) (limit on number of directorships) (2017 amendment).
(2) No person who is or has been a director of a company that:
  - (a) has not filed financial statements or annual returns for any continuous period of 3 financial years; or
  - (b) has failed to repay deposits accepted or pay interest on them, or to redeem debentures on the due date or pay interest due on them, or to pay any declared dividend, where the failure continues for one year or more,
  is eligible to be re-appointed as director of that company or appointed in any other company for 5 years from the date of the company's failure. Provisos (2017 amendment):
  - a person appointed director of a company already in default under (a) or (b) does not incur this disqualification for 6 months from his appointment;
  - the disqualifications in (1)(d), (e) and (g) do not take effect — (i) for 30 days from the date of conviction or disqualification order; (ii) where an appeal or petition is filed within those 30 days, until 7 days after its disposal; or (iii) where a further appeal or petition is filed within those 7 days, until its disposal. [verify placement of proviso]
(3) A private company may, by its articles, provide for disqualifications in addition to those in (1) and (2).

### Section 165. Number of directorships
(1) No person may hold office as director, including alternate directorship, in more than 20 companies at the same time. Of these, not more than 10 may be public companies. Explanations: for reckoning the limit of public companies, directorships of private companies that are holding or subsidiary companies of a public company are included; and for reckoning the limit of 20 companies, directorship in a dormant company is excluded (2017 amendment).
(2) Subject to (1), the members of a company may by special resolution specify a lower number of companies in which its directors may act as directors.
(3) A person holding directorships in excess of the limits in (1) immediately before commencement of the Act had to, within one year of commencement:
  - (a) choose not more than the permitted number of companies in which he wished to continue as director;
  - (b) resign as director from the remaining companies; and
  - (c) intimate his choice under (a) to each company in which he held directorship before commencement and to the Registrar having jurisdiction over each such company.
(4) A resignation under (3)(b) takes effect immediately on its despatch to the company concerned.
(5) Such a person may not act as director in more than the specified number of companies after the earlier of: (a) despatching the resignations under (3)(b); or (b) expiry of one year from commencement of the Act.
(6) A person who accepts appointment as director in contravention of (1) is liable to a penalty of ₹5,000 for each day after the first during which the contravention continues (2019 amendment).

### Section 166. Duties of directors
(1) Subject to the Act, a director must act in accordance with the company's articles.
(2) A director must act in good faith to promote the objects of the company for the benefit of its members as a whole, and in the best interests of the company, its employees, the shareholders, the community, and for the protection of the environment.
(3) A director must discharge his duties with due and reasonable care, skill and diligence, and exercise independent judgment.
(4) A director must not involve himself in a situation in which he has or may have a direct or indirect interest that conflicts, or may conflict, with the company's interest.
(5) A director must not achieve or attempt to achieve any undue gain or advantage for himself or his relatives, partners or associates; if found guilty of making an undue gain, he is liable to pay an amount equal to that gain to the company.
(6) A director may not assign his office; any such assignment is void.
(7) A director who contravenes this section is punishable with fine of not less than ₹1 lakh, extending to ₹5 lakh.

### Section 167. Vacation of office of director
(1) The office of a director becomes vacant if:
  - (a) he incurs any disqualification specified in section 164; but where the disqualification is under section 164(2), he vacates office in all companies other than the company in default (2017 amendment);
  - (b) he absents himself from all Board meetings held during a period of 12 months, with or without seeking leave of absence;
  - (c) he acts in contravention of section 184 relating to contracts or arrangements in which he is directly or indirectly interested;
  - (d) he fails to disclose his interest in any contract or arrangement in which he is directly or indirectly interested, in contravention of section 184;
  - (e) he becomes disqualified by an order of a court or the Tribunal;
  - (f) he is convicted by a court of any offence, whether or not involving moral turpitude, and sentenced to imprisonment for not less than 6 months (the original proviso requiring vacation even if an appeal is filed was omitted by the 2017 amendment) [verify];
  - (g) he is removed under the provisions of the Act; or
  - (h) having been appointed director by virtue of holding an office or employment in the holding, subsidiary or associate company, he ceases to hold that office or employment.
(2) A person who functions as director knowing that his office has become vacant on any ground in (1) is punishable with imprisonment up to 1 year, or fine of not less than ₹1 lakh extending to ₹5 lakh, or both.
(3) Where all directors vacate office under any disqualification in (1), the promoter, or in his absence the Central Government, must appoint the required number of directors, who hold office until directors are appointed by the company in general meeting.
(4) A private company may by its articles provide additional grounds for vacation of a director's office.

### Section 168. Resignation of director
(1) A director may resign by giving written notice to the company. On receipt, the Board must take note of it, and the company must intimate the Registrar in the prescribed manner, form and time, and also record the resignation in the directors' report laid at the immediately following general meeting. The director may also forward a copy of his resignation, with detailed reasons, to the Registrar within 30 days of resignation, in the prescribed manner (the 2017 amendment made this optional).
(2) The resignation takes effect from the date the company receives the notice or the date, if any, specified by the director in it, whichever is later. A director who has resigned remains liable for offences that occurred during his tenure.
(3) Where all directors resign or vacate office under section 167, the promoter, or in his absence the Central Government, must appoint the required number of directors, who hold office until directors are appointed by the company in general meeting.

### Section 169. Removal of directors
(1) A company may, by ordinary resolution, remove a director (other than one appointed by the Tribunal under section 242) before the expiry of his term, after giving him a reasonable opportunity of being heard. Provisos:
  - this does not apply where the company has opted under section 163 to appoint at least two-thirds of its directors by proportional representation;
  - an independent director re-appointed for a second term under section 149(10) may be removed only by special resolution, after giving him a reasonable opportunity of being heard.
(2) Special notice is required for a resolution to remove a director under this section, or to appoint someone in place of a removed director at the meeting at which he is removed.
(3) On receiving notice of a resolution to remove a director, the company must forthwith send a copy to the director concerned, who is entitled to be heard on the resolution at the meeting, whether or not he is a member.
(4) If the director makes written representations and requests that members be notified, the company must, if time permits:
  - (a) state in any notice of the resolution to members that representations have been made; and
  - (b) send a copy of the representations to every member to whom notice of the meeting is sent (before or after receipt of the representations).
  If the copy is not sent because of insufficient time or the company's default, the director may (without prejudice to his right to be heard orally) require that the representations be read out at the meeting. The copy need not be sent or read out if the Tribunal, on application by the company or an aggrieved person, is satisfied that the rights are being abused to secure needless publicity for defamatory matter; the Tribunal may order the company's costs to be paid wholly or partly by the director even if he is not a party.
(5) A vacancy created by removal may, if the director was appointed by the company in general meeting or by the Board, be filled by appointing another director at the same meeting, provided special notice of the intended appointment was given under (2).
(6) A director so appointed holds office until the date his predecessor would have held office had he not been removed.
(7) If the vacancy is not filled under (5), it may be filled as a casual vacancy under the Act; but the removed director may not be re-appointed as director by the Board.
(8) Nothing in this section:
  - (a) deprives a removed director of any compensation or damages payable to him for termination of his appointment as director, or of any other appointment terminating with it, under the terms of his contract or appointment; or
  - (b) derogates from any power to remove a director under other provisions of the Act.

### Section 170. Register of directors and key managerial personnel and their shareholding
(1) Every company must keep at its registered office a register containing the prescribed particulars of its directors and key managerial personnel, including details of securities held by each of them in the company or its holding, subsidiary, subsidiary of its holding company, or associate companies.
(2) A return containing the prescribed particulars and documents of the directors and KMP must be filed with the Registrar within 30 days of the appointment of every director and KMP, and within 30 days of any change.
(3) If default is made in complying with this section, the company and every officer in default are liable to a penalty of ₹50,000, plus ₹500 per day for a continuing default after the first day, subject to a maximum of ₹3 lakh for the company and ₹1 lakh for an officer in default (2020 amendment) [verify].

### Section 171. Members' right to inspect
(1) The register kept under section 170:
  - (a) must be open for inspection during business hours; members may take extracts from it, and copies must be provided to members free of cost within 30 days of request; and
  - (b) must also be kept open for inspection at every AGM and be accessible to any person attending the meeting.
(2) If inspection under (1)(a) is refused, or a requested copy is not sent within 30 days of the request, the Registrar must, on application made to him, order immediate inspection and supply of the copy. [Penalty for default falls under section 172 — verify whether a separate penalty sub-section exists.]

### Section 172. Punishment
If a company defaults in complying with any provision of this Chapter for which no specific penalty or punishment is provided, the company and every officer in default are liable to a penalty of ₹50,000, and for continuing failure a further penalty of ₹500 for each day the failure continues, subject to a maximum of ₹3 lakh for the company and ₹1 lakh for an officer in default (2020 amendment).


> Plain-language restatement, not statutory text. Section numbers, headings, defined terms, thresholds, time limits and penalties are kept as in the Act (as amended, including the Companies (Amendment) Acts of 2015, 2017, 2019 and 2020, and the IBC 2016). Items marked `[verify]` should be checked against the official text.

## CHAPTER XII — MEETINGS OF BOARD AND ITS POWERS

### Section 173. Meetings of Board
- (1) The first Board meeting must be held within **30 days** of incorporation. After that, the Board must meet at least **four times a year**, and the gap between two consecutive meetings must not exceed **120 days**.
  - Proviso: The Central Government may, by notification, exempt any class of companies from this sub-section or modify how it applies to them.
- (2) Directors may take part in a Board meeting in person or by **video conferencing or other audio-visual means**, as prescribed. These means must be able to record and identify who is taking part, and to record and store the proceedings with the date and time. The Central Government may notify matters that cannot be dealt with at a meeting held this way.
- (3) Every director must get at least **7 days' written notice** of a Board meeting, sent to the address registered with the company, by hand, by post or electronically.
  - Proviso: A meeting can be called at shorter notice to transact **urgent business**, but at least **one independent director** (if the company has one) must attend. If no independent director attends, decisions taken there must be circulated to all directors, and they become final only when **at least one independent director** ratifies them.
- (4) Every officer whose duty is to give notice under this section and who fails to do so is liable to a **penalty of ₹25,000**.
- (5) A **One Person Company, small company and dormant company** (and, by exemption notification, a private company that is a start-up `[verify]`) is treated as complying if it holds **at least one Board meeting in each half of a calendar year**, with a gap of **not less than 90 days** between the two meetings.
  - Proviso: Nothing in this sub-section or in section 174 applies to a One Person Company that has **only one director**.

### Section 174. Quorum for meetings of Board
- (1) The quorum is **one-third of the total strength** of the Board or **two directors**, whichever is higher. Directors taking part by video conferencing or other audio-visual means count towards the quorum.
- (2) The remaining directors can act even if there are vacancies. But if their number falls below the quorum fixed by the Act, they may act only to (a) bring the number of directors up to the quorum, or (b) call a general meeting. They may do nothing else.
- (3) If the **interested directors** make up **two-thirds or more** of the total strength, the remaining directors who are present and not interested form the quorum, as long as there are **at least two** of them.
- (4) If a meeting cannot be held for want of quorum, it stands adjourned to the **same day in the next week**, at the same time and place, unless the articles say otherwise. If that day is a national holiday, it moves to the next day that is not a national holiday.
- Explanation: "Total strength" leaves out directors whose places are vacant, and any fraction is rounded up to one.

### Section 175. Passing of resolution by circulation
- (1) A resolution is valid when passed by circulation if:
  - the draft, with any necessary papers, has been sent to **all directors**, or all members of the committee, at their addresses registered with the company in India, by hand, post, courier or prescribed electronic means; and
  - it is approved by a **majority** of the directors or members who are entitled to vote on it.
  - Proviso: If **at least one-third** of the total number of directors then in office ask for the resolution to be decided at a meeting, the chairperson must put it before a meeting.
- (2) A resolution passed by circulation must be noted at the next Board or committee meeting and recorded in the minutes of that meeting.

### Section 176. Defects in appointment of directors not to invalidate actions taken
- An act done by a person as a director stays valid even if it is later found that:
  - their appointment was invalid because of a defect or disqualification, or
  - their appointment had ended under a provision of the Act or the articles.
- Proviso: This protection does not cover acts done after the company has **noticed** that the appointment was invalid or had ended.

### Section 177. Audit Committee
- (1) The Board of every **listed public company**, and of every other prescribed class of company, must set up an Audit Committee. The prescribed class (Rule 6, Meetings of Board Rules) is a public company with:
  - paid-up share capital of **₹10 crore or more**; or
  - turnover of **₹100 crore or more**; or
  - aggregate outstanding loans, borrowings, debentures or deposits of **more than ₹50 crore**.
  - Unlisted public companies that are joint ventures, wholly owned subsidiaries or dormant companies are exempt `[verify]`.
- (2) The committee must have **at least three directors**, and **independent directors must be the majority**. The majority of members, including the chairperson, must be able to read and understand financial statements.
- (3) An Audit Committee that existed before the Act began had to be reconstituted under sub-section (2) within **one year** of commencement.
- (4) The committee acts under written terms of reference from the Board. These must include:
  - (i) recommending the appointment, remuneration and terms of appointment of the company's auditors;
  - (ii) reviewing and monitoring the auditor's independence and performance, and how effective the audit process is;
  - (iii) examining the financial statements and the auditors' report on them;
  - (iv) approving, or later modifying, transactions with **related parties**. The provisos to clause (iv) say:
    - The committee may give **omnibus approval** for related party transactions, subject to prescribed conditions.
    - For a related party transaction that is not covered by section 188, if the committee does not approve it, the committee must send its recommendation to the Board.
    - A transaction of **₹1 crore or less** entered into by a director or officer without the committee's approval is **voidable at the committee's option** if it is not ratified within **3 months** of the date of the transaction. If the transaction is with a related party of a director, or was authorised by another director, the director concerned must indemnify the company against any loss.
    - Clause (iv) does not apply to a transaction (other than one under section 188) between a **holding company and its wholly owned subsidiary**.
  - (v) scrutinising inter-corporate loans and investments;
  - (vi) valuing the company's undertakings or assets, where necessary;
  - (vii) evaluating internal financial controls and risk management systems;
  - (viii) monitoring the end use of money raised through public offers, and related matters.
- (5) The committee may ask the auditors for their comments on internal control systems, the scope of the audit and their observations. It may review the financial statements before they go to the Board, and may discuss related issues with the internal and statutory auditors and with management.
- (6) The committee has authority to:
  - investigate any matter covered by sub-section (4) or referred to it by the Board;
  - obtain professional advice from outside sources; and
  - have full access to the information in the company's records.
- (7) The auditors and the key managerial personnel have the **right to be heard** at committee meetings when the auditor's report is considered, but they have **no right to vote**.
- (8) The Board's report under section 134(3) must disclose:
  - the composition of the Audit Committee; and
  - any recommendation of the committee that the Board did not accept, with the reasons.
- (9) Every listed company, and every other prescribed class of company, must set up a **vigil mechanism** through which directors and employees can report genuine concerns. The prescribed classes (Rule 7) are:
  - companies that accept deposits from the public; and
  - companies that have borrowed **more than ₹50 crore** from banks and public financial institutions.
- (10) The vigil mechanism must:
  - protect anyone who uses it from victimisation;
  - give direct access to the chairperson of the Audit Committee in appropriate or exceptional cases; and
  - be described on the company's website (if any) and in the Board's report.

### Section 178. Nomination and Remuneration Committee and Stakeholders Relationship Committee
- (1) The Board of every listed company, and of every other prescribed class of company (the same classes as for the Audit Committee), must set up a **Nomination and Remuneration Committee**. It must have **three or more non-executive directors**, and **at least one-half** of them must be independent directors.
  - Proviso: The chairperson of the company, whether executive or non-executive, may be a member of this committee but may **not chair** it.
- (2) The committee must:
  - identify people who are qualified to become directors or to join senior management, using criteria it lays down;
  - recommend their appointment and removal to the Board; and
  - specify how the performance of the Board, its committees and individual directors is to be evaluated, whether by the Board, by the committee or by an independent external agency, and review how that evaluation is carried out.
- (3) The committee must set criteria for directors' qualifications, positive attributes and independence. It must recommend to the Board a policy on the remuneration of directors, key managerial personnel and other employees.
- (4) In drawing up that policy, the committee must ensure that:
  - (a) the level and make-up of remuneration is reasonable and enough to attract, keep and motivate the directors needed to run the company well;
  - (b) the link between pay and performance is clear and meets appropriate performance benchmarks; and
  - (c) pay for directors, key managerial personnel and senior management balances fixed and incentive pay, reflecting both short-term and long-term goals.
  - Proviso: The policy must be placed on the company's website (if any). Its salient features, and any changes to it, must be disclosed in the Board's report, along with the web address.
- (5) A company with **more than 1,000** shareholders, debenture-holders, deposit-holders and other security holders (taken together) at any time during a financial year must set up a **Stakeholders Relationship Committee**. It is chaired by a **non-executive director**, and its other members are decided by the Board.
- (6) The Stakeholders Relationship Committee considers and resolves the grievances of the company's security holders.
  - Proviso: If the committee does not consider a grievance in good faith, that alone is not a contravention of this section.
- (7) The chairperson of each committee under this section, or a member the chairperson authorises, must attend the company's general meetings.
- (8) If this section is contravened, the **company** is liable to a **penalty of ₹5 lakh**, and **every officer in default** to a **penalty of ₹1 lakh**.
- Explanation: "Senior management" means the company's core management team, excluding the Board. It covers everyone one level below the CEO, managing director, whole-time director or manager (including the CEO or manager if they are not on the Board). It specifically includes the functional heads and the persons acting as company secretary and chief financial officer `[verify]`.

### Section 179. Powers of Board
- (1) The Board may exercise **all the powers** that the company is authorised to exercise, subject to:
  - the Act, the memorandum and articles, and any regulations made by the company in general meeting that are consistent with them; and
  - the rule that the Board cannot exercise a power that the Act, the memorandum or the articles reserve for the company in general meeting.
- (2) A regulation made by the company in general meeting does not invalidate any earlier act of the Board that would have been valid without that regulation.
- (3) The Board may exercise the following powers **only by resolutions passed at Board meetings**:
  - (a) making calls on shareholders for money unpaid on their shares;
  - (b) authorising a buy-back of securities under section 68;
  - (c) issuing securities, including debentures, in or outside India;
  - (d) borrowing money;
  - (e) investing the company's funds;
  - (f) granting loans, or giving guarantees or providing security for loans;
  - (g) approving the financial statements and the Board's report;
  - (h) diversifying the company's business;
  - (i) approving an amalgamation, merger or reconstruction;
  - (j) taking over a company, or acquiring a controlling or substantial stake in another company;
  - (k) any other prescribed matter. Rule 8 prescribes making political contributions, appointing or removing key managerial personnel, and appointing internal auditors and the secretarial auditor `[verify]`.
  - Proviso: The Board may delegate the powers in clauses (d) to (f), by a resolution at a meeting and on the conditions it sets, to a committee of directors, the managing director, the manager or any other principal officer (or, for a branch office, a principal officer of that branch).
  - For a banking company: accepting deposits repayable on demand or otherwise, and borrowing from other banks, the RBI, the SBI or other institutions as allowed, are **not** "borrowing" for clause (d).
  - Explanation: Clause (e) and clause (f) do not apply to a banking company in the ordinary course of its business (the full text spells out how) `[verify]`.
- (4) Nothing in this section stops the company in general meeting from restricting or putting conditions on the Board's powers.

### Section 180. Restrictions on powers of Board
- (1) The Board may do the following **only with the company's consent by special resolution**:
  - (a) sell, lease or otherwise dispose of the whole or substantially the whole of an **undertaking**, or of more than one undertaking.
    - "Undertaking" means one in which the company's investment is **more than 20% of its net worth** (as per the audited balance sheet of the preceding financial year), or which produces **20% of the company's total income** in the previous financial year.
    - "Substantially the whole of the undertaking" means **20% or more of the value of the undertaking** (as per the audited balance sheet of the preceding financial year).
  - (b) invest, other than in trust securities, the compensation the company receives from a merger or amalgamation;
  - (c) borrow money when the total, including money already borrowed, would exceed the company's **paid-up share capital, free reserves and securities premium** taken together. **Temporary loans** from the company's bankers in the ordinary course of business are not counted.
    - Proviso: For a banking company, accepting deposits repayable on demand or otherwise and withdrawable by cheque, draft, order or otherwise is not "borrowing".
    - Explanation: "Temporary loans" means loans repayable on demand or within **six months** from the date of the loan. Examples are short-term cash credit, discounting of bills and loans of a seasonal character. Loans raised to finance capital expenditure are not temporary loans.
  - (d) remit, or give extra time to repay, any debt that a director owes the company.
- (2) A special resolution under clause (c) of sub-section (1) must state the **total amount** up to which the Board may borrow.
- (3) Clause (a) of sub-section (1) does not affect:
  - (a) the title of a buyer or lessee who acquires property in good faith and after due inquiry; or
  - (b) a sale or lease of property by a company whose ordinary business is selling or leasing property.
- (4) A special resolution under clause (a) of sub-section (1) may set conditions, including conditions on how the sale proceeds are used, deployed or invested. This section does not authorise a reduction of share capital except as the Act provides.
- (5) A debt that takes borrowing beyond the limit in clause (c) of sub-section (1) is **not valid or effective**, unless the lender proves that it lent in good faith and without knowing that the limit had been exceeded.
- Note: By exemption notification (2015), this section does not apply to **private companies** `[verify]`.

### Section 181. Company to contribute to bona fide and charitable funds, etc.
- The Board may contribute to bona fide charitable and other funds.
- **Prior permission** of the company in general meeting is needed if the total contributed in a financial year would exceed **5% of the company's average net profits** for the **three immediately preceding financial years**.

### Section 182. Prohibitions and restrictions regarding political contributions
- (1) A company may contribute any amount, directly or indirectly, to any political party, unless it is:
  - a **Government company**; or
  - a company that has existed for **less than three financial years**.
  - Original first proviso: Total contributions in a financial year were capped at **7.5% of the average net profits** of the three immediately preceding financial years. The Finance Act, 2017 removed this cap. In **February 2024** the Supreme Court declared that removal unconstitutional, along with the related disclosure changes, so the earlier position is treated as restored `[verify]`.
  - Proviso: A contribution can be made only under a **resolution of the Board** passed at a meeting. Subject to the section, that resolution is authority to make the contribution.
- (2) Without limiting sub-section (1), the following count as political contributions:
  - (a) a donation, subscription or payment to any person to carry on any activity that is likely to affect public support for a political party; and
  - (b) spending on advertisements in any publication (souvenir, brochure, pamphlet or the like) issued by or for the benefit of a political party.
- (3) The company must disclose in its **profit and loss account** the total amount contributed in the year.
  - The original text also required the name of each party and the amount given to it. The 2017 change that removed this was among those struck down `[verify]`.
- (3A) (Inserted 2017) Contributions may be made only by account-payee cheque, account-payee bank draft, ECS or other electronic clearing through a bank account, or (formerly) **electoral bonds**. The electoral bond scheme was struck down in 2024 `[verify]`.
- (4) If this section is contravened:
  - the company is punishable with a **fine of up to five times** the amount contributed; and
  - every officer in default is punishable with **imprisonment of up to 6 months** and a **fine of up to five times** the amount contributed.

### Section 183. Power of Board and other persons to make contributions to National Defence Fund, etc.
- (1) The Board, or any person or authority exercising the Board's powers, or the company in general meeting, may contribute any amount it thinks fit to the **National Defence Fund** or to any other fund the Central Government approves for national defence. The limits in section 181 and section 182 do not apply.
- (2) The company must disclose in its profit and loss account the total amount contributed under this section during the financial year.

### Section 184. Disclosure of interest by director
- (1) Every director must disclose, in the prescribed manner (Form MBP-1), their concern or interest in any company, body corporate, firm or other association of individuals, including their shareholding. The disclosure is made:
  - at the first Board meeting they attend as a director;
  - after that, at the **first Board meeting of every financial year**; and
  - whenever the earlier disclosure changes, at the first Board meeting held after the change.
- (2) This sub-section applies when a director is directly or indirectly concerned or interested in a contract or arrangement (made or proposed) with:
  - (a) a body corporate in which the director, alone or together with another director, holds **more than 2%** of the shareholding, or of which the director is a promoter, manager or Chief Executive Officer; or
  - (b) a firm or other entity in which the director is a partner, owner or member.
  - The director must disclose the nature of their interest at the Board meeting where the contract or arrangement is discussed, and must **not take part** in that meeting.
  - Proviso: If the director was not interested when the contract was made, they must disclose the interest promptly after becoming interested, or at the first meeting held after that.
- (3) A contract or arrangement made without the disclosure, or with the interested director taking part, is **voidable at the option of the company**.
- (4) A director who contravenes sub-section (1) or (2) is punishable with **imprisonment of up to one year**, or a **fine of not less than ₹50,000 and up to ₹1 lakh**, or both `[verify]`.
- (5) Nothing in this section:
  - (a) affects any rule of law that restricts a director from having an interest in contracts with the company; or
  - (b) applies to a contract or arrangement between two companies where the directors of one company, alone or together, hold **not more than 2%** of the paid-up share capital of the other.

### Section 185. Loan to directors, etc.
- (1) A company must not, directly or indirectly, give a loan (including a loan shown as a book debt), give a guarantee, or provide security for a loan taken by:
  - (a) any director of the company or of its holding company, or any partner or relative of such a director; or
  - (b) any firm in which such a director or relative is a partner.
- (2) A company may give a loan, guarantee or security to **any person in whom any of its directors is interested** if:
  - (a) a **special resolution** is passed in general meeting, and the explanatory statement in the notice discloses full particulars of the loan, guarantee or security and its purpose; and
  - (b) the borrowing company uses the loan for its **principal business activities**.
  - Explanation: "Any person in whom any of the directors of the company is interested" means:
    - (a) a private company of which the director is a director or member;
    - (b) a body corporate in whose general meeting the director, or two or more directors together, can exercise or control **25% or more** of the total voting power; or
    - (c) a body corporate whose Board, managing director or manager usually acts on the directions or instructions of the Board, or of any director, of the lending company.
- (3) Sub-sections (1) and (2) do not apply to:
  - (a) a loan to a managing or whole-time director:
    - (i) as part of the conditions of service that the company extends to all its employees; or
    - (ii) under a scheme approved by the members by special resolution;
  - (b) a company that gives loans, guarantees or security in the ordinary course of its business, if interest is charged at a rate **not less than the bank rate** declared by the RBI;
  - (c) a loan by a holding company to its **wholly owned subsidiary**, or a guarantee or security given by a holding company for a loan made to its wholly owned subsidiary; or
  - (d) a guarantee or security given by a holding company for a loan made by a bank or financial institution to its **subsidiary**.
  - Proviso: Under clauses (c) and (d), the subsidiary must use the loan for its principal business activities.
- (4) If this section is contravened:
  - the **company** is punishable with a **fine of ₹5 lakh to ₹25 lakh**;
  - **every officer in default** is punishable with **imprisonment of up to 6 months**, or a **fine of ₹5 lakh to ₹25 lakh**; and
  - the **director or other person** who received the loan, guarantee or security is punishable with **imprisonment of up to 6 months**, or a **fine of ₹5 lakh to ₹25 lakh**, or both.
- Note: By exemption notification, this section does not apply to a **private company** that meets all of the following `[verify]`:
  - no other body corporate has invested in its share capital;
  - its borrowings from banks, financial institutions or bodies corporate are less than **twice its paid-up share capital or ₹50 crore**, whichever is lower; and
  - it has no subsisting default in repaying those borrowings.

### Section 186. Loan and investment by company
- (1) A company may make investments through **not more than two layers of investment companies**, unless the Act provides otherwise. This does not affect:
  - (i) a company acquiring a company incorporated outside India, if that foreign company has investment subsidiaries beyond two layers as allowed by the law of its country; or
  - (ii) a subsidiary that has an investment subsidiary to meet a requirement of any law, rule or regulation.
- (2) A company must not, directly or indirectly:
  - (a) give a loan to any person or other body corporate;
  - (b) give a guarantee or provide security for a loan to any other body corporate or person; or
  - (c) acquire securities of any other body corporate, by subscription, purchase or otherwise,
  - beyond **60% of its paid-up share capital, free reserves and securities premium**, or **100% of its free reserves and securities premium**, whichever is **more**.
- (3) Going beyond the limit in sub-section (2) needs **prior approval by special resolution** in general meeting.
  - Proviso: No special resolution is needed where a holding company gives a loan, guarantee or security to its **wholly owned subsidiary** or to a **joint venture company**, or acquires securities of its wholly owned subsidiary. These must still be disclosed in the financial statements.
- (4) The company must disclose to members, in its financial statements, full particulars of the loans given, investments made and guarantees or security provided, and the purpose for which the recipient will use them.
- (5) A loan, investment, guarantee or security under this section needs:
  - a Board resolution passed at a meeting with the **consent of all directors present**; and
  - the **prior approval of the public financial institution** concerned, where a term loan from it is subsisting.
  - Proviso: The financial institution's approval is not needed if the total is within the limits in sub-section (2) and there is no default in repaying loan instalments or paying interest to that institution.
- (6) A company registered under section 12 of the SEBI Act, 1992, and covered by a prescribed class, must not take inter-corporate loans or deposits above the prescribed limit. It must give details of such loans or deposits in its financial statements.
- (7) A loan must not carry interest lower than the prevailing yield on **one-year, three-year, five-year or ten-year Government Security** closest to the tenor of the loan.
- (8) A company that is in **default in repaying deposits**, or interest on them, must not give any loan, guarantee or security, or make any acquisition under this section, until the default is made good.
- (9) The company must keep a **register** (Form MBP-2) with the prescribed particulars, entered in chronological order.
- (10) The register is kept at the registered office. It is open to inspection by members, who may take extracts, and copies are given on payment of the prescribed fee.
- (11) Except for sub-section (1), this section does not apply to:
  - (a) a loan, guarantee or security given, or investment made, in the ordinary course of business by a **banking company, insurance company or housing finance company**, or by a company set up to finance industrial enterprises or to provide infrastructural facilities;
  - (b) an investment:
    - (i) made by an **investment company**;
    - (ii) in shares allotted under section 62(1)(a), or in rights shares issued by a body corporate; or
    - (iii) made in the course of investment or lending activities by an **NBFC** registered under Chapter III-B of the RBI Act, 1934, whose principal business is acquiring securities `[verify]`.
- (12) The Central Government may make rules for the purposes of this section.
- (13) If this section is contravened:
  - the **company** is punishable with a **fine of ₹25,000 to ₹5 lakh**; and
  - **every officer in default** is punishable with **imprisonment of up to 2 years** and a **fine of ₹25,000 to ₹1 lakh** `[verify]`.
- Explanation:
  - (a) "Investment company" means a company whose principal business is acquiring shares, debentures or other securities. A company is treated as principally engaged in that business if its investments in such securities make up **not less than 50% of its total assets**, or the income from them makes up **not less than 50% of its gross income**.
  - (b) "Infrastructure facilities" means the facilities listed in Schedule VI.

### Section 187. Investments of company to be held in its own name
- (1) All investments a company makes or holds in any property, security or other asset must be held in **its own name**.
  - Proviso: A company may hold shares in its subsidiary in the name of a nominee or nominees, if that is needed to keep the number of members from falling below the statutory minimum.
- (2) Sub-section (1) does not stop a company from:
  - (a) depositing shares or securities with its bankers so that dividends or interest can be collected;
  - (b) depositing shares or securities with, transferring them to, or holding them in the name of, the State Bank of India or a scheduled bank, to make their transfer easier;
  - (c) depositing shares or securities with, or transferring them to, any person as **security** for repaying a loan or performing an obligation; or
  - (d) holding investments in the name of a **depository**, where the company is the beneficial owner.
- (3) If investments are not held in the company's own name, the company must keep a **register** with the prescribed particulars, open to inspection by members and others.
- (4) If this section is contravened:
  - the **company** is punishable with a **fine of ₹25,000 to ₹25 lakh**; and
  - **every officer in default** is punishable with **imprisonment of up to 6 months**, or a **fine of ₹25,000 to ₹1 lakh**, or both `[verify]`.

### Section 188. Related party transactions
- (1) A company may enter into a contract or arrangement with a **related party** for any of the following only with the **consent of the Board**, given by resolution at a meeting and subject to prescribed conditions:
  - (a) sale, purchase or supply of goods or materials;
  - (b) selling or otherwise disposing of, or buying, property of any kind;
  - (c) leasing of property of any kind;
  - (d) availing or rendering of any services;
  - (e) appointing any agent to buy or sell goods, materials, services or property;
  - (f) a related party's appointment to any **office or place of profit** in the company, its subsidiary or its associate company; and
  - (g) underwriting the subscription of any securities or derivatives of the company.
  - Provisos:
    - A contract above the **prescribed thresholds** needs the company's **prior approval by resolution** (an ordinary resolution since 2015) in general meeting.
    - A member who is a related party in that particular contract or arrangement must **not vote** on the resolution.
    - The voting bar does not apply where **90% or more of the members** (by number) are relatives of promoters or are related parties.
    - Nothing in this sub-section applies to transactions in the **ordinary course of business** on an **arm's length basis**.
    - The general meeting resolution is not needed for transactions between a holding company and its **wholly owned subsidiary**, where the subsidiary's accounts are consolidated with the holding company's and placed before the shareholders at the general meeting.
  - Prescribed thresholds (Rule 15, Meetings of Board Rules) `[verify]`:
    - (a) sale, purchase or supply of goods or materials, directly or through an agent: **10% or more of turnover**;
    - (b) selling or disposing of, or buying, property: **10% or more of net worth**;
    - (c) leasing of property: **10% or more of turnover**;
    - (d) availing or rendering of services: **10% or more of turnover**;
    - appointment to an office or place of profit at a monthly remuneration **above ₹2.5 lakh**;
    - underwriting remuneration **above 1% of net worth**.
    - Turnover and net worth are taken from the audited financial statements of the preceding financial year.
  - Explanation:
    - (a) "Office or place of profit" is an office or place where:
      - a director receives remuneration beyond what they are entitled to as a director, whether as salary, fee, commission, perquisites, rent-free accommodation or otherwise; or
      - an individual (other than a director), a firm, a private company or another body corporate receives remuneration, whether as salary, fee, commission, perquisites, rent-free accommodation or otherwise.
    - (b) "Arm's length transaction" means a transaction between two related parties carried out as if they were unrelated, so that there is no conflict of interest.
- (2) Every such contract or arrangement must be referred to in the **Board's report** to shareholders, with the justification for entering into it.
- (3) If a director or employee enters into a contract or arrangement without the Board's consent or the general meeting resolution, and it is **not ratified** by the Board or the shareholders at a meeting within **three months** of the date it was entered into, it is **voidable at the option of the Board or the shareholders**. If the contract is with a related party of a director, or was authorised by another director, the directors concerned must **indemnify the company** against any loss.
- (4) Whatever else applies, the company may take action against a director or employee who entered into the contract or arrangement in breach of this section, to recover any loss it suffers.
- (5) A director or other employee who enters into, or authorises, a contract or arrangement in breach of this section is punishable as follows:
  - (i) in a **listed company**: **imprisonment of up to one year**, or a **fine of ₹25,000 to ₹5 lakh**, or both;
  - (ii) in any **other company**: a **fine of ₹25,000 to ₹5 lakh** `[verify]`.
- Note: The definition of "related party" is in section 2(76).

### Section 189. Register of contracts or arrangements in which directors are interested
- (1) Every company must keep one or more registers (Form MBP-4) giving particulars of all contracts or arrangements covered by section 184(2) or section 188. After entries are made, the register is placed before the **next Board meeting** and signed by all the directors present.
- (2) Every director and key managerial personnel must disclose to the company, within **30 days** of appointment or of leaving office, the particulars of their concern or interest in other associations that the register must record, and any other prescribed information about themselves.
- (3) The register is kept at the registered office. It is open for inspection during business hours, extracts may be taken, and copies must be given to members on payment of the prescribed fee.
- (4) The register must also be produced at the start of every **annual general meeting** and kept open and accessible during the meeting to anyone entitled to attend.
- (5) Sub-section (1) does not apply to a contract or arrangement:
  - (a) for the sale, purchase or supply of goods, materials or services, if their value (or the cost of the services) does not exceed **₹5 lakh in total in any year**; or
  - (b) made by a **banking company** for collecting bills in the ordinary course of business.
- (6) Every director who fails to comply with this section and its rules is liable to a **penalty of ₹25,000**.

### Section 190. Contract of employment with managing or whole-time directors
- (1) Every company must have either:
  - (a) a **written contract of service** with a managing or whole-time director; or
  - (b) if the contract is not in writing, a **written memorandum** setting out its terms.
- (2) The contracts or memoranda are kept at the registered office and are open to inspection by any member **without charge**.
- (3) This section does not apply to a **private company**.
- (4) If this section is contravened, the **company** is liable to a **penalty of ₹25,000**, and **every officer in default** to a **penalty of ₹5,000 for each default** `[verify]`.

### Section 191. Payment to director for loss of office, etc., in connection with transfer of undertaking, property or shares
- (1) A director may not receive any payment as compensation for loss of office, or as consideration for retirement, in connection with:
  - (a) the transfer of the whole or any part of the company's undertaking or property; or
  - (b) the transfer of all or any shares of the company where the transfer results from:
    - (i) an offer made to the general body of shareholders;
    - (ii) an offer by or for another body corporate, aimed at making the company its subsidiary, or a subsidiary of its holding company;
    - (iii) an offer by an individual aimed at obtaining the right to exercise, or control the exercise of, **at least one-third** of the total voting power; or
    - (iv) any other offer that depends on being accepted to a given extent,
  - unless the particulars of the proposed payment, including the amount, are **disclosed to the members** and the proposal is **approved by the company in general meeting** `[verify]`.
- (2) A payment made in breach of sub-section (1) is held by the director **in trust**:
  - for the company, in a transfer of undertaking or property; or
  - for the shareholders who sold their shares as a result of the offer, in a share transfer.
  - The director must bear any expenses of distributing that amount `[verify]`.
- (3) If a director receives an unreasonably high price for shares, or other valuable consideration not available to other shareholders, the excess is treated as compensation for loss of office for the purposes of this section `[verify]`.
- (4) This section does not cover payments made in good faith as **damages for breach of contract**, or as **pension** for past services `[verify]`.
- (5) A director who defaults in complying with this section is punishable with a **fine of ₹1 lakh to ₹5 lakh** `[verify]`.

### Section 192. Restriction on non-cash transactions involving directors
- (1) A company must not enter into an arrangement under which:
  - (a) a director of the company, or of its holding, subsidiary or associate company, or a person connected with that director, acquires (or is to acquire) assets from the company for consideration other than cash; or
  - (b) the company acquires (or is to acquire) assets for consideration other than cash from such a director or connected person,
  - unless the arrangement has **prior approval by a resolution** of the company in general meeting. If the director or connected person is a director of the **holding company**, approval by a resolution in the holding company's general meeting is also needed.
- (2) The notice for the general meeting must include particulars of the arrangement and the value of the assets, calculated by a **registered valuer**.
- (3) An arrangement made in breach of this section, and any related transaction, is **voidable at the instance of the company**, unless:
  - (a) the money or other consideration cannot be restored and the company has been indemnified by another person for any loss or damage it suffered; or
  - (b) another person who acquired rights in good faith, for value and without notice of the breach would be affected.

### Section 193. Contract by One Person Company
- (1) If a One Person Company limited by shares or by guarantee enters into a contract with its **sole member who is also its director**, and the contract is not in writing, the company must ensure that its terms are:
  - set out in a memorandum; or
  - recorded in the minutes of the first Board meeting held after the contract is entered into.
  - Proviso: This sub-section does not apply to contracts entered into in the ordinary course of business.
- (2) The company must inform the **Registrar** of every such contract recorded in the minutes within **15 days** of the date the Board approves it.

### Section 194. Prohibition on forward dealings in securities of company by director or key managerial personnel
- `[Omitted]` by the Companies (Amendment) Act, 2017 (with effect from **7 May 2018** `[verify]`).
- Before it was omitted, the section barred directors and key managerial personnel from buying "call" or "put" options in the securities of the company, its holding company, its subsidiary or its associate company. Such forward dealing is now governed by the SEBI framework.

### Section 195. Prohibition on insider trading of securities
- `[Omitted]` by the Companies (Amendment) Act, 2017 (with effect from **7 May 2018** `[verify]`).
- Before it was omitted, the section barred insider trading in a company's securities by any person, including directors and key managerial personnel. Insider trading is now governed by the SEBI (Prohibition of Insider Trading) Regulations.

## CHAPTER XIII — APPOINTMENT AND REMUNERATION OF MANAGERIAL PERSONNEL

### Section 196. Appointment of managing director, whole-time director or manager
- (1) A company may not appoint or employ a **managing director and a manager** at the same time.
- (2) A managing director, whole-time director or manager may be appointed or reappointed for **not more than five years at a time**. A reappointment cannot be made earlier than **one year before the current term expires**.
- (3) A company may not appoint, or keep employing, as managing director, whole-time director or manager, any person who:
  - (a) is **under 21 years** of age, or has **attained 70 years** of age;
    - Proviso: A person who has attained 70 may be appointed by **special resolution**. The explanatory statement in the notice must give the justification for appointing them.
    - Proviso (2017): If the special resolution is not passed but the votes in favour exceed the votes against, the person may still be appointed if the **Central Government**, on the Board's application, is satisfied that the appointment is most beneficial to the company.
  - (b) is an **undischarged insolvent**, or has at any time been adjudged insolvent;
  - (c) has at any time suspended payments to creditors, or made a composition with them; or
  - (d) has at any time been **convicted** by a court of an offence and sentenced to **more than six months**.
- (4) Subject to section 197 and Schedule V:
  - the appointment, terms and conditions, and remuneration are approved by the **Board at a meeting**;
  - this is then subject to approval by **resolution at the next general meeting**; and
  - **Central Government approval** is also needed if the appointment does not meet the conditions in Schedule V.
  - Proviso: The notice of the Board meeting and of the general meeting must include the terms and conditions of the appointment, the remuneration payable, and the interest of any director in the appointment.
  - Proviso: A return in the prescribed form (MR-1) must be filed with the **Registrar within 60 days** of the appointment.
- (5) Subject to sub-section (3), if the general meeting does not approve an appointment, acts done by the appointee before that decision remain valid.

### Section 197. Overall maximum managerial remuneration and managerial remuneration in case of absence or inadequacy of profits
- (1) A **public company** may not pay its directors (including the managing director and whole-time directors) and its manager, taken together, total managerial remuneration of more than **11% of its net profits** for the financial year. Net profits are worked out under section 198, without deducting directors' remuneration from gross profits.
  - Proviso (as amended in 2017): The company in general meeting may authorise payment above 11%, subject to Schedule V. Central Government approval is no longer needed.
  - Proviso: Unless the company approves otherwise by **special resolution** in general meeting:
    - (i) any **one** managing director, whole-time director or manager may not be paid more than **5% of net profits**. Where there is more than one such director, all of them and the manager together may not be paid more than **10%**;
    - (ii) directors who are neither managing nor whole-time directors may not be paid more than:
      - (A) **1% of net profits**, if there is a managing director, whole-time director or manager; or
      - (B) **3% of net profits** in any other case.
  - Proviso: If the company has **defaulted** in paying dues to a bank, a public financial institution, non-convertible debenture holders or any other secured creditor, it must get **their prior approval** before seeking approval in general meeting.
- (2) The percentages above do not include the sitting fees payable under sub-section (5).
- (3) If the company has **no profits or inadequate profits** in a financial year, it may pay remuneration only in line with **Schedule V**.
- (4) Directors' remuneration, including that of managing directors, whole-time directors and the manager, is fixed under this section by:
  - the articles; or
  - a resolution; or
  - a special resolution, if the articles require one.
  - Remuneration for services rendered in any other capacity is included, unless:
    - (a) the services are of a **professional nature**; and
    - (b) in the opinion of the Nomination and Remuneration Committee (if the company has one) or the Board, the director has the qualifications needed to practise that profession.
- (5) A director may be paid a **sitting fee** for attending Board or committee meetings, or for any other purpose the Board decides. The fee must not exceed the prescribed amount, which is **₹1 lakh per meeting** under Rule 4. The fee for independent directors and women directors must **not be less than** the fee paid to other directors.
- (6) A director or manager may be paid by **monthly payment**, by a **specified percentage of net profits**, or partly one way and partly the other.
- (7) An **independent director** is **not entitled to stock options**. They may receive:
  - the sitting fee under sub-section (5);
  - reimbursement of expenses for attending Board and other meetings; and
  - **profit-related commission** approved by the members.
