- (8) Net profits for this section are calculated under section 198.
- (9) If a director receives remuneration above the permitted limit, or without a sanction that was required, they must **refund** it to the company within **two years**, or any shorter period the company allows. Until refunded, they hold the amount **in trust** for the company.
- (10) The company may not waive recovery of a refundable amount unless it approves the waiver by **special resolution** within **two years** from the date the amount became refundable.
  - Proviso: If the company has defaulted in paying dues to a bank, public financial institution, non-convertible debenture holders or other secured creditor, it must get their prior approval before the special resolution.
- (11) Where Schedule V applies because there are no or inadequate profits, a provision that increases a director's remuneration (whether in the memorandum, articles, an agreement, a resolution or otherwise) has no effect unless the increase is in line with **Schedule V**.
- (12) Every **listed company** must disclose in the Board's report:
  - the **ratio** of each director's remuneration to the **median employee's** remuneration; and
  - other prescribed details.
- (13) If the company takes out insurance for its managing director, whole-time director, manager, CEO, CFO or company secretary against liability for negligence, default, misfeasance, breach of duty or breach of trust, the premium is **not treated as remuneration**.
  - Proviso: If the person is proved guilty, the premium is treated as part of their remuneration.
- (14) A managing or whole-time director who receives commission from the company may also receive remuneration or commission from its **holding or subsidiary company**, if this is **disclosed in the Board's report**.
- (15) If this section is contravened, every person in default is liable to a **penalty of ₹1 lakh**, and the **company** to a **penalty of ₹5 lakh**.
- (16) The **auditor**, in their report under section 143, must state:
  - whether the company paid its directors' remuneration in line with this section;
  - whether any director was paid more than the limit laid down; and
  - any other prescribed details.

### Section 198. Calculation of profits
- (1) Net profits for section 197 are worked out as follows:
  - (a) credit is given for the sums in sub-section (2), and not for the sums in sub-section (3); and
  - (b) the sums in sub-section (4) are deducted, and the sums in sub-section (5) are not.
- (2) Credit is given for **bounties and subsidies** received from any Government or public authority, unless the Central Government directs otherwise.
- (3) No credit is given for:
  - (a) premiums on shares or debentures issued or sold by the company;
  - (b) profits on selling forfeited shares;
  - (c) profits of a **capital nature**, including profits from selling the undertaking or any part of it;
  - (d) profits from selling immovable property or fixed assets of a capital nature, unless the company's business includes buying and selling such property or assets;
    - Proviso: If a fixed asset is sold for more than its written-down value, credit is given for the excess, but only up to the difference between its original cost and its written-down value;
  - (e) any change in the carrying amount of an asset or liability that is recognised in equity reserves (including the surplus in the profit and loss account) on measuring it at **fair value**.
- (4) The following are deducted:
  - (a) all usual working charges;
  - (b) directors' remuneration;
  - (c) bonus or commission paid or payable to staff, or to any engineer, technician or other person employed or engaged by the company, whole-time or part-time;
  - (d) any tax the Central Government has notified as a tax on excess or abnormal profits;
  - (e) any tax on business profits imposed for special reasons or in special circumstances and notified by the Central Government;
  - (f) interest on debentures issued by the company;
  - (g) interest on mortgages, and on loans and advances secured by a charge on fixed or floating assets;
  - (h) interest on unsecured loans and advances;
  - (i) expenses on repairs to immovable or movable property, as long as the repairs are not of a capital nature;
  - (j) outgoings, including contributions made under section 181;
  - (k) depreciation, to the extent specified in section 123;
  - (l) any excess of expenditure over income from an earlier year (calculated under this section), to the extent not already deducted in a later year;
  - (m) compensation or damages payable because of a legal liability, including liability for breach of contract;
  - (n) any sum paid as insurance against the risk of the liabilities in clause (m); and
  - (o) debts considered bad and written off or adjusted during the year.
- (5) The following are **not** deducted:
  - (a) income tax and super tax payable under the Income-tax Act, 1961, or any other tax on the company's income, other than the taxes in clauses (d) and (e) of sub-section (4);
  - (b) compensation, damages or payments made **voluntarily**, that is, not because of a liability covered by clause (m) of sub-section (4);
  - (c) losses of a **capital nature**, including a loss on selling the undertaking or any part of it. This does not include the excess of written-down value over sale proceeds or scrap value of an asset; and
  - (d) any change in the carrying amount of an asset or liability that is recognised in equity reserves (including the surplus in the profit and loss account) on measuring it at fair value.

### Section 199. Recovery of remuneration in certain cases
- This applies where a company has to **restate its financial statements** because of **fraud** or **non-compliance** with any requirement of the Act or its rules.
- The company must recover the excess from any past or present managing director, whole-time director, manager or Chief Executive Officer (by whatever name called).
- The excess is the remuneration (including stock options) they received for the restated period that is more than they would have been paid on the restated figures.
- This is in addition to any other liability under the Act or any other law.

### Section 200. Central Government or company to fix limit with regard to remuneration
- Despite anything else in this Chapter, the **Central Government or the company**, when sanctioning an appointment or remuneration under this Chapter, may fix the remuneration within the Act's limits, as an amount or as a percentage of profits, as it thinks fit.
- In fixing the remuneration, it must consider:
  - (a) the company's financial position;
  - (b) remuneration or commission the person draws in any other capacity;
  - (c) remuneration or commission the person draws from any other company;
  - (d) the person's professional qualifications and experience; and
  - (e) any other prescribed matters.

### Section 201. Forms of, and procedure in relation to, certain applications
- (1) Every application to the Central Government under this Chapter must be made in the **prescribed form**.
- (2) Before applying, the company must give its members a **general notice** of the nature of the application. The notice must be published at least once:
  - in a newspaper in the principal **vernacular language** of the district where the registered office is; and
  - in an **English** newspaper circulating in that district.
  - Copies of the notices, with the company's certificate that they were duly published, must be attached to the application.

### Section 202. Compensation for loss of office of managing or whole-time director or manager
- (1) A company may pay a **managing director, whole-time director or manager**, but **no other director**, compensation for loss of office, or consideration for retirement from office or in connection with loss of office or retirement.
- (2) No payment may be made where:
  - (a) the director resigns because the company is reconstructed or amalgamated, and is appointed managing director, whole-time director, manager or other officer of the reconstructed or resulting company;
  - (b) the director resigns for any other reason;
  - (c) the director's office is vacated under section 167(1);
  - (d) the company is wound up, by Tribunal order or voluntarily, and the winding up was caused by the director's **negligence or default**;
  - (e) the director has been guilty of **fraud, breach of trust, gross negligence or gross mismanagement** in running the company, its subsidiary or its holding company; or
  - (f) the director instigated, or took part (directly or indirectly) in, ending their own office.
- (3) The payment may not exceed what the director would have earned for the **unexpired term** or for **three years**, whichever is **shorter**. It is calculated on the **average remuneration** actually earned in the **three years** before leaving office, or over the shorter period served if they held office for less than three years.
  - Proviso: No payment may be made if the company begins winding up, before the director leaves office or within **12 months** after, and its assets (after deducting the costs of winding up) are not enough to repay shareholders the share capital they contributed, including any premium.
- (4) This section does not stop the company paying a managing director, whole-time director or manager for services rendered in any other capacity.

### Section 203. Appointment of key managerial personnel
- (1) Every company of a prescribed class must have the following **whole-time key managerial personnel**:
  - (i) a managing director, or Chief Executive Officer, or manager, and in their absence a whole-time director;
  - (ii) a company secretary; and
  - (iii) a Chief Financial Officer.
  - The prescribed class (Rule 8) is every **listed company** and every other **public company** with paid-up share capital of **₹10 crore or more**.
  - Under Rule 8A, any other company with paid-up share capital of **₹5 crore or more** must have a **whole-time company secretary**.
  - Proviso: An individual may not be appointed or reappointed as both **chairperson** and **managing director or CEO** at the same time, unless:
    - the articles provide otherwise; or
    - the company does not carry on multiple businesses.
  - Proviso: The previous proviso does not apply to notified classes of companies that carry on multiple businesses and have appointed one or more CEOs for each business.
- (2) Every whole-time key managerial personnel must be appointed by a **Board resolution** that sets out the terms and conditions, including remuneration.
- (3) A whole-time key managerial personnel may not hold office in **more than one company** at the same time, except in a **subsidiary** of the company.
  - Proviso: They may be a **director** of any company, with the **permission of the Board**.
  - Proviso: A person holding such office in more than one company when the Act began had to choose one company within **six months**.
  - Proviso: A company may appoint as managing director a person who is managing director or manager of **one, and not more than one**, other company. The appointment must be made or approved by a Board resolution with the **consent of all directors present**, and specific notice of the meeting and resolution must have been given to all directors then in India.
- (4) A vacancy in the office of any whole-time key managerial personnel must be filled by the Board at a meeting within **six months** from the date of the vacancy.
- (5) If this section is contravened:
  - the **company** is liable to a **penalty of ₹5 lakh**; and
  - **every director and key managerial personnel in default** is liable to a **penalty of ₹50,000**, plus **₹1,000 for each day** the default continues after the first day, up to a maximum of **₹5 lakh**.
- Explanation: "Whole-time director" includes a director in the whole-time employment of the company.

### Section 204. Secretarial audit for bigger companies
- (1) Every **listed company**, and every other company of a prescribed class, must attach to its Board's report a **secretarial audit report** in the prescribed form (MR-3), given by a **company secretary in practice**. The prescribed classes (Rule 9) are:
  - every public company with paid-up share capital of **₹50 crore or more**;
  - every public company with turnover of **₹250 crore or more**; and
  - every company with outstanding loans or borrowings from banks or public financial institutions of **₹100 crore or more**.
- (2) The Board must explain fully, in its report, any **qualification, observation or other remark** made by the company secretary in practice.
- (3) The company must give the company secretary in practice **all the assistance and facilities** needed to audit its secretarial and related records.
- (4) If this section is contravened, the **company**, **every officer in default** and the **company secretary in practice** are each liable to a **penalty of ₹2 lakh**.

### Section 205. Functions of company secretary
- (1) The functions of the company secretary include:
  - (a) reporting to the Board on the company's compliance with the Act, its rules and other laws that apply to the company;
  - (b) ensuring the company complies with the applicable **secretarial standards**; and
  - (c) any other prescribed duties. Rule 10 includes:
    - guiding directors on their duties;
    - helping to convene Board, committee and general meetings, attending them and keeping their minutes;
    - obtaining approvals from the Board, the general meeting, the Government and other authorities;
    - representing the company before regulators;
    - helping the Board conduct the company's affairs;
    - advising the Board on good corporate governance and compliance; and
    - any other duties assigned by the Board.
  - Explanation: "Secretarial standards" means the secretarial standards issued by the Institute of Company Secretaries of India and approved by the Central Government under section 118(10).
- (2) Sub-section (1) does not affect the duties and functions that the Act or any other law gives to the Board, the chairperson, the managing director or a whole-time director.

## CHAPTER XIV — INSPECTION, INQUIRY AND INVESTIGATION

### Section 206. Power to call for information, inspect books and conduct inquiries
- (1) If, on going through a document a company has filed or information received, the **Registrar** thinks further information, explanation or documents are needed, they may serve a **written notice** on the company. The notice asks the company to provide them within the reasonable time it specifies.
- (2) On receiving the notice, the company and all its officers must provide the information or explanation, to the best of their knowledge and power, and produce the documents within the time allowed.
- (3) The Registrar may serve a further written notice requiring the company to produce, at a stated place and time, such further books of account, books, papers and explanations as the Registrar needs, if:
  - nothing is provided within the time allowed; or
  - the Registrar finds what was provided **inadequate**, or concludes that an **unsatisfactory state of affairs** exists, or that the company has not disclosed a full and fair statement of information.
- (4) The Registrar may act under this sub-section if satisfied, from information available or a **representation** made by any person, that:
  - the company's business is being carried on for a **fraudulent or unlawful purpose**; or
  - the company is not complying with the Act; or
  - investors' grievances are not being addressed.
  - The Registrar must first inform the company of the allegations and give it an opportunity to be heard. They may then call for information or explanation by written order and carry out any **inquiry** they think fit, after hearing the company.
  - Proviso: If satisfied that the circumstances warrant it, the Central Government may direct the Registrar, or an inspector it appoints, to carry out the inquiry.
  - Proviso: If the business has been carried on for a fraudulent or unlawful purpose, every officer in default is punishable for **fraud** under section 447.
- (5) The Central Government may, if satisfied that the circumstances warrant it, direct an **inspection** of the company's books and papers by an inspector it appoints.
- (6) If a company fails to provide the information or explanation, or to produce the documents, required under this section:
  - the company and every officer in default are punishable with a fine of up to **₹1 lakh**; and
  - for a continuing failure, an additional fine of **₹500 for every day** after the first day `[verify]`.
- (7) The Central Government may, by general or special order, authorise any **statutory authority** to inspect the books of account of a company or class of companies `[verify]`.

### Section 207. Conduct of inspection and inquiry
- (1) When the Registrar or an inspector calls for books of account and other books and papers under section 206, every director, officer and other employee must:
  - produce all those documents;
  - give the statements, information or explanations asked for, in the form required; and
  - give all assistance with the inspection.
- (2) During the inspection or inquiry, the Registrar or inspector may make copies of books and papers, or have them made, or put **identification marks** on them as evidence of the inspection.
- (3) Despite any other law or any contract, the Registrar or inspector has the same powers as a **civil court** trying a suit under the Code of Civil Procedure, 1908, for:
  - (a) discovery and production of books of account and other documents, at the place and time they specify;
  - (b) summoning people, enforcing their attendance and examining them **on oath**; and
  - (c) inspecting any books, registers and other documents of the company, at any place.
- (4)
  - (i) A director or officer who disobeys a direction of the Registrar or inspector under this section is punishable with **imprisonment of up to one year** and a **fine of ₹25,000 to ₹1 lakh**.
  - (ii) A director or officer convicted of an offence under this section is treated as having **vacated office** from the date of conviction, and is **disqualified** from holding office in any company.

### Section 208. Report on inspection made
- After an inspection or inquiry under section 206 and section 207, the Registrar or inspector must send a **written report** to the Central Government, with any relevant documents.
- The report may recommend that a **further investigation** into the company's affairs is needed, giving reasons.

### Section 209. Search and seizure
- (1) The Registrar or an inspector may act under this section if they have **reasonable grounds to believe** that books and papers of, or relating to, any of the following may be **destroyed, mutilated, altered, falsified or secreted**:
  - a company;
  - its key managerial personnel, directors, auditor or company secretary in practice, if the company has not appointed a secretary.
  - After obtaining an order from the **Special Court** for seizure `[verify]`, the Registrar or inspector may:
    - (a) enter the places where the books and papers are kept, with any help needed, and **search** them; and
    - (b) **seize** the books and papers they think necessary, after letting the company take copies or extracts at its own cost.
- (2) The seized books and papers must be returned to the person they were taken from as soon as possible, and in any case **no later than the 180th day** after seizure.
  - Proviso: Before returning them, the Registrar or inspector may take copies or extracts, put identification marks on them, or deal with them in any other necessary way.
- (3) Except as this section provides, searches and seizures follow the **Code of Criminal Procedure, 1973**. From 1 July 2024 references to it are read as the Bharatiya Nagarik Suraksha Sanhita, 2023 `[verify]`.

### Section 210. Investigation into affairs of company
- (1) The **Central Government** may order an **investigation** into a company's affairs if it thinks it necessary:
  - (a) on receiving the Registrar's or inspector's **report** under section 208;
  - (b) on being told that the company has passed a **special resolution** that its affairs ought to be investigated; or
  - (c) in the **public interest**.
- (2) If a **court or the Tribunal** orders, in any proceedings before it, that a company's affairs ought to be investigated, the Central Government **must** order an investigation.
- (3) For this section, the Central Government may appoint **one or more persons as inspectors** to investigate and report on the company's affairs as it directs.

### Section 211. Establishment of Serious Fraud Investigation Office
- (1) The Central Government must set up, by notification, an office called the **Serious Fraud Investigation Office (SFIO)** to investigate frauds relating to companies.
  - Proviso: Until the SFIO was set up under this section, the office set up under the Government of India resolution of **2 July 2003** was treated as the SFIO.
- (2) The SFIO is headed by a **Director**, who must be an officer not below the rank of **Joint Secretary** to the Government of India and have knowledge and experience of corporate affairs. It also has experts in:
  - (i) banking;
  - (ii) corporate affairs;
  - (iii) taxation;
  - (iv) forensic audit;
  - (v) capital market;
  - (vi) information technology;
  - (vii) law; and
  - (viii) any other prescribed field.
- (3) The Central Government appoints the **Director**, the **experts** (from people of ability, integrity and experience) and the other officers and employees it thinks necessary.
- (4) The terms and conditions of service of the Director, the experts and the other officers and employees are prescribed.

### Section 212. Investigation into affairs of company by Serious Fraud Investigation Office
- (1) Without limiting section 210, the Central Government may, by order, assign an investigation into a company's affairs to the **SFIO** if it thinks it necessary:
  - (a) on receiving the Registrar's or inspector's report under section 208;
  - (b) on being told that the company has passed a **special resolution** that its affairs need investigation;
  - (c) in the **public interest**; or
  - (d) on a request from any **department** of the Central Government or a State Government.
  - The SFIO Director may appoint as many inspectors as needed.
- (2) Once a case is assigned to the SFIO, **no other investigating agency** of the Central or any State Government may investigate offences under the Act in that case. If one has already started, it must stop and hand over the relevant documents and records to the SFIO.
- (3) The SFIO must investigate in the manner and by the procedure in this Chapter, and report to the Central Government **within the period specified** in the order.
- (4) The SFIO Director must have the company's affairs investigated by an **Investigating Officer**, who has the powers of an inspector under section 217.
- (5) The company, and its present and former officers and employees, must give the Investigating Officer all the information, explanation, documents and assistance they ask for.
- (6) Despite the Code of Criminal Procedure, 1973, offences under **section 447** (fraud) are **cognizable**. A person accused of such an offence may not be released on bail or on their own bond unless:
  - (i) the **Public Prosecutor** has had the chance to oppose the bail application; and
  - (ii) where the Public Prosecutor opposes it, the court is satisfied that there are **reasonable grounds** to believe the person is **not guilty** and is **not likely to commit any offence** while on bail.
  - Proviso: A person **under 16 years**, a **woman**, or someone who is **sick or infirm** may be released on bail if the Special Court so directs.
  - Proviso: The Special Court may take cognizance only on a **written complaint** by:
    - (i) the SFIO Director; or
    - (ii) a Central Government officer authorised by general or special written order.
- (7) These limits on bail are in addition to the limits under the Code of Criminal Procedure or any other law.
- (8) An authorised Director, Additional Director or Assistant Director of the SFIO may **arrest** a person if, on the material in their possession, they have **reason to believe** (recorded in writing) that the person is guilty of an offence under sub-section (6). The person must be told the **grounds of arrest** as soon as possible.
- (9) Immediately after the arrest, the officer must send a copy of the arrest order and the material relied on to the SFIO, in a **sealed envelope** and in the prescribed manner. The SFIO must keep them for the prescribed period.
- (10) Every arrested person must be taken within **24 hours** to the Judicial Magistrate or Metropolitan Magistrate with jurisdiction. The time needed for the journey is excluded.
- (11) If the Central Government directs, the SFIO must submit an **interim report**.
- (12) When the investigation is complete, the SFIO must submit its **investigation report** to the Central Government.
- (13) Despite anything in the Act or any other law, any person concerned may get a **copy of the investigation report** by applying to the court.
- (14) After examining the report, and taking any legal advice it thinks fit, the Central Government may direct the SFIO to **start prosecution** against the company, its present or former officers or employees, or any other person directly or indirectly connected with its affairs.
- (14A) If the report says that **fraud** has taken place and a director, key managerial personnel or other officer, or any other person or entity, has taken **undue advantage or benefit** (as an asset, property, cash or otherwise), the Central Government may apply to the Tribunal for:
  - **disgorgement** of that asset, property or cash; and
  - holding that person **personally liable without any limitation of liability**.
- (15) Despite anything in the Act or any other law, an investigation report filed with the Special Court for framing charges is treated as a **police report** under section 173 of the Code of Criminal Procedure.
- (16) Any investigation or other action that the SFIO started under the Companies Act, 1956 continues under that Act, as if this Act had not been passed.
- (17) Information sharing:
  - (a) While the SFIO is investigating an offence under the Act, any other investigating agency, State Government, police authority or income-tax authority that has information or documents about that offence must give them to the SFIO.
  - (b) The SFIO must share information or documents it holds with those agencies and authorities where they are relevant to an offence or matter they are investigating or examining under any other law.

### Section 213. Investigation into company's affairs in other cases
- The **Tribunal** may order that a company's affairs ought to be investigated by inspectors appointed by the Central Government, after giving the parties concerned a reasonable opportunity to be heard. It may do so:
  - (a) on an application by:
    - (i) at least **100 members**, or members holding at least **one-tenth of the total voting power**, in a company with share capital; or
    - (ii) at least **one-fifth of the persons** on the register of members, in a company without share capital,
    - supported by enough evidence to show that the applicants have good reasons for seeking the investigation; or
  - (b) on an application by any other person, or on its own, if satisfied that there are circumstances suggesting that:
    - (i) the company's business is being carried on to **defraud** its creditors, members or any other person, or for a fraudulent or unlawful purpose, or in a manner **oppressive** to any of its members, or that the company was formed for a fraudulent or unlawful purpose;
    - (ii) the people involved in forming or managing the company have been guilty of **fraud, misfeasance or other misconduct** towards the company or its members; or
    - (iii) the members have not been given all the information about the company's affairs they could reasonably expect, including information on how commission payable to a managing or other director or the manager is calculated.
- Once the Tribunal makes the order, the Central Government **must appoint one or more inspectors** to investigate and report.
- Proviso: If the investigation proves that the business was carried on with intent to defraud or for a fraudulent or unlawful purpose, or that the company was formed for such a purpose, or that any person concerned was guilty of fraud, then every officer in default and every person concerned is punishable for **fraud** under section 447.

### Section 214. Security for payment of costs and expenses of investigation
- Before appointing an inspector under clause (a) of section 213 (on members' application), the Central Government may require the applicants to give **security of up to ₹25,000**, as it specifies, towards the costs and expenses of the investigation.
- The security is **refunded** to the applicants if the investigation leads to a **prosecution**.

### Section 215. Firm, body corporate or association not to be appointed as inspector
- A **firm, body corporate or other association** may not be appointed as an inspector under the Act.

### Section 216. Investigation of ownership of company
- (1) If the Central Government sees a reason to, it may appoint one or more inspectors to investigate and report on matters relating to the company and its membership. The aim is to find out the **true persons** who:
  - (a) are or have been financially interested in the company's success or failure, whether really or apparently;
  - (b) are or have been able to control, or materially influence, the company's policy; or
  - (c) have or had a **beneficial interest** in the company's shares, or who are its **beneficial owners or significant beneficial owners**.
- (2) Without limiting sub-section (1), the Central Government **must** appoint inspectors if the **Tribunal**, in any proceeding before it, orders that the company's affairs ought to be investigated as to its membership and other matters, for the purposes in sub-section (1).
- (3) When appointing an inspector, the Central Government may define the **scope** of the investigation, by subject matter, by period or otherwise. In particular, it may limit the investigation to particular shares or debentures.
- (4) Subject to the terms of appointment, the inspector may investigate any circumstances suggesting an **arrangement or understanding** that is relevant to the investigation, even if it is not legally binding, as long as it is or was observed in practice or is likely to be.

### Section 217. Procedure, powers, etc., of inspectors
- (1) The following people must help an inspector:
  - the present and former officers, employees and agents of the company under investigation; and
  - where another body corporate or person is being investigated under section 219, its present and former officers, employees and agents.
  - Each of them must:
    - (a) **preserve and produce** to the inspector, or to a person the inspector authorises, all books and papers of or relating to the company (or the other body corporate or person) that are in their custody or power; and
    - (b) give the inspector all the help with the investigation that they reasonably can.
- (2) The inspector may require **any other body corporate** to give information, or to produce books and papers before the inspector or an authorised person, where this is relevant or necessary to the investigation.
- (3) The inspector may not keep books and papers produced under this section for **more than 180 days**. After that they must be returned to the company, body corporate, firm or individual who produced them.
  - Proviso: The inspector may call for them again, by written order, for **another 180 days**.
- (4) The inspector may **examine on oath**, about the company's (or other body corporate's) affairs:
  - (a) any person referred to in sub-section (1); and
  - (b) with the **prior approval of the Central Government**, any other person.
  - For this, the inspector may require any such person to appear in person.
- (5) Despite any other law or any contract, the inspector has the same powers as a **civil court** trying a suit under the Code of Civil Procedure, 1908, for:
  - (a) discovery and production of books of account and other documents, at the place and time the inspector specifies;
  - (b) summoning people, enforcing their attendance and examining them on oath; and
  - (c) inspecting the company's books, registers and other documents at any place.
- (6)
  - (i) A director or officer who disobeys a direction of the inspector under this section is punishable with **imprisonment of up to one year** and a **fine of ₹25,000 to ₹1 lakh**.
  - (ii) A director or officer convicted under this section is treated as having **vacated office** from the date of conviction, and is **disqualified** from holding office in any company.
- (7) Notes of an examination under sub-section (4) are taken down in writing, read over to or by the person examined, and signed by them. They may then be **used in evidence against** that person.
- (8) A person is punishable with **imprisonment of up to 6 months** and a **fine of ₹25,000 to ₹1 lakh**, plus a further fine of up to **₹2,000 for every day** after the first day the failure or refusal continues, if they fail without reasonable cause, or refuse:
  - (a) to produce a book or paper that sub-section (1) or (2) requires them to produce;
  - (b) to give information that sub-section (2) requires them to give;
  - (c) to appear before the inspector in person when required under sub-section (4), or to answer the inspector's questions under that sub-section; or
  - (d) to sign the notes of examination under sub-section (7).
- (9) Officers of the Central Government, State Governments, police and statutory authorities must give the inspector any assistance the inspector requires, with the **prior approval of the Central Government**, for the inspection, inquiry or investigation.
- (10) The Central Government may make **reciprocal arrangements** with the government of a foreign State to obtain evidence. It may then, by notification, apply the provisions on such evidence to that State `[verify]`.
- (11) Despite anything in the Act or the Code of Criminal Procedure, the inspector may apply to a competent court if evidence may be available in a country or place outside India. The court may issue a **letter of request** to a competent court or authority there, asking it to:
  - examine orally any person who is likely to know the facts, and record their statement; and
  - require that person, or any other person, to produce documents,
  - and to send all the evidence back to the court that issued the letter.
- (12) Every statement recorded and document received under sub-section (11) is treated as evidence collected during the investigation.
- (13) A competent court or authority in a country outside India may send a letter of request about an investigation into a company's affairs under the laws of that country. On receiving it, the Central Government may, if it thinks fit, pass it to an inspector it appoints. The inspector then:
  - summons the person and records their statement, or has the documents produced; and
  - sends all the evidence to the Central Government to be passed on to the requesting court or authority.

### Section 218. Protection of employees during investigation
- (1) This section applies despite anything in any other law. It covers the period during:
  - an investigation under section 210, 212, 213 or 219; or
  - proceedings under Chapter XVI against any person concerned in the conduct and management of the company's affairs.
  - During that period, if the company proposes to:
    - (a) **discharge or suspend** an employee;
    - (b) **punish** an employee, whether by dismissal, removal, reduction in rank or otherwise; or
    - (c) change an employee's **terms of employment** to their disadvantage,
  - it must first get the **Tribunal's approval** for the proposed action `[verify]`.
- (2) If the Tribunal objects to the proposed action, it must send the company a **written notice** of its objection by post.
- (3) If the company receives no notice of objection within **30 days** of applying, it may go ahead with the proposed action.
- (4) If the company is dissatisfied with the objection, it may appeal to the **Appellate Tribunal** in the prescribed manner within **30 days** of receiving the notice.
- (5) The Appellate Tribunal's decision on the appeal is **final and binding** on the Tribunal and the company.
- (6) If the company contravenes this section:
  - the **company** is punishable with a **fine of ₹1 lakh to ₹25 lakh**; and
  - **every officer in default** is punishable with **imprisonment of up to 6 months**, or a **fine of ₹25,000 to ₹1 lakh**, or both `[verify]`.

### Section 219. Power of inspector to conduct investigation into affairs of related companies, etc.
- This applies to an inspector appointed under section 210, 212 or 213 to investigate a company's affairs.
- If the inspector thinks it necessary for that investigation, they may, with the **prior approval of the Central Government**, also investigate and report on the affairs of:
  - (a) any other body corporate that is, or at any relevant time was, the company's **subsidiary or holding company**, or a subsidiary of its holding company;
  - (b) any other body corporate that is, or at any relevant time was, managed by a person who is or was, at the relevant time, the company's **managing director or manager**;
  - (c) any other body corporate whose Board is made up of the company's **nominees**, or usually acts on the directions or instructions of the company or any of its directors; or
  - (d) any person who is or was, at any relevant time, the company's **managing director, manager or employee**.
- The inspector does this only to the extent the results are relevant to the investigation of the company.

### Section 220. Seizure of documents by inspector
- (1) During an investigation, if the inspector has **reasonable grounds to believe** that books and papers of or relating to the company, another body corporate, or its managing director or manager, may be **destroyed, mutilated, altered, falsified or secreted**, the inspector may:
  - (a) enter the places where they are kept, with any help needed, and search them in the required manner; and
  - (b) **seize** the books and papers they think necessary for the investigation, after letting the company take copies or extracts at its own cost.
- (2) The inspector may keep the seized books and papers for as long as needed, but **no later than the conclusion of the investigation**. They must then be returned to the company, body corporate, managing director or manager they were taken from.
  - Proviso: Before returning them, the inspector may take copies or extracts, put identification marks on them, or deal with them in any other necessary way.
- (3) Except as this section provides, searches and seizures follow the provisions of the **Code of Criminal Procedure, 1973** on searches and seizures.

### Section 221. Freezing of assets of company on inquiry and investigation
- (1) The **Tribunal** may act under this section when a matter comes before it:
  - on a reference by the **Central Government**;
  - in connection with an inquiry or investigation under this Chapter; or
  - on a complaint by members in the number specified in section 244(1), by a **creditor** owed **one lakh rupees or more**, or by any other person who has a reasonable ground to believe the condition below is met.
  - If it appears to the Tribunal that the company's funds, assets or properties are likely to be **removed, transferred or disposed of** in a way that harms the company, its shareholders or creditors, or the public interest, it may order that the removal, transfer or disposal:
    - must not take place for up to **three years**, as specified in the order; or
    - may take place only on the conditions and restrictions the Tribunal thinks fit.
- (2) If the Tribunal's order is contravened:
  - the **company** is punishable with a **fine of ₹1 lakh to ₹25 lakh**; and
  - **every officer in default** is punishable with **imprisonment of up to 6 months**, or a **fine of ₹25,000 to ₹1 lakh**, or both.

### Section 222. Imposition of restrictions upon securities
- (1) The Tribunal may make an order under this section in connection with an investigation under section 216, or on a complaint by any person, if:
  - there is good reason to find out the relevant facts about any securities issued or to be issued by a company; and
  - the Tribunal believes those facts cannot be found out unless restrictions are imposed.
  - The Tribunal may then order that the securities be subject to the **restrictions** it thinks fit, for up to **three years**, as specified in the order.
- (2) If the order is contravened:
  - the **company** is punishable with a **fine of ₹1 lakh to ₹25 lakh**; and
  - **every officer in default** is punishable with **imprisonment of up to 6 months**, or a **fine of ₹25,000 to ₹1 lakh**, or both.

### Section 223. Inspector's report
- (1) An inspector appointed under this Chapter **may**, and must if the Central Government so directs, make **interim reports** to the Central Government. At the end of the investigation, the inspector must make a **final report**. Each report is in writing, or printed, as the Central Government directs.
- (2) A report by an inspector appointed under section 213 (on the Tribunal's order) must also be **filed with the Tribunal** `[verify]`.
- (3) The Central Government may give copies of the report as follows `[verify]`:
  - (a) to the company at its registered office, and to any other body corporate, managing director or manager the report deals with;
  - (b) on request and on payment of the prescribed fee, to any member of the company or of another body corporate the report deals with, and to any person whose interests as a creditor appear to be affected; and
  - (c) to the applicants for the investigation.
- (4) The Central Government may have the report **printed and published** `[verify]`.
- (5) A copy of the report, authenticated as prescribed, is **admissible in any legal proceeding** as evidence of the **inspector's opinion** on any matter it contains `[verify]`.

### Section 224. Actions to be taken in pursuance of inspector's report
- (1) If an inspector's report under section 223 states that an officer of a company, or any other person, has committed an **offence**, the Central Government may **prosecute** that person. All officers, employees and agents of the company (or other body corporate) must give the Government all the help they reasonably can with the prosecution.
- (2) If the report shows that it would be right to do so, the Central Government may, unless the company is already being wound up, have the following filed with the Tribunal `[verify]`:
  - a petition to **wind up** the company on the ground that this is **just and equitable**;
  - an application under section 241; or
  - both.
- (3) The Central Government may itself bring proceedings in the name of the company (or other body corporate) if the report shows that, in the public interest, proceedings should be brought for:
  - (a) recovery of **damages** for fraud, misfeasance or other misconduct in promoting, forming or managing it; or
  - (b) recovery of its **property** that has been misapplied or wrongfully kept.
- (4) The company (or body corporate) must **indemnify** the Central Government for the costs and expenses of proceedings brought under sub-section (3).
- (5) If the report states that **fraud** has taken place and that a director, key managerial personnel or other officer, or any other person or entity, has taken **undue advantage or benefit** (as an asset, property, cash or otherwise), the Central Government may apply to the Tribunal for:
  - **disgorgement** of that asset, property or cash; and
  - holding that person **personally liable without any limitation of liability**.
- (6) Where the Tribunal passes an order under sub-section (5), the director, key managerial personnel or other officer, or the other person or entity, is liable for action under section 447 `[verify]`.

### Section 225. Expenses of investigation
- (1) The **Central Government** pays the expenses of, and incidental to, an investigation in the first instance. The following must then reimburse it to the extent stated:
  - (a) a person **convicted** in a prosecution brought under this Chapter, or ordered to pay damages or restore property in proceedings under section 224, to the extent the court specifies in those proceedings;
  - (b) a company or body corporate in whose name proceedings were brought, up to the amount or value of the money or property it recovered;
  - (c) unless the investigation leads to a prosecution:
    - (i) any company, body corporate, managing director or manager dealt with in the report, wholly or to the extent the Central Government directs. This does not apply where the inspector was appointed **on the Government's own initiative** `[verify]`; and
    - (ii) the **applicants** for the investigation, where the inspector was appointed under section 213, to the extent the Central Government directs.
- (2) The amount a company or body corporate must pay under clause (b) of sub-section (1) is a **first charge** on the money or property it recovered.
- (3) An amount that any person or body corporate must reimburse may be recovered from them as an **arrear of land revenue**.
- (4) For this section, expenses of an investigation include the reasonable costs and expenses of the inspector and of any proceedings, as directed `[verify]`.

### Section 226. Voluntary winding up of company, etc., not to stop investigation proceedings
- An investigation may not be started, continued, stopped or suspended `[verify]` only because:
  - (a) an application has been made under section 241;
  - (b) the company has passed a **special resolution** for **voluntary winding up**; or
  - (c) other proceedings to wind up the company are pending before the Tribunal.
- Proviso: If the Tribunal passes a **winding-up order**, the inspector must tell the Tribunal that the investigation is pending. The Tribunal may then pass any orders it thinks fit.

### Section 227. Legal advisers and bankers not to disclose certain information
- Nothing in this Chapter requires anyone to disclose to the Tribunal, the Central Government, the Registrar or an inspector:
  - (a) any **privileged communication** made to a **legal adviser** in that capacity, except the client's name and address; or
  - (b) any information held by a company's (or other body corporate's or person's) **bankers**, in that capacity, about the affairs of **any other customer**.

### Section 228. Investigation, etc., of foreign companies
- The provisions of this Chapter apply, **with the necessary changes**, to the inspection, inquiry or investigation of **foreign companies**.

### Section 229. Penalty for furnishing false statement, mutilation, destruction of documents
- This section covers:
  - a person who must give an explanation or make a statement during an inspection, inquiry or investigation; and
  - an officer or other employee of a company or body corporate that is also under investigation.
- Such a person is punishable for **fraud** under section 447 if they:
  - (a) destroy, mutilate, falsify, conceal, tamper with or remove without authority any document relating to the property, assets or affairs of the company or body corporate, or are a party to doing so;
  - (b) make, or are a party to making, a **false entry** in any document about the company or body corporate; or
  - (c) give an explanation that is **false**, or that they know to be false.

## CHAPTER XV — COMPROMISES, ARRANGEMENTS AND AMALGAMATIONS

### Section 230. Power to compromise or make arrangements with creditors and members
- (1) Where a compromise or arrangement is proposed between a company and:
  - (a) its creditors or any class of them; or
  - (b) its members or any class of them,
  - the **Tribunal** may order a **meeting** of those creditors or members (or class) to be called, held and conducted as it directs. The application may be made by:
    - the company;
    - any creditor or member; or
    - if the company is being wound up, the **liquidator** appointed under this Act or under the Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code, 2016.
  - Explanation: "Arrangement" includes reorganising the company's share capital by **consolidating** shares of different classes, by **dividing** shares into different classes, or both.
- (2) The applicant must disclose to the Tribunal, **by affidavit**:
  - (a) all **material facts** about the company, such as:
    - its latest financial position;
    - its latest auditor's report on the accounts; and
    - any investigation or proceedings pending against it;
  - (b) any **reduction of share capital** included in the scheme;
  - (c) any scheme of **corporate debt restructuring** agreed to by at least **75% of the secured creditors in value**, including:
    - (i) a creditors' responsibility statement in the prescribed form;
    - (ii) safeguards for the other secured and unsecured creditors;
    - (iii) the auditor's report that, after the restructuring, the company's fund requirements will meet the **liquidity test**, based on the Board's estimates;
    - (iv) where the company proposes to follow the RBI's corporate debt restructuring guidelines, a statement to that effect; and
    - (v) a **valuation report** by a **registered valuer** on the company's shares, property and all its assets, tangible and intangible, movable and immovable.
- (3) Where a meeting is called under the Tribunal's order:
  - **Notice** must be sent individually to all the creditors or class of creditors, all the members or class of members, and the debenture-holders, at their registered addresses.
  - The notice must come with a statement that discloses:
    - the details of the compromise or arrangement;
    - a copy of the valuation report, if any;
    - its effect on creditors, key managerial personnel, promoters, non-promoter members and debenture-holders;
    - its effect on any material interests of the directors or the debenture trustees; and
    - any other prescribed matters.
  - The notice must also be placed on the company's website (if any).
  - For a **listed company**, it must be sent to **SEBI** and the **stock exchanges** for posting on their websites.
  - It must also be **published in newspapers** in the prescribed manner.
  - Proviso: If notice is also given by advertisement, it must state the time within which copies of the scheme will be available **free of charge** at the registered office.
- (4) The notice must allow those receiving it to vote on the scheme in person, by proxy or by **postal ballot** within **one month** of receiving the notice.
  - Proviso: An **objection** to the scheme may be made only by persons holding **at least 10% of the shareholding**, or owed **at least 5% of the total outstanding debt** as per the latest audited financial statement.
- (5) The notice and all accompanying documents must also be sent, in the prescribed form, to:
  - the Central Government;
  - the income-tax authorities;
  - the Reserve Bank of India;
  - SEBI;
  - the Registrar;
  - the stock exchanges;
  - the Official Liquidator;
  - the Competition Commission of India, where necessary; and
  - any other sectoral regulator or authority likely to be affected.
  - They must make any representations within **30 days** of receiving the notice. If they do not, they are presumed to have none.
- (6) If, at the meeting, a **majority of persons representing three-fourths in value** of the creditors or class of creditors, or of the members or class of members, agree to the scheme (voting in person, by proxy or by postal ballot), and the **Tribunal sanctions** it by order, the scheme **binds**:
  - the company;
  - all the creditors or class of creditors;
  - all the members or class of members; and
  - where the company is being wound up, its liquidator and contributories.
- (7) The Tribunal's order under sub-section (6) must provide for all or any of the following:
  - (a) where **preference shares** are to be converted into equity shares, the preference shareholders get the choice of taking their **arrears of dividend in cash**, or accepting **equity shares** equal in value to the dividend due;
  - (b) protection of any class of creditors;
  - (c) where the scheme varies shareholders' rights, giving effect to it under section 48;
  - (d) if creditors agree to the scheme under sub-section (6), **abatement** of any proceedings pending before the Board for Industrial and Financial Reconstruction under section 15 of the Sick Industrial Companies (Special Provisions) Act, 1985; and
  - (e) any other matters the Tribunal thinks necessary to implement the scheme effectively, including an **exit offer to dissenting shareholders**.
  - Proviso: No scheme may be sanctioned unless the company's **auditor certifies** to the Tribunal that the accounting treatment proposed in the scheme conforms to the **accounting standards** under section 133.
- (8) The company must file the Tribunal's order with the **Registrar within 30 days** of receiving it.
- (9) The Tribunal may **dispense with** a meeting of creditors or a class of creditors if creditors (or the class) holding **at least 90% in value** agree to the scheme and confirm this by **affidavit**.
- (10) The Tribunal may not sanction a scheme that involves a **buy-back** of securities unless the buy-back complies with **section 68**.
- (11) A scheme may include a **takeover offer** made in the prescribed manner.
  - Proviso: For a **listed company**, the takeover offer must follow the **SEBI regulations**.
  - Under Rule 26A (inserted in 2020), members of an **unlisted company** holding **75% or more** of its shares may apply for a takeover offer to acquire the remaining shares `[verify]`.
- (12) An aggrieved party may apply to the Tribunal, in the prescribed manner, about any grievance relating to a takeover offer for a company **other than a listed company**. The Tribunal may pass any order it thinks fit.
- Explanation: Section 66 (reduction of share capital) does not apply to a reduction of share capital carried out under a Tribunal order under this section.

### Section 231. Power of Tribunal to enforce compromise or arrangement
- (1) When the Tribunal sanctions a scheme under section 230, it:
  - (a) has power to **supervise** how the scheme is implemented; and
  - (b) may, when making the order or at any time after, give **directions** or make **modifications** to the scheme that it thinks necessary for proper implementation.
- (2) If the Tribunal is satisfied that the scheme cannot be implemented satisfactorily, with or without modifications, and that the company cannot pay its debts as the scheme provides, it may order the company to be **wound up**. Such an order is treated as made under section 273 `[verify]`.
- (3) This section also applies, so far as possible, to schemes sanctioned before this section came into force.

### Section 232. Merger and amalgamation of companies
- (1) This sub-section applies where an application is made to the Tribunal under section 230 to sanction a compromise or arrangement, and it is shown that:
  - (a) the scheme is for, or connected with, the **reconstruction** of a company or companies by **merger or amalgamation** of two or more companies; and
  - (b) under the scheme, the whole or part of the undertaking, property or liabilities of one company (the **transferor company**) is to be transferred to another company (the **transferee company**), or divided among and transferred to two or more companies.
  - The Tribunal may then order **meetings** of the creditors or class of creditors, or of the members or class of members, to be called, held and conducted as it directs. Sub-sections (3) to (6) of section 230 apply with the necessary changes.
- (2) After such an order, the merging companies, or the companies to be divided, must also circulate for the meeting:
  - (a) the **draft terms of the scheme**, drawn up and adopted by the directors of the merging company;
  - (b) confirmation that a copy of the draft scheme has been **filed with the Registrar**;
  - (c) a report adopted by the directors explaining the scheme's effect on each class of shareholders, key managerial personnel, promoters and non-promoter shareholders. It must set out the **share exchange ratio** and any special valuation difficulties;
  - (d) the **expert's valuation report**, if any; and
  - (e) a **supplementary accounting statement**, if the last annual accounts of any merging company relate to a financial year ending **more than six months** before the first meeting called to approve the scheme.
- (3) Once satisfied that the procedure in sub-sections (1) and (2) has been followed, the Tribunal may **sanction** the scheme. In that order, or a later one, it may provide for:
  - (a) **transfer** to the transferee company of the whole or part of the transferor company's undertaking, property or liabilities, from a date the parties fix, unless the Tribunal, for recorded reasons, fixes another date;
  - (b) **allotment** or appropriation by the transferee company of shares, debentures, policies or similar instruments to any person under the scheme;
    - Proviso: The transferee company may not, as a result of the scheme, hold any shares in **its own name** or in the name of any **trust**, whether for itself or for any subsidiary or associate company. Any such shares must be **cancelled or extinguished**;
  - (c) **continuation** of legal proceedings by or against the transferor company that are pending on the transfer date, by or against the transferee company;
  - (d) **dissolution** of the transferor company **without winding up**;
  - (e) provision for persons who **dissent** from the scheme within the time and in the manner the Tribunal directs;
  - (f) where share capital is held by **non-resident shareholders** under the foreign direct investment norms or any other law, how the transferee company's shares are allotted to them;
  - (g) **transfer of employees** of the transferor company to the transferee company;
  - (h) where the transferor company is **listed** and the transferee company is **unlisted**:
    - (A) the transferee company stays unlisted until it becomes listed; and
    - (B) shareholders of the transferor company who choose to **opt out** of the transferee company are paid the value of their shares, and other benefits, using a pre-determined price formula or a valuation. The Tribunal may make arrangements for this;
    - Proviso: The payment or valuation for any share may not be less than what SEBI regulations specify;
  - (i) where the transferor company is dissolved, **setting off** the fee it paid on its authorised capital against the fee the transferee company must pay on its authorised capital after the amalgamation; and
  - (j) any incidental, consequential and supplemental matters needed to carry out the merger or amalgamation fully and effectively.
  - Proviso: No scheme may be sanctioned unless the company's **auditor certifies** to the Tribunal that the proposed accounting treatment conforms to the accounting standards under section 133.
- (4) Where the order provides for transferring property or liabilities, the order itself transfers the property to, and vests the liabilities in, the transferee company. If the order so directs, the property is freed from any charge that ceases to have effect because of the scheme.
- (5) Every company covered by the order must file a **certified copy** of it with the **Registrar** for registration within **30 days** of receiving it.
- (6) The scheme must clearly state an **appointed date** from which it takes effect. It is treated as effective from that date and not from any later date.
- (7) Until the scheme is completed, every company covered by the order must file an **annual statement** with the Registrar, in the prescribed form and time, certified by a **chartered accountant, cost accountant or company secretary in practice**. It must say whether the scheme is being complied with in line with the Tribunal's orders.
- (8) If a company contravenes this section:
  - the **company** is punishable with a **fine of ₹1 lakh to ₹25 lakh**; and
  - **every officer in default** is punishable with **imprisonment of up to one year**, or a **fine of ₹1 lakh to ₹3 lakh**, or both `[verify]`.
- Explanation: For this section:
  - (i) **merger by absorption** means one or more existing companies merging into another existing company;
  - (ii) **merger by formation of a new company** means two or more companies merging into a new company;
  - (iii) "merging companies" means the transferor and transferee companies in a merger by absorption, and the transferor companies in a merger by formation of a new company;
  - (iv) "property" includes assets, rights and interests of every kind, and "liabilities" includes debts and obligations of every kind.

### Section 233. Merger or amalgamation of certain companies
- (1) Despite section 230 and section 232, a scheme of merger or amalgamation may be entered into under this section (the **fast-track route**) between:
  - **two or more small companies**;
  - a **holding company and its wholly owned subsidiary**; or
  - any other prescribed class of companies. Rule 25 includes:
    - two or more **start-up** companies;
    - one or more start-ups with one or more small companies; and
    - certain holding–subsidiary combinations of unlisted companies, and a foreign holding company with its Indian wholly owned subsidiary (2024 amendment) `[verify]`.
  - The following conditions apply:
  - (a) the transferor and transferee companies issue **notice** of the proposed scheme inviting objections or suggestions within **30 days** from:
    - the Registrar and the Official Liquidator of the place where each company's registered office is; and
    - persons affected by the scheme;
  - (b) each company considers the objections and suggestions at its **general meeting**, and the scheme is approved by members (or a class of members) holding **at least 90% of the total number of shares**;
  - (c) each company files a **declaration of solvency** in the prescribed form with the Registrar of the place where its registered office is; and
  - (d) the scheme is approved by a **majority representing nine-tenths in value** of the creditors or class of creditors. This is at a meeting called on **21 days' notice**, with the scheme sent to the creditors, or by their written approval.
- (2) The **transferee company** must file a copy of the approved scheme, in the prescribed manner, with:
  - the **Central Government** (Regional Director);
  - the **Registrar**; and
  - the **Official Liquidator** of the place where its registered office is.
- (3) If the Registrar and the Official Liquidator have no objections or suggestions, the Central Government must **register** the scheme and send a **confirmation** to the companies.
- (4) If the Registrar or the Official Liquidator has objections or suggestions, they may send them in writing to the Central Government within **30 days**.
  - Proviso: If nothing is sent within that time, they are presumed to have **no objection**.
- (5) If, after receiving objections or suggestions or for any other reason, the Central Government thinks the scheme is **not in the public interest** or in the interest of creditors, it may apply to the **Tribunal** within **60 days** of receiving the scheme under sub-section (2). It must state its objections and ask the Tribunal to consider the scheme under section 232.
- (6) On receiving such an application, the Tribunal may, for recorded reasons:
  - direct that the scheme be considered under the **section 232 procedure**; or
  - **confirm** the scheme by any order it thinks fit.
  - Proviso: If the Central Government neither objects nor applies to the Tribunal, it is treated as having **no objection** and the scheme is to be confirmed. Under amended Rule 25 (2023), the Regional Director must issue a confirmation order within fixed timelines, failing which the scheme is **deemed approved** `[verify]`.
- (7) A copy of the confirmation order under sub-section (6) must be sent to the Registrar with jurisdiction over the transferee company, and to the persons concerned. The Registrar must **register** the scheme, issue a confirmation to the companies, and send it to the Registrars where the transferor companies are located.
- (8) Registration of the scheme under sub-section (3) or (7) **dissolves the transferor company** without winding up.
- (9) Registration of the scheme has these effects:
  - (a) the transferor company's property and liabilities are transferred to the transferee company and become its property and liabilities;
  - (b) any charges on the transferor company's property apply to, and can be enforced as, charges on the transferee company's property;
  - (c) legal proceedings by or against the transferor company that are pending before any court continue by or against the transferee company; and
  - (d) where the scheme provides for buying the shares of **dissenting shareholders** or settling debts owed to **dissenting creditors**, any unpaid amount becomes the transferee company's **liability**.
- (10) On the merger or amalgamation, the transferee company may not hold any shares in **its own name** or in the name of any **trust**, whether for itself or for any subsidiary or associate company. All such shares are **cancelled or extinguished**.
- (11) The transferee company must apply to the Registrar with the registered scheme, showing its **revised authorised capital**, and pay the prescribed fees on it.
  - Proviso: Any fee the transferor company paid on its authorised capital before the merger is **set off** against the fee the transferee company must pay on its authorised capital as increased by the merger.
- (12) This section applies, with the necessary changes, to the companies in sub-section (1) for:
  - a compromise or arrangement under section 230; and
  - a division or transfer under section 232(1)(b).
- (13) The Central Government may provide for the merger or amalgamation of companies in the prescribed manner.
- (14) A company covered by this section may instead use **section 232** to have a merger or amalgamation scheme approved.

### Section 234. Merger or amalgamation of company with foreign company
- (1) Unless otherwise provided, the provisions of this Chapter apply, with the necessary changes, to mergers and amalgamations between companies registered under this Act and companies incorporated in the **jurisdictions of notified countries**.
  - Proviso: The Central Government may make rules on such mergers and amalgamations in **consultation with the Reserve Bank of India**.
- (2) Subject to any other law, a **foreign company** may merge into a company registered under this Act, or the other way round, with the **prior approval of the RBI**.
  - The scheme may provide for paying the merging company's shareholders in **cash**, in **Depository Receipts**, or partly in each.
- Explanation: For this section, "foreign company" means any company or body corporate incorporated outside India, whether or not it has a place of business in India.
- Note: In force from 13 April 2017, together with Rule 25A and the FEMA (Cross Border Merger) Regulations, 2018 `[verify]`.

### Section 235. Power to acquire shares of shareholders dissenting from scheme or contract approved by majority
- (1) This applies to a scheme or contract involving the transfer of shares, or any class of shares, in a company (the **transferor company**) to another company (the **transferee company**).
  - If the holders of **at least nine-tenths in value** of the shares to be transferred approve it within **four months** of the offer, the transferee company may give notice, in the prescribed manner, to any **dissenting shareholder** that it wants to acquire their shares.
  - Shares already held, at the date of the offer, by the transferee company, its nominee or its subsidiaries are left out of this count.
  - The notice may be given at any time within **two months** after the end of those four months.
- (2) Once notice is given, the transferee company is **entitled and bound** to acquire those shares on the same terms as the approving shareholders. This does not apply if, on an application by the dissenting shareholder to the Tribunal within **one month** of the notice, the Tribunal orders otherwise.
- (3) If the Tribunal has not ordered otherwise, then after **one month** from the notice, or after any pending application by the dissenting shareholder has been decided, the transferee company must:
  - send a copy of the notice to the transferor company, with an **instrument of transfer** executed for the shareholder by a person the transferee company appoints, and by the transferee company for itself; and
  - pay or transfer to the transferor company the price or other consideration for the shares.
  - The transferor company must then **register** the transferee company as the holder of those shares.
  - Proviso: No instrument of transfer is needed for any share for which a **share warrant** is outstanding.
- (4) Any money the transferor company receives under this section must be paid into a **separate bank account**. That money, and any other consideration received, is held **in trust** for the persons entitled to the shares and must be **paid out to them within 60 days**.
- Explanation: "Dissenting shareholder" includes a shareholder who has not agreed to the scheme or contract, and one who has failed or refused to transfer their shares to the transferee company under it.

### Section 236. Purchase of minority shareholding
- (1) An acquirer, or a person acting in concert with them, must notify the company of their intention to **buy the remaining equity shares** if they become the registered holder of **90% or more of the issued equity share capital**, whether through:
  - amalgamation;
  - share exchange;
  - conversion of securities; or
  - any other reason.
  - The same applies to any person or group of persons who become a **90% majority** or hold 90% of the issued equity share capital.
- (2) The acquirer, person or group must **offer to buy** the minority shareholders' equity shares at a price determined from a valuation by a **registered valuer**, in line with the prescribed rules.
- (3) Without limiting sub-sections (1) and (2), the **minority shareholders** may themselves offer their equity shares to the majority shareholders, at a price determined under the rules prescribed under sub-section (2).
- (4) The majority shareholders must **deposit** an amount equal to the value of the shares to be acquired in a **separate bank account**, operated by the transferor company, for **at least one year**. The money must be **paid out to entitled shareholders within 60 days**.
  - Proviso: Payments must continue for **one year** to entitled shareholders who were not paid within the 60 days for any reason, or who did not receive or claim the payment made within that time.
- (5) In a merger or amalgamation, the transferor company acts as the **transfer agent**: it receives and pays the price, and takes delivery of shares and delivers them to the majority `[verify]`.
- (6) If shareholders do not physically deliver their shares within the time the company specifies:
  - their share certificates are treated as **cancelled**;
  - the transferor company may issue shares in place of the cancelled ones and complete the transfer under the law; and
  - it pays the price out of the majority's deposit under sub-section (4), by dispatching the payment to the minority.
- (7) This applies where the majority requires a full purchase and deposits the price with the company for shareholders who have **died or ceased to exist**, or whose heirs, successors, administrators or assignees have not been brought on record by transmission. Those shareholders' right to remain shareholders is **extinguished**. The majority's only duty is to pay the price once the heirs or successors are brought on record.
- (8) If minority shareholders holding **75% or more** of the minority equity shareholding agree a **higher price** for transferring their shares, the majority must offer and **share that higher price** with the remaining minority shareholders on the same terms `[verify]`.
- (9) If the majority fails to complete a full purchase of the minority's shares, this section continues to apply to the **remaining minority** shareholders, even if:
  - (a) the company's shares held by them have been **delisted**; and
  - (b) **one year**, or the period specified in SEBI regulations, has passed `[verify]`.

### Section 237. Power of Central Government to provide for amalgamation of companies in public interest
- (1) If the Central Government is satisfied that it is **essential in the public interest** for two or more companies to amalgamate, it may provide for their amalgamation into a single company by an order **notified in the Official Gazette**. The order sets out the resulting company's constitution, property, powers, rights, interests, authorities and privileges, and its liabilities, duties and obligations.
- (2) The order may also provide for:
  - legal proceedings pending by or against any transferor company to continue by or against the transferee company; and
  - any consequential, incidental and supplemental provisions the Government thinks necessary to give effect to the amalgamation.
- (3) Every member or creditor (including a debenture-holder) of each transferor company keeps, as nearly as possible, the **same interest or rights** in the resulting company as they had in the original company.
  - If their interest or rights are **less**, they are entitled to **compensation** to that extent.
  - The compensation is assessed by the prescribed authority, and every assessment is published in the Official Gazette.
  - The resulting company pays the compensation.
- (4) A person aggrieved by an assessment of compensation may **appeal to the Tribunal** within **30 days** of its publication in the Official Gazette. The Tribunal then makes the assessment.
- (5) No amalgamation order may be made under this section unless:
  - (a) a copy of the **draft order** has been sent to each company concerned;
  - (b) the time for appealing under sub-section (4) has expired, or any appeal made has been finally decided; and
  - (c) the Central Government has considered the suggestions and objections, and made any modifications it thinks fit. These may come from any company, any class of its shareholders, or any of its creditors or class of creditors, within the period the Government fixes. That period must be **not less than two months** from the date the company received the draft.
- (6) A copy of every order under this section must be **laid before each House of Parliament** as soon as possible after it is made.

### Section 238. Registration of offer of schemes involving transfer of shares
- (1) For every offer of a scheme or contract under section 235 involving the transfer of shares in a transferor company to a transferee company:
  - (a) every **circular** containing the offer, and every recommendation by the transferor company's directors that members accept it, must be accompanied by the prescribed information in the prescribed manner;
  - (b) every offer must include a statement by or for the transferee company describing the steps it has taken to make sure the **necessary cash will be available**; and
  - (c) every circular must be **presented to the Registrar for registration**, and may not be issued until registered.
  - Proviso: The Registrar may refuse, for reasons recorded in writing, to register a circular that:
    - does not contain the information required by clause (a); or
    - presents that information in a way likely to give a **false impression**.
  - The Registrar must tell the parties of the refusal within **30 days** of the application.
- (2) An appeal lies to the **Tribunal** against the Registrar's refusal to register a circular.
- (3) A director or other person who issues a circular that has not been presented and registered under clause (c) of sub-section (1) is punishable with a **fine of ₹25,000 to ₹5 lakh** `[verify]`.

### Section 239. Preservation of books and papers of amalgamated companies
- The books and papers of a company that has been amalgamated with another company, or whose shares have been acquired by another company under this Chapter, may not be disposed of without the **prior permission of the Central Government**.
- Before giving permission, the Government may appoint a person to examine them. The purpose is to find out whether they contain evidence of an **offence** in connection with:
  - the promotion or formation of the transferor company, or the management of its affairs; or
  - its amalgamation, or the acquisition of its shares.

### Section 240. Liability of officers in respect of offences committed prior to merger, amalgamation, etc.
- Despite anything in any other law, the liability of the **officers in default** of a transferor company for offences under this Act committed **before** its merger, amalgamation or acquisition **continues after** the merger, amalgamation or acquisition.

## CHAPTER XVI — PREVENTION OF OPPRESSION AND MISMANAGEMENT

### Section 241. Application to Tribunal for relief in cases of oppression, etc.
- (1) Any member of a company may apply to the **Tribunal** for an order under this Chapter if they complain that:
  - (a) the company's affairs have been or are being conducted:
    - in a manner **prejudicial to the public interest**;
    - in a manner **prejudicial or oppressive** to them or to any other member or members; or
    - in a manner **prejudicial to the interests of the company**; or
  - (b) a **material change** has taken place in the management or control of the company, and because of it the company's affairs are likely to be conducted in a manner prejudicial to its interests, or to its members or any class of members.
    - The change may be in the Board of Directors, the manager, the ownership of the company's shares, the membership (if the company has no share capital), or in any other way.
    - A change brought about by, or in the interests of, creditors (including debenture-holders) or any class of shareholders is excluded.
  - The member must have the **right to apply under section 244**.
- (2) If the **Central Government** thinks the company's affairs are being conducted in a manner prejudicial to the public interest, it may itself apply to the Tribunal.
  - Proviso (2017): For prescribed companies or classes of companies, such applications must be made to, and decided by, the **Principal Bench** of the Tribunal.
- (3) (Inserted 2017) The Central Government may start a case against a person and refer it to the Tribunal, asking it to inquire and decide whether that person is a **fit and proper person** to be a director or to hold any other office connected with the conduct and management of any company.
