(2) It is a good defence for the accused to prove that he had no intent to defraud or to conceal the true state of the company's affairs, or to defeat the law [verify: the clauses to which this defence applies].

(3) This sub-section applies where a person pawns, pledges or disposes of property in circumstances that amount to an offence under sub-clause (viii) of clause (d) of sub-section (1). Any person who takes the property in pawn or pledge, or otherwise receives it, knowing it was so pawned, pledged or disposed of, is punishable with:
- imprisonment of not less than three years, which may extend to five years; and
- a fine of not less than three lakh rupees, which may extend to five lakh rupees [verify].

### Section 337. Penalty for frauds by officers
This section applies to a person who, at the time of the alleged offence, was an officer of a company that is later ordered to be wound up by the Tribunal under this Act. He commits an offence if he:
- (a) by false pretences or any other fraud, induced any person to give credit to the company;
- (b) with intent to defraud the company's creditors or any other person:
  - made, or caused to be made, any gift or transfer of, or charge on, the company's property; or
  - caused or connived at the levying of any execution against the company's property; or
- (c) with intent to defraud the company's creditors, concealed or removed any part of the company's property:
  - after the date of any unsatisfied judgment or order for payment of money obtained against the company; or
  - within two months before that date.

Punishment: imprisonment of not less than one year, which may extend to three years, and a fine of not less than one lakh rupees, which may extend to three lakh rupees.

### Section 338. Liability where proper accounts not kept
(1) This sub-section applies where, in the winding up of a company, it is shown that proper books of account were not kept throughout the shorter of:
- the two years immediately before the winding up began; or
- the period between incorporation and the start of the winding up.

Every officer in default is punishable with imprisonment of not less than one year, which may extend to three years, and a fine of not less than one lakh rupees, which may extend to three lakh rupees. This does not apply if he shows that he acted honestly and that, in the circumstances in which the business was carried on, the default was excusable.

(2) For the purposes of sub-section (1), proper books of account are treated as not having been kept if the company did not keep:
- (a) the books or accounts needed to show and explain its transactions and financial position, including books with day-to-day entries, in sufficient detail, of all cash received and paid; and
- (b) where its business involved dealings in goods:
  - statements of annual stock-takings; and
  - except for goods sold by ordinary retail trade, statements of all goods sold and purchased, showing the goods and their buyers and sellers in enough detail to identify them.

### Section 339. Liability for fraudulent conduct of business
(1) If, in the course of winding up, it appears that any business of the company has been carried on with intent to defraud creditors or other persons, or for any fraudulent purpose, the Tribunal may make a declaration under this sub-section. It may do so on the application of the Official Liquidator, the Company Liquidator, or any creditor or contributory, if it thinks proper.

The declaration is that any of the following persons are personally responsible, without any limitation of liability, for all or any of the company's debts or other liabilities, as the Tribunal directs:
- any person who is or has been a director, manager or officer of the company; or
- any person who knowingly took part in carrying on the business in that manner.

On hearing the application, the Official Liquidator or Company Liquidator may himself give evidence or call witnesses.

(2) Effect of a declaration:
- (a) The Tribunal may give further directions to give effect to the declaration. In particular, it may make the liability of the person concerned a charge on:
  - any debt or obligation due from the company to him; or
  - any mortgage or charge, or interest in a mortgage or charge, on the company's assets held by or vested in him, any person on his behalf, any assignee from or through him, or any person acting on his behalf.

  It may make further orders from time to time to enforce any such charge.
- (b) For clause (a), "assignee" includes any person to whom, or in whose favour, the debt, obligation, mortgage or charge was created, issued or transferred, or the interest created, at the direction of the person liable. It does not include an assignee who gave valuable consideration in good faith and without notice of the matters on which the declaration is based. Consideration by way of marriage does not count as valuable consideration for this purpose.

(3) Where any business of a company is carried on with such intent or for such purpose, every person who knowingly took part in carrying it on in that manner is liable for action under section 447.

(4) This section applies even if the person concerned may be criminally liable for the matters on which the declaration is based.

### Section 340. Power of Tribunal to assess damages against delinquent directors, etc.
(1) This sub-section applies if, in the course of winding up, it appears that any of the following persons has misapplied, retained, or become liable or accountable for any money or property of the company, or has been guilty of misfeasance or breach of trust in relation to it:
- any person who took part in the company's promotion or formation; or
- any person who is or has been a director, manager, Company Liquidator or officer of the company.

On an application by the Company Liquidator, or any creditor or contributory, the Tribunal may examine that person's conduct. It may then order him to:
- repay or restore the money or property, or any part of it, with interest at a rate the Tribunal thinks just; or
- contribute to the company's assets such sum by way of compensation as the Tribunal thinks just.

The application must be made within five years from the latest of:
- the date of the winding up order;
- the first appointment of the Company Liquidator; or
- the misapplication, retainer, misfeasance or breach of trust.

(2) This section applies even if the person may be criminally liable for the matter.

### Section 341. Liability under sections 339 and 340 to extend to partners or directors in firms or companies
Where a declaration under section 339 or an order under section 340 is made against a firm or body corporate, the Tribunal may also make a declaration or order against any person who was a partner in that firm or a director of that body corporate at the relevant time.

### Section 342. Prosecution of delinquent officers and members of company
(1) In a winding up by the Tribunal under this Act, if it appears to the Tribunal that any past or present officer, or any member, of the company has committed an offence in relation to the company, it may direct the Company Liquidator either to prosecute the offender himself or to refer the matter to the Registrar. The Tribunal may act on the application of any person interested in the winding up, or on its own motion.

(2) [verify: the provision for a liquidator in a voluntary winding up to report to the Tribunal was omitted or amended by the IBC, 2016.]

(3) If a person is prosecuted under this section, the following must give all the assistance in the prosecution that they are reasonably able to give:
- the Company Liquidator; and
- every past and present officer and agent of the company, other than the defendant.

Explanation: For this sub-section, "agent" includes any banker or legal adviser of the company, and any person employed by the company as auditor.

(4) If a person fails or neglects to give the assistance required by sub-section (3), the Tribunal may, on the Company Liquidator's application, direct him to comply [verify].

(5) Where the Registrar receives a reference and considers that the case is one where a prosecution ought to be instituted, he must report the matter to the Central Government. The Central Government may then direct the Registrar or any other person to institute proceedings [verify].

(6) A person who fails or neglects to give assistance as required is punishable with a fine [verify amount; possibly twenty-five thousand rupees to one lakh rupees, or changed to a monetary penalty in 2020].

### Section 343. Company Liquidator to exercise certain powers subject to sanction
(1) When a company is being wound up by the Tribunal, the Company Liquidator may, with the Tribunal's sanction:
- (a) pay any class of creditors in full;
- (b) make any compromise or arrangement with creditors, or persons claiming to be creditors, or persons having or alleging any present or future, certain or contingent claim against the company, or whereby the company may be made liable; or
- (c) compromise any of the following on agreed terms:
  - any call or liability to a call;
  - any debt, or liability capable of resulting in a debt;
  - any present or future, certain or contingent, ascertained or sounding-in-damages claim existing (or thought to exist) between the company and a contributory, alleged contributory, other debtor, or person apprehending liability to the company; and
  - all questions in any way relating to or affecting the company's assets, liabilities or winding up.

  He may also take security for discharging any such call, debt, liability or claim, and give a complete discharge for it.

(2) Any creditor or contributory may apply to the Tribunal about the exercise, or proposed exercise, of any of these powers.

### Section 344. Statement that company is in liquidation
(1) Where a company is being wound up, every document of the following kinds must contain a statement that the company is being wound up, if the company's name appears on or in it:
- any invoice;
- any order for goods or services; and
- any business letter,

issued by or on behalf of the company, its Company Liquidator, or a receiver or manager of its property.

(2) If there is a default in complying with sub-section (1), the following persons, if they wilfully authorise or permit the default, are punishable with a fine of not less than fifty thousand rupees, which may extend to three lakh rupees:
- the company;
- every officer of the company;
- the Company Liquidator; and
- any receiver or manager [verify; possibly converted to a monetary penalty by the Companies (Amendment) Act, 2020].

### Section 345. Books and papers of company to be evidence
Where a company is being wound up, all books and papers of the company and of the Company Liquidator are prima facie evidence, as between the contributories, of the truth of everything recorded in them.

### Section 346. Inspection of books and papers by creditors and contributories
(1) At any time after a winding up order is made, any creditor or contributory may inspect the company's books and papers, but only in accordance with the rules prescribed.

(2) Sub-section (1) does not exclude or restrict any rights conferred by any law on:
- (a) the Central Government or a State Government;
- (b) any authority or officer of such Government; or
- (c) any person acting under the authority of such Government, authority or officer.

### Section 347. Disposal of books and papers of company
(1) When the company's affairs have been completely wound up and it is about to be dissolved, its books and papers, and those of the Company Liquidator, may be disposed of as the Tribunal directs.

(2) After five years from the company's dissolution, no responsibility lies on the company, the Company Liquidator, or any person entrusted with the books and papers, because a book or paper cannot be produced to a person claiming to be interested in it.

(3) The Central Government may make rules to:
- (a) prevent, for any period it thinks proper (not exceeding five years from dissolution), the destruction of the books and papers of a wound-up company and of its Company Liquidator; and
- (b) enable any creditor or contributory to make representations to the Central Government about the matters in clause (a), and to appeal to the Tribunal against any direction the Central Government gives on them.

(4) A person who contravenes any rule under sub-section (3), or any direction of the Central Government under it, is punishable with imprisonment up to six months, or a fine up to fifty thousand rupees, or both [verify; possibly modified in 2020].

### Section 348. Information as to pending liquidations
(1) If a winding up is not concluded within one year after it began, the Company Liquidator must file a statement with the Tribunal on the proceedings and position of the liquidation. The requirements are:
- the first statement must be filed within two months after the end of that year;
- further statements must be filed at intervals of not more than one year (or any shorter prescribed interval) until the winding up is concluded;
- each statement must be in the prescribed form, contain the prescribed particulars, and be audited by a person qualified to act as the company's auditor.

The Central Government may exempt the Company Liquidator from this requirement, wholly or partly. No such audit is needed where section 294 applies.

(2) When filing a statement under sub-section (1), the Company Liquidator must at the same time file a copy with the Registrar, who keeps it with the company's other records.

(3) Where the statement relates to a Government company in liquidation, the Company Liquidator must send a copy to:
- (a) the Central Government, if it is a member of the company;
- (b) the State Government, if it is a member; or
- (c) both Governments, if both are members.

(4) Any person who states in writing that he is a creditor or contributory may, personally or through an agent, inspect the statement at all reasonable times on payment of the prescribed fee, and obtain a copy or extract.

(5) A person who untruthfully states that he is a creditor or contributory under sub-section (4) is treated as having committed an offence under section 182 of the Indian Penal Code. On the Company Liquidator's application, he is punishable accordingly.

(6) If the Company Liquidator contravenes this section, he is punishable with a fine up to five thousand rupees for every day the failure continues [verify; possibly converted to a monetary penalty in 2020].

(7) [verify: a sub-section providing punishment where a Company Liquidator wilfully causes the statement not to be audited may exist.]

### Section 349. Official Liquidator to make payments into public account of India
Every Official Liquidator must pay the money he receives as Official Liquidator of any company into the public account of India in the Reserve Bank of India, in the prescribed manner and at the prescribed times.

### Section 350. Company Liquidator to deposit monies into scheduled bank
(1) Every Company Liquidator must deposit the money he receives as Company Liquidator of any company into a scheduled bank, in a special bank account opened by him for that purpose. This must be done in the prescribed manner and at the prescribed times.

(2) A Company Liquidator who retains more than five thousand rupees for more than ten days must pay interest on the excess at twelve per cent per annum, and is liable to any penalty the Tribunal determines. This does not apply if:
- the Tribunal has, on his application, authorised him to retain a higher amount; or
- he explains the retention to the Tribunal's satisfaction.

### Section 351. Liquidator not to deposit monies into private banking account
A Company Liquidator must not deposit any money he receives in that capacity into any private banking account [verify: any accompanying consequence or penalty is not reliably recalled].

### Section 352. Company Liquidation Dividend and Undistributed Assets Account
(1) Where a company is being wound up, the Company Liquidator must immediately deposit the following money into a separate special account called the "Company Liquidation Dividend and Undistributed Assets Account", kept in a scheduled bank:
- (a) dividends payable to any creditor that have remained unpaid for six months after the date they were declared; and
- (b) assets refundable to any contributory that have remained undistributed for six months after the date they became refundable.

(2) On the company's dissolution, the Company Liquidator must pay into that Account any money representing unpaid dividends or undistributed assets that he holds at the date of dissolution.

(3) When making a payment under sub-section (1) or (2), the Company Liquidator must give the officer appointed by the Central Government a statement in the prescribed form. For all sums included in the payment, it must set out:
- the nature of the sums;
- the names and last known addresses of the persons entitled to participate in them;
- the amount each person is entitled to;
- the nature of each person's claim; and
- any other prescribed particulars.

(4) The Company Liquidator is entitled to a receipt from the scheduled bank for money paid under sub-sections (1) and (2). The receipt is an effective discharge for him in respect of that money.

(5) [verify: a provision on where the Company Liquidator makes payments in a winding up by the Tribunal, for example by transfer to the public account of India.]

(6) Any person claiming to be entitled to money paid into the Account may apply to the Tribunal for payment. This applies whether the money was paid in under this section or under any earlier company law. If satisfied that the claimant is entitled, the Tribunal may order payment of the sum due. Before making the order, it must give notice of the application to any person it thinks fit, who may appear and be heard.

(7) Money paid into the Account that remains unclaimed for fifteen years is transferred to the general revenue account of the Central Government. A claim to money so transferred may still be made under sub-section (6) and dealt with as if the transfer had not occurred. Any order for payment on the claim is treated as an order for refund of revenue [verify].

(8) [verify: further provision.]

(9) If a Company Liquidator keeps any money that he should have paid into the Account, he must:
- (a) pay interest on the amount retained at twelve per cent per annum, and any penalty as determined [verify];
- (b) pay any expenses caused by his default; and
- (c) in a winding up by the Tribunal, also be liable to be removed from his appointment by the Tribunal, and to forfeit all or part of his remuneration [verify].

### Section 353. Liquidator to make returns, etc.
(1) A Company Liquidator may default in filing, delivering or making any return, account or other document, or in giving any notice, that the law requires. If he fails to make good the default within fourteen days after being served with a notice requiring him to do so, the Tribunal may order him to make it good within a specified time. The Tribunal acts on an application by any contributory or creditor, or by the Registrar.

(2) The order may provide that the Company Liquidator bears all costs of and incidental to the application.

(3) This section does not affect any law imposing penalties on a Company Liquidator for such a default.

### Section 354. Meetings to ascertain wishes of creditors or contributories
(1) In all matters relating to the winding up of a company, the Tribunal may:
- (a) have regard to the wishes of the creditors or contributories, as proved to it by sufficient evidence;
- (b) if it thinks fit, direct meetings of creditors or contributories to be called, held and conducted as it directs, in order to find out those wishes; and
- (c) appoint a person to chair any such meeting and report the result to it.

(2) For creditors, regard is had to the value of each creditor's debt.

(3) For contributories, regard is had to the number of votes each contributory may cast under this Act or the articles.

### Section 355. Court, tribunal or person, etc., before whom affidavit may be sworn
(1) An affidavit required under this Chapter may be sworn:
- in India, before any court, tribunal, judge or person lawfully authorised to take and receive affidavits; and
- in any other country, before any court, judge or person lawfully authorised to take and receive affidavits in that country, or before any Indian diplomatic or consular officer.

(2) All courts, tribunals, judges, justices, commissioners and persons acting judicially in India must take judicial notice of the seal, stamp or signature of any such court, judge, person, or diplomatic or consular officer. This applies when it is attached, appended or subscribed to any such affidavit or other document to be used for the purposes of this Chapter.

### Section 356. Powers of Tribunal to declare dissolution of company void
(1) Where a company has been dissolved, whether under this Act or under the Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code, 2016 [verify reference], the Tribunal may declare the dissolution void, on any terms it thinks fit. It may do so at any time within two years of the date of dissolution, on an application by:
- the Company Liquidator; or
- any other person who appears to the Tribunal to be interested.

After such a declaration, any proceedings may be taken as if the company had not been dissolved.

(2) Within thirty days after the order, or any longer time the Tribunal allows, the Company Liquidator or the applicant must file a certified copy of the order with the Registrar, who registers it. If the Company Liquidator or the applicant fails to do so, he is punishable with a fine up to ten thousand rupees for every day the default continues [verify; possibly converted by the Companies (Amendment) Act, 2020 into a monetary penalty].

### Section 357. Commencement of winding up by Tribunal
A winding up of a company by the Tribunal under this Act is treated as beginning at the time the winding up petition is presented. (Provisions relating to a prior voluntary winding up resolution were omitted or amended by the IBC, 2016 [verify].)

### Section 358. Exclusion of certain time in computing period of limitation
This section applies notwithstanding the Limitation Act, 1963 or any other law. In computing the limitation period for any suit or application in the name and on behalf of a company being wound up by the Tribunal, the following period is excluded: the period from the date the winding up began until one year after the date of the winding up order.

### PART IV — OFFICIAL LIQUIDATORS

### Section 359. Appointment of Official Liquidator
(1) For the purposes of winding up of companies by the Tribunal, the Central Government may appoint as many of the following as it considers necessary to discharge the functions of the Official Liquidator:
- Official Liquidators; and
- Joint, Deputy or Assistant Official Liquidators.

(2) Liquidators appointed under sub-section (1) are whole-time officers of the Central Government.

(3) The Central Government pays the salaries and allowances of the Official Liquidators and the Joint, Deputy and Assistant Official Liquidators.

### Section 360. Powers and functions of Official Liquidator
(1) The Official Liquidator conducts the winding up proceedings of a company and performs the duties the Central Government [verify: or the Tribunal] specifies, in:
- (a) the winding up of a company under section 361 (summary procedure) [verify]; and
- (b) any other cases the Tribunal directs.

(2) The Official Liquidator has the same powers as a Company Liquidator under this Act.

### Section 361. Summary procedure for liquidation
(1) The Central Government may order a company to be wound up summarily under this Part where the company:
- (a) has assets of book value not exceeding one crore rupees [verify: threshold may have been revised]; and
- (b) belongs to a prescribed class or classes of companies.

(2) When such an order is made, the Central Government appoints the Official Liquidator as the company's liquidator.

(3) On appointment, the Official Liquidator must take all the company's assets, effects and actionable claims into his custody.

(4) Within thirty days of his appointment, the Official Liquidator must submit a report to the Central Government stating whether any fraud has been committed in the company's promotion, formation or management [verify].

(5) If, on considering the report, the Central Government believes fraud has been committed, it may order further investigation under section 210. This does not affect the winding up. On considering the investigation report, it may pass orders and give directions under sections 339 to 342, or direct the Official Liquidator to apply to the Tribunal for necessary orders [verify].

### Section 362. Sale of assets and recovery of debts due to company
(1) The Official Liquidator must dispose of all the company's assets, movable or immovable, as quickly as possible and within the time the Central Government specifies.

(2) Within the time the Central Government specifies, the Official Liquidator must send notices to recover debts due to the company, and take steps to realise them [verify].

(3) Amounts recovered by the Official Liquidator are deposited in accordance with section 349.

### Section 363. Settlement of claims of creditors by Official Liquidator
(1) Within thirty days of his appointment, the Official Liquidator must call on the company's creditors to prove their claims within thirty days of receiving the notice.

(2) Within thirty days of receiving the claims, the Official Liquidator must prepare a list of creditors' claims. If he considers a claim inadmissible, he may reject it after giving reasons in writing.

(3) The Official Liquidator then pays creditors in accordance with the list and the order of priority under this Act, after providing for the costs of the winding up [verify].

### Section 364. Appeal by creditor
(1) A creditor aggrieved by the Official Liquidator's decision under section 363 may appeal to the Tribunal [verify: possibly the Central Government] within thirty days of the decision.

(2) After calling for the Official Liquidator's report, the appellate authority may dismiss the appeal or modify the Official Liquidator's decision.

(3) No payment may be made to creditors until the appeal is disposed of.

### Section 365. Order of dissolution of company
(1) After the claims of creditors have been finally settled and paid, and the affairs of the company have been completely wound up, the Official Liquidator must submit a report to the Central Government [verify: or the Tribunal]. If satisfied, it must order the company to be dissolved.

(2) The Registrar must be informed of the order, and must strike the company's name off the register of companies and publish a notice to that effect in the Official Gazette [verify].

## CHAPTER XXI — COMPANIES AUTHORISED TO REGISTER UNDER THIS ACT

### PART I — COMPANIES CAPABLE OF BEING REGISTERED

### Section 366. Companies capable of being registered
(1) For this Part, "company" includes any partnership firm, limited liability partnership, cooperative society, society, or any other business entity formed under any other law that applies for registration under this Part.

(2) A company may register under this Act as:
- an unlimited company;
- a company limited by shares; or
- a company limited by guarantee.

This applies to any company formed before or after this Act commenced under any Act of Parliament other than this Act, or under any other law, or otherwise duly constituted according to law. The company must have the prescribed number of members [verify: the Rules prescribe a minimum of seven members [verify: or two]]. Registration is not invalid merely because it is made with a view to the company being wound up. The following conditions apply:
- (a) a company registered under the Companies Act, 1956 or any previous company law may not register under this section;
- (b) a company whose members' liability is limited by any Act or other law may not register under this section as an unlimited company or as a company limited by guarantee;
- (c) a company may register under this section only if a majority of its members present in person or by proxy at a general meeting called for the purpose assent to the registration;
- (d) where a company whose members' liability is not limited by any Act or other law is about to register as a limited company, the required majority is not less than three-fourths of the members present in person or by proxy at the meeting;
- (e) where a company is about to register as a company limited by guarantee, the assent must be accompanied by a resolution declaring that each member undertakes to contribute to the company's assets, up to a specified amount, if the company is wound up while he is a member or within one year after he ceases to be a member. The contribution is for:
  - payment of the debts and liabilities contracted before he ceased to be a member;
  - the costs and expenses of winding up; and
  - adjustment of the rights of contributories among themselves.

(3) In computing any majority under this section on a poll, regard is had to the number of votes each member is entitled to under the company's regulations.

### Section 367. Certificate of registration of existing companies
Once the requirements of this Part for registration are complied with and the prescribed fees are paid, the Registrar must certify under his hand that the applicant company is incorporated as a company under this Act. For a limited company, he must also certify that it is limited. The company is then incorporated accordingly.

### Section 368. Vesting of property on registration
On registration under this Part, all movable and immovable property (including actionable claims) belonging to or vested in the company at the date of registration passes to and vests in the company as incorporated under this Act, for all the company's estate and interest in it.

### Section 369. Saving of existing liabilities
Registration of a company under this Part does not affect the company's rights or liabilities in respect of any debt or obligation incurred, or any contract entered into, by, to, with or on behalf of the company before registration.

### Section 370. Continuation of pending legal proceedings
All suits and other legal proceedings pending at the time of registration under this Part may continue as if registration had not taken place. This covers proceedings by or against the company, or by or against any public officer or member of the company.

Proviso: No execution may issue against the effects of any individual member on a decree or order obtained in such a suit or proceeding. If the company's property and effects are insufficient to satisfy the decree or order, an order may be obtained for winding up the company.

### Section 371. Effect of registration under this Part
(1) When a company is registered under this Part, subject to this section:
- (a) all provisions in any Act of Parliament, other law, or other instrument constituting or regulating the company are treated as the company's conditions and regulations. They apply in the same manner and with the same incidents as if:
  - the provisions that would have had to be in the memorandum, had the company been formed under this Act, were contained in a registered memorandum; and
  - the rest were contained in registered articles.

  For a company registered as limited by guarantee, this includes the resolution declaring the amount of the guarantee.
- (b) all provisions of this Act apply to the company and its members, contributories and creditors as if it had been formed under this Act, subject to the following:
  - (i) Table F in Schedule I does not apply unless adopted by special resolution;
  - (ii) the provisions of this Act on numbering of shares do not apply to a joint stock company whose shares are not numbered;
  - (iii) the company may not alter any provision in an Act of Parliament or other law relating to it;
  - (iv) the company may not alter any provision in letters patent relating to it without the Central Government's sanction;
  - (v) the company may not alter any provision in a royal charter or letters patent relating to its objects, except to the extent this Act permits [verify];
  - (vi) in a winding up, every person liable for the company's debts and liabilities contracted before registration is a contributory, and must contribute all sums due from him in respect of that liability. On the death or insolvency of any such contributory, the provisions of this Act on legal representatives, heirs and assignees of contributories apply [verify].

(2) The provisions of this Act on the following matters apply notwithstanding any provision in an Act of Parliament, other law, or other instrument constituting or regulating the company:
- (a) registration of an unlimited company as limited;
- (b) the power of an unlimited company, on registration as limited:
  - to increase the nominal amount of its share capital; and
  - to provide that part of its share capital can be called up only on winding up; and
- (c) the power of a limited company to decide that part of its share capital can be called up only on winding up.

(3) This section does not authorise the company to alter any provision of an instrument relating to it that would have had to be in its memorandum had it been originally formed under this Act, and that this Act does not authorise it to alter.

(4) Nothing in this Act takes away any power to alter its constitution or regulations that is vested in the company by any Act of Parliament, other law, or instrument constituting or regulating it.

(5) In this section, "instrument" includes a deed of settlement, deed of partnership, or limited liability partnership agreement [verify].

### Section 372. Power of court to stay or restrain proceedings
This section applies to a company registered under this Part where a creditor applies to stay or restrain proceedings. In that case, the provisions of this Act on staying and restraining suits and legal proceedings against a company, after a winding up petition is presented and before a winding up order is made, extend to suits and legal proceedings against any contributory of the company.

### Section 373. Suits stayed on winding up order
Where an order has been made to wind up a company registered under this Part, no suit or other legal proceeding may be started or continued against the company, or against any contributory, in respect of any debt of the company. This applies except with the Tribunal's leave and subject to any terms the Tribunal imposes.

### Section 374. Obligations of companies registering under this Part
Every company seeking registration under this Part must:
- (a) where it has secured loans, obtain a no-objection certificate from the secured creditors concerned;
- (b) obtain the consent of its members (for a limited liability partnership, of all its partners) to registration under this Act [verify];
- (c) [verify: make arrangements or give undertakings for winding up or dissolution of the entity under the law under which it was formed]; and
- (d) file with the Registrar the prescribed documents and particulars along with the application [verify].

(The supporting documents are prescribed by the Companies (Authorised to Register) Rules, 2014 [verify].)

### PART II — WINDING UP OF UNREGISTERED COMPANIES

### Section 375. Winding up of unregistered companies
(1) Subject to this Part, any unregistered company may be wound up under this Act. All provisions of this Act on winding up apply to it, with the exceptions and additions in sub-sections (2) to (4).

(2) No unregistered company may be wound up voluntarily under this Act [verify: post-IBC wording].

(3) An unregistered company may be wound up in the following circumstances:
- (a) if the company is dissolved, has ceased to carry on business, or is carrying on business only to wind up its affairs;
- (b) if the company is unable to pay its debts [verify: retained after the IBC, 2016]; or
- (c) if the Tribunal considers it just and equitable to wind the company up.

(4) An unregistered company is treated as unable to pay its debts in any of these cases:
- (a) a creditor (by assignment or otherwise) to whom the company owes a sum exceeding one lakh rupees then due has served a written demand for payment, and the company has neglected for three weeks after service to pay, secure or compound for the sum to the creditor's satisfaction. The demand may be served:
  - by leaving it at the company's principal place of business;
  - by delivering it to the secretary, or a director, manager or principal officer; or
  - in any other manner the Tribunal approves or directs;
- (b) a suit or other legal proceeding has been instituted against any member for a debt or demand due, or claimed to be due, from the company or from him as a member. Notice of the suit has been served on the company in a manner described in clause (a). Within ten days after service, the company has not:
  - (i) paid, secured or compounded for the debt or demand;
  - (ii) got the suit or proceeding stayed; or
  - (iii) indemnified the defendant to his satisfaction against the suit or proceeding and all costs, damages and expenses arising from it;
- (c) execution or other process on a decree or order of any court or Tribunal in favour of a creditor has been returned wholly or partly unsatisfied. This applies to process against the company, any member as such, or any person authorised to be sued as nominal defendant on the company's behalf; or
- (d) it is otherwise proved to the Tribunal's satisfaction that the company is unable to pay its debts.

Explanation: For this Part, "unregistered company":
- (a) does not include:
  - (i) a railway company incorporated by an Act of Parliament, other Indian law, or an Act of the Parliament of the United Kingdom;
  - (ii) a company registered under this Act; or
  - (iii) a company registered under any previous company law and not being a company whose registered office was in Burma, Aden or Pakistan immediately before the separation of those countries from India [verify];
- (b) apart from these exclusions, includes any partnership firm, limited liability partnership or society, co-operative society, association or company consisting of more than seven members when the winding up petition is presented [verify].

### Section 376. Power to wind up foreign companies, although dissolved
A body corporate incorporated outside India that has carried on business in India and ceases to do so may be wound up as an unregistered company under this Part. This applies even if it has been dissolved or has otherwise ceased to exist under the laws of the country of its incorporation.

### Section 377. Provisions of Chapter cumulative
(1) The provisions of this Part on unregistered companies are in addition to, and do not reduce, the provisions of this Act on winding up of companies by the Tribunal.

(2) In the case of unregistered companies, the Tribunal or Company Liquidator may exercise any power or do any act that it or he could exercise or do in winding up companies formed and registered under this Act. However, an unregistered company is not treated as a company under this Act except when it is being wound up, and then only to the extent this Part provides.

### Section 378. Saving and construction of enactments conferring power to wind up partnership firm, association or company, etc., in certain cases
This Part does not affect any law providing for a partnership firm, limited liability partnership, society or other association or company to be wound up, or wound up as a company or as an unregistered company, under the Companies Act, 1956 or any Act it repealed. However, references in any such law to a provision of the Companies Act, 1956 or a repealed Act are to be read as references to the corresponding provision, if any, of this Act.

## CHAPTER XXIA — PRODUCER COMPANIES

(Chapter XXIA, sections 378A to 378ZU, was inserted by the Companies (Amendment) Act, 2020 (29 of 2020) [verify: w.e.f. 11-02-2021]. It is broadly modelled on Part IXA, sections 581A to 581ZT, of the Companies Act, 1956. Those provisions previously applied to Producer Companies through the proviso to section 465(1). The numbering and headings of individual sections below are reconstructed and should be checked [verify].)

### Section 378A. Definitions
In this Chapter, unless the context requires otherwise:
- (a) "active member" means a member who meets the quantum and period of patronage of the Producer Company required by its articles;
- (b) "Chief Executive" means the Chief Executive appointed under section 378W [verify cross-reference];
- (c) "inter-State co-operative society" means a multi-State co-operative society registered under the Multi-State Co-operative Societies Act, 2002 [verify];
- (d) "limited return" means the maximum dividend specified by the articles;
- (e) "member" means a person or producer institution, whether incorporated or not, admitted as a member of a Producer Company, consisting only of primary producers;
- (f) "mutual assistance principles" means the principles set out in section 378? [verify cross-reference]. In substance they are:
  - membership is voluntary and open to all eligible persons who can participate in or avail of the company's facilities or services and who accept the duties of membership;
  - each member generally has only a single vote, irrespective of shareholding;
  - the company is administered by a Board elected or appointed in a manner consistent with the articles, and the Board is accountable to the members;
  - there is a limited return on share capital;
  - surplus is distributed equitably, by providing for development of the business, providing common facilities, and distributing among members in proportion to their participation in the business;
  - provision is made for educating members, employees and others in the principles of mutuality and techniques of mutual assistance;
  - the company actively co-operates with other Producer Companies and organisations at local, national or international level to serve members' interests and their communities;
- (g) "patronage" means the use of the Producer Company's services by its members through participation in its business activities;
- (h) "patronage bonus" means payments made by the Producer Company out of its surplus income to members, in proportion to their respective patronage;
- (i) "primary produce" means:
  - (i) produce of farmers arising from agriculture (including animal husbandry, horticulture, floriculture, pisciculture, viticulture, forestry, forest products, re-vegetation, bee raising and farming plantation products), or from any other primary activity or service that promotes the interest of farmers or consumers;
  - (ii) produce of persons engaged in handloom, handicraft and other cottage industries;
  - (iii) any product resulting from any of these activities, including by-products;
  - (iv) any product resulting from an ancillary activity that would assist or promote any of these activities; and
  - (v) any activity intended to increase the production of, or improve the quality of, any of the above;
- (j) "Producer" means any person engaged in any activity connected with or relatable to any primary produce;
- (k) "Producer Company" means a body corporate having objects or activities specified in section 378B and registered as a Producer Company under this Act;
- (l) "producer institution" means a Producer Company, or any other institution whose only members are producers or Producer Companies, whether incorporated or not, that has any of the objects in section 378B and agrees to use the services of the Producer Company as provided in its articles; and
- (m) "withheld price" means the part of the price due and payable for goods supplied by a member that the Producer Company withholds for payment at a later date.

[verify clause lettering throughout.]

### Section 378B. Objects of Producer Company
A Producer Company has one or more of the following objects:
- (a) production, harvesting, procurement, grading, pooling, handling, marketing, selling and export of members' primary produce, or import of goods or services for their benefit. The company may do these things itself or through another institution;
- (b) processing of members' produce, including preserving, drying, distilling, brewing, vinting, canning and packaging;
- (c) manufacture, sale or supply of machinery, equipment or consumables mainly to its members;
- (d) providing education on mutual assistance principles to its members and others;
- (e) rendering technical services, consultancy, training, research and development, and all other activities to promote members' interests;
- (f) generation, transmission and distribution of power, revitalisation of land and water resources, and their use, conservation and communications, relatable to primary produce;
- (g) insurance of producers or their primary produce;
- (h) promoting techniques of mutuality and mutual assistance;
- (i) welfare measures or facilities for members, as the Board decides;
- (j) any other activity ancillary or incidental to clauses (a) to (i), or that otherwise promotes mutuality and mutual assistance among members; and
- (k) financing procurement, processing, marketing or other activities in clauses (a) to (j), including extending credit or other financial services to members.

Explanation: Every Producer Company must deal primarily with the produce of its active members in carrying out any of its objects.

### Section 378C. Formation of Producer Company and its registration
(1) Any of the following may form an incorporated company as a Producer Company under this Act, if it has objects specified in section 378B and otherwise complies with this Chapter and the other provisions of this Act on registration:
- ten or more individuals, each of them a producer;
- two or more producer institutions; or
- a combination of ten or more individuals and producer institutions.

(2) If the Registrar is satisfied that all requirements of this Act on registration, and on matters precedent and incidental to it, have been complied with, he must, within thirty days of receiving the documents:
- register the memorandum, articles and other documents, if any; and
- issue a certificate of incorporation.

(3) On registration, the Producer Company becomes a body corporate as if it were a private company to which this Chapter applies. However, there is no limit on the maximum number of its members. The words "Producer Company Limited" must form part of its name.

(4) [verify: minimum paid-up capital requirement, for example five lakh rupees or as prescribed.]

(5) A Producer Company may not, in any circumstances whatsoever, become or be treated as a public company under this Act [verify sub-section numbering].

### Section 378D. Membership and voting rights of members of Producer Company
(1) Where a Producer Company's membership consists only of individual members, each member has a single vote, irrespective of shareholding.

(2) Where its membership consists only of producer institutions, their voting rights are determined by their participation in the company's business in the previous financial year, as the articles specify. In the first year after registration, voting rights are determined by shareholding.

(3) Where its membership consists of both individuals and producer institutions, voting is on the basis of a single vote for every member.

(4) The articles may restrict voting by a member who is not an active member, and may lay down conditions for continuing membership and for cessation of membership [verify].

(5) A member who is not a producer, or who has dealings or business that conflict with the company's interests, may not exercise voting rights or hold office, as provided in the articles [verify].

### Section 378E. Benefits to members
(1) Members initially receive only the value for produce or products pooled and supplied that the Producer Company anticipates, as the Board determines. The withheld price may be paid later, in cash, in kind, or by allotment of equity shares, in the manner the Board decides.

(2) Members may receive a limited return on share capital, as the articles specify.

(3) Members may be allotted bonus shares in accordance with this Chapter.

(4) Members may receive a patronage bonus out of surplus, in proportion to their patronage [verify sub-section structure].

### Section 378F. Memorandum of Producer Company
The memorandum of a Producer Company must state:
- (a) its name, with "Producer Company Limited" as the last words;
- (b) the State in which its registered office is to be situated;
- (c) its main objects, which must be one or more of the objects in section 378B;
- (d) the names and addresses of the subscribers to the memorandum;
- (e) the amount of share capital with which it is to be registered, and its division into shares of a fixed amount;
- (f) the names, addresses and occupations of the subscribers, being producers, who are to act as first directors;
- (g) that the liability of its members is limited; and
- (h) if its objects are not confined to one State, the States to whose territories they extend.

Each subscriber must sign the memorandum [verify].

### Section 378G. Articles of association
(1) The subscribers to the memorandum must sign the articles of association.

(2) The articles must provide for matters including the following [verify the full list]:
- qualifications for membership, the conditions for continuing membership, and the rights and responsibilities of members;
- the manner in which members use the company's services, and the services they are entitled to;
- the number, election, tenure, rotation and removal of directors;
- the procedure for Board and general meetings;
- the manner of making payment of withheld price, patronage bonus and limited return;
- the creation of general and specific reserves, and the manner of distributing surplus;
- the requirement that members provide information to the Board;
- the admission of new members and the manner of transfer of shares and voting rights; and
- other matters relating to mutual assistance principles.

### Section 378H. Amendment of memorandum
(1) A Producer Company may amend its memorandum by special resolution, subject to this Chapter [verify].

(2) Where the amendment involves transferring the registered office from one State to another, a copy of the special resolution must be filed with the Registrar of each State concerned [verify].

(3) The amended memorandum, with a copy of the special resolution, must be filed with the Registrar within thirty days of the resolution. The Registrar registers it and certifies the registration [verify].

### Section 378I. Amendment of articles
(1) The articles of a Producer Company may be amended by special resolution.

(2) A copy of the amended articles must be filed with the Registrar within thirty days of their adoption.

### Section 378J. Option to inter-State co-operative societies to become Producer Companies
(1) An inter-State co-operative society whose objects are not confined to one State may apply to the Registrar for registration as a Producer Company under this Chapter.

(2) The application must be accompanied by [verify]:
- (a) a copy of a special resolution of not less than two-thirds of the society's total members, approving its incorporation as a Producer Company;
- (b) a statement of the names and addresses of the society's directors and chief executive;
- (c) a list of the society's members;
- (d) a statement that the society is engaged in one or more of the objects in section 378B; and
- (e) a declaration by two or more directors certifying that the particulars given are correct.

(3) If satisfied that the requirements are complied with, the Registrar must, within thirty days of receiving the application, register it and certify that the society is registered and incorporated as a Producer Company.

(4) On registration, the Registrar must immediately inform the registering authority with which the society was previously registered, so that the society can be removed from its register.

### Section 378K. Effect of registration
On registration of an inter-State co-operative society as a Producer Company:
- the society becomes a Producer Company;
- it is governed by this Chapter and the applicable provisions of this Act; and
- it ceases to be governed by the law under which it was registered as a co-operative society [verify].

### Section 378L. Vesting of undertaking in Producer Company
On registration of an inter-State co-operative society as a Producer Company:
- all the society's properties, assets, rights, interests, liabilities and obligations immediately before registration vest in the Producer Company;
- pending legal proceedings by or against the society continue by or against the Producer Company; and
- the society's employees become employees of the Producer Company on terms not less favourable to them [verify].

### Section 378M. [Heading not reliably recalled] [verify]
[verify: the heading and content of this section are not reliably recalled. It is understood to deal with the management of a Producer Company (for example, provision for a Board of directors and its composition) or with cessation or withdrawal of membership.]

### Section 378N. Number of directors
Every Producer Company must have at least five and not more than fifteen directors. Where an inter-State co-operative society is incorporated as a Producer Company, it may have more than fifteen directors for a period of one year from the date of its incorporation [verify].

### Section 378O. Appointment / election of directors [verify heading]
(1) The members who signed the memorandum and are designated as directors govern the company until directors are elected [verify].

(2) Directors must be elected or appointed within ninety days of registration [verify].

(3) Each director holds office for not less than one year and not more than five years, as the articles specify. A retiring director is eligible for re-election [verify].

(4) The articles may provide for co-opting expert directors or additional directors, not exceeding one-fifth of the total number of directors. Such directors have no voting rights and are not eligible to be the chairman [verify].

### Section 378P. Vacation of office by directors [verify heading]
A director vacates office on the grounds specified in section 167, and on any additional grounds the articles provide. These may include ceasing to be an active member, or defaults relating to dues to the Producer Company [verify].

### Section 378Q. Powers and functions of Board [verify heading]
Subject to the articles, the Board exercises all powers needed for the efficient administration of the Producer Company. These include [verify list]:
- determining the dividend payable;
- determining the quantum of withheld price and recommending patronage;
- admitting new members;
- pursuing and formulating corporate strategy and plans;
- appointing a Chief Executive and other officers;
- exercising superintendence, direction and control over the Chief Executive and other officers;
- ensuring proper books of account are kept, and preparing the annual accounts for the general meeting;
- acquiring or disposing of property in the ordinary course of business;
- investing funds in the ordinary course of business;
- sanctioning loans to members; and
- taking other measures necessary for the proper conduct of the business.

### Section 378R. Matters to be transacted at general meeting [verify heading]
The following matters require approval by the members at a general meeting:
- approval of the budget and the annual accounts;
- approval of patronage bonus;
- issue of bonus shares;
- declaration of limited return and its distribution;
- other matters as specified by the articles [verify].

### Section 378S. Chief Executive and his functions [verify heading]
(1) Every Producer Company must have a full-time Chief Executive, by whatever name called, appointed by the Board from persons other than its members. He is an ex officio director and is not liable to retire by rotation.

(2) The Chief Executive manages the company's affairs, subject to the Board's superintendence, direction and control, and is accountable for the company's performance. His functions include [verify]:
- day-to-day administration;
- operating bank accounts;
- making arrangements for safe custody of cash and assets;
- maintaining books of account and preparing annual accounts;
- furnishing members with periodic information; and
- assisting the Board in formulating goals, objectives, strategies, plans and policies.

### Section 378T. Secretary of Producer Company [verify heading]
Every Producer Company whose average annual turnover exceeds five crore rupees in each of three consecutive financial years must have a whole-time secretary who possesses the prescribed qualifications [verify threshold].

### Section 378U. Meetings of Board and quorum [verify heading]
(1) Board meetings are held at least once in every three months, and at least four meetings are held in each year [verify].

(2) Notice of every Board meeting must be given in writing to every director at least seven days before the meeting [verify].

(3) The quorum is one-third of the total strength of directors, subject to a minimum of three [verify].

### Section 378V. Committee of directors [verify heading]
The Board may constitute committees to assist it in the effective discharge of its functions. A committee may include persons other than directors, as the Board decides, but members of a committee who are not directors have no voting rights. The Board fixes their fees and allowances [verify].

### Section 378W. Liability of directors [verify heading]
(1) Where a director knowingly acquiesces in or permits any action that contravenes this Chapter, causing loss to the Producer Company or its members, the company may recover from that director the loss or damage caused, any wrongful gain, or any profit made [verify].

(2) This liability is in addition to any other liability or punishment under this Act or any other law [verify].

### Section 378X. Officers and employees of Producer Company [verify heading]
The Board may appoint officers and employees, and fix their remuneration and terms and conditions of service, on the recommendation of the Chief Executive and as specified by the articles [verify].

### Section 378Y. General meetings [verify heading]
(1) Every Producer Company must hold an annual general meeting each year, in addition to any other meetings [verify].

(2) The first annual general meeting must be held within ninety days of incorporation. The gap between two annual general meetings must not exceed fifteen months [verify].

(3) Notice of the annual general meeting is given at least fourteen days before the meeting, accompanied by the specified documents. These include the agenda, the minutes of the previous meeting, the names of candidates for election, the audited balance sheet, the profit and loss account, the auditor's report, and the Board's report [verify].

(4) The quorum, procedure and other matters are as specified in the articles [verify].

### Section 378Z. Share capital and members' rights [verify heading]
(1) A Producer Company may have only equity share capital. Its shares may be held only by members [verify].

(2) Members' rights depend on patronage, and the articles may provide for these rights [verify].

### Section 378ZA. Transferability of shares and attached rights [verify heading]
(1) Shares are not transferable except to an active member, at par value, with the Board's previous approval [verify].

(2) A member must transfer his shares within three months of ceasing to be a member, or of becoming an inactive member [verify].

(3) The nominee of a deceased member may be admitted as a member, or the shares may be transferred at par value [verify].

### Section 378ZB. Special user rights [verify heading]
The articles may provide for issuing special user rights to members who are active members, with the approval of the Board. These rights entitle such members to supply produce or use the company's services, on terms specified in the articles [verify].

### Section 378ZC. Books of account [verify heading]
(1) Every Producer Company must keep proper books of account at its registered office, as required by section 128 [verify].

(2) The books of account must record [verify]:
- all sums received and spent;
- all sales and purchases;
- all assets and liabilities; and
- for a company engaged in production, processing or manufacturing, the use of materials, labour and other items of cost.

### Section 378ZD. Internal audit [verify heading]
Every Producer Company must have internal audit of its accounts carried out at such intervals and in such manner as the articles specify, by a chartered accountant [verify].

### Section 378ZE. Provisions relating to audit [verify heading]
The provisions of this Act on audit apply to a Producer Company. The auditor must also report on matters specific to Producer Companies [verify list], for example:
- the amount of debts due and bad debts;
- verification of cash balance and securities;
- loans to directors;
- donations or subscriptions;
- any transaction that appears contrary to the provisions of this Chapter; and
- any other matter considered necessary.

### Section 378ZF. Loans, etc., to members [verify heading]
Subject to the articles, the Board may provide financial assistance to members by way of [verify]:
- (a) credit facility to any member in connection with the company's business, for a period not exceeding six months; and
- (b) loans and advances, against the security specified in the articles, to any member, repayable within a period exceeding three months but not exceeding seven years from disbursement.

Loans to directors or their relatives require the members' approval in a general meeting [verify].

### Section 378ZG. Reserves and investment of general reserves [verify heading]
(1) Every Producer Company must maintain a general reserve in every financial year, in addition to any reserve maintained for specific purposes [verify].

(2) If the company does not have sufficient funds in any financial year for the general reserve, it may make a reserve in proportion to the supply of produce by members during that year, as the articles provide [verify].

(3) The company must invest its funds in the manner the articles specify, or deposit them in permitted securities and banks [verify].

### Section 378ZH. Investment in other companies, formation of subsidiaries, etc. [verify heading]
(1) A Producer Company may invest, by special resolution, in other companies, or form subsidiaries or joint ventures, for the purpose of promoting the objects of the Producer Company [verify].

(2) The aggregate of such investments, other than in Producer Companies or for the supply of produce, must not exceed thirty per cent of the aggregate of its paid-up capital and free reserves, without the approval of a special resolution [verify].

### Section 378ZI. Disposal of investments [verify heading]
The Board may, by resolution passed at its meeting, dispose of any investments made under section 378ZH, with the approval of the members where required [verify].

### Section 378ZJ. Investment of general reserves [verify heading]
The general reserves of a Producer Company are to be invested in a manner that secures their safety and liquidity. This includes deposits in banks and investments in Government securities or other permitted investments [verify].

### Section 378ZK. Issue of bonus shares [verify heading]
A Producer Company may, on the Board's recommendation and the members' approval in a general meeting, issue bonus shares by capitalising amounts from general reserves, in proportion to the shares held by members on the date of issue [verify].

### Section 378ZL. Donation or subscription by Producer Company [verify heading]
A Producer Company may, by special resolution, make donations or subscriptions to any institution or individual for:
- promoting the social and economic welfare of producer members, producers or the general public; or
- promoting mutual assistance principles.

The aggregate of such donations and subscriptions in any financial year must not exceed three per cent of the net profit of that year [verify].

### Section 378ZM. Restriction on amendment / political contributions [verify heading]
[verify: heading and content not reliably recalled. A Producer Company is understood to be prohibited from contributing directly or indirectly to any political party, or to any person for a political purpose.]

### Section 378ZN. Amalgamation, merger or division, etc., to form new Producer Companies [verify heading]
A Producer Company may, by special resolution and with the Tribunal's [verify] approval, amalgamate with other Producer Companies, merge, divide into two or more new Producer Companies, transfer its assets to another Producer Company, or form a new Producer Company [verify].

### Section 378ZO. Penalty for contravention [verify heading]
[verify: Where a Producer Company or its officers contravene provisions of this Chapter for which no specific penalty is provided, they are liable to a penalty, for example up to one lakh rupees, with a further amount for continuing contravention. Exact wording and amounts are not reliably recalled.]

### Section 378ZP. Disputes [verify heading]
(1) Disputes relating to the formation, management or business of a Producer Company are settled by conciliation, or by arbitration under the Arbitration and Conciliation Act, 1996, as if the parties had consented to arbitration. This covers disputes:
- among members, past members or persons claiming through members;
- between members and the Board or officers; and
- between the Board and any director or officer [verify].

(2) Such disputes are not to be referred to a civil court [verify].

### Section 378ZQ. Producer Company to be deemed to be a private company? / Re-conversion [verify heading]
[verify: heading and content not reliably recalled. The section is understood to provide for the reconversion of a Producer Company to an inter-State co-operative society, or for a Producer Company not becoming a public company.]

### Section 378ZR. [Heading not reliably recalled] [verify]

### Section 378ZU. Application of other provisions of this Act to Producer Companies [verify heading]
(1) Except as otherwise provided in this Chapter, the provisions of this Act on private limited companies apply to a Producer Company, so far as they are not inconsistent with this Chapter [verify].

(2) The Central Government may, by notification, direct that any provision of this Act does not apply to Producer Companies, or applies with such exceptions, modifications and adaptations as it may specify [verify].

## CHAPTER XXII — COMPANIES INCORPORATED OUTSIDE INDIA

### Section 379. Application of Act to foreign companies
This section applies to a foreign company where not less than fifty per cent of its paid-up share capital (equity, preference, or both) is held, singly or in aggregate, by any of the following:
- one or more citizens of India;
- one or more companies or bodies corporate incorporated in India; or
- one or more citizens of India together with one or more companies or bodies corporate incorporated in India.

Such a company must comply with this Chapter, and with such other provisions of this Act as may be prescribed, in respect of the business it carries on in India, as if it were a company incorporated in India.

### Section 380. Documents, etc., to be delivered to Registrar by foreign companies
(1) Within thirty days of establishing a place of business in India, every foreign company must deliver the following to the Registrar for registration:
- (a) a certified copy of its charter, statutes, or memorandum and articles, or other instrument constituting or defining its constitution. If the instrument is not in English, a certified English translation must also be delivered;
- (b) the full address of its registered or principal office;
- (c) a list of its directors and secretary, with the prescribed particulars;
- (d) the name(s) and address(es) of one or more persons resident in India who are authorised to accept service of process, notices or other documents on its behalf;
- (e) the full address of its office in India that is treated as its principal place of business in India;
- (f) particulars of any earlier opening and closing of a place of business in India;
- (g) a declaration that none of its directors, and not its authorised representative in India, has ever been convicted or debarred from formation of companies and management in India or abroad; and
- (h) any other prescribed information.

(2) A foreign company existing when this Act commenced, which had not already delivered the documents and particulars required by section 592(1) of the Companies Act, 1956, remains obliged to deliver them in accordance with that Act.

(3) If any document delivered under this section is altered, the foreign company must, within thirty days of the alteration, deliver to the Registrar a return in the prescribed form giving particulars of the alteration.

### Section 381. Accounts of foreign company
(1) In every calendar year, every foreign company must:
- (a) prepare a balance sheet and profit and loss account in the prescribed form, with the prescribed particulars and the prescribed documents annexed or attached; and
- (b) deliver a copy of those documents to the Registrar.

The Central Government may, by notification, direct that clause (a) does not apply to a particular foreign company or class of foreign companies, or applies with the exceptions and modifications specified in the notification.

(2) If any such document is not in English, a certified English translation must be annexed to it.

(3) With the documents under sub-section (1), every foreign company must send the Registrar a list, in the prescribed form, of all its places of business in India as at the balance sheet date.

### Section 382. Display of name, etc., of foreign company
Every foreign company must:
- (a) conspicuously display, on the outside of every office or place where it carries on business in India:
  - its name; and
  - the country in which it is incorporated,

  in letters easily legible in English, and also in the characters of the language, or one of the languages, in general use in that locality;
- (b) state its name and the country of its incorporation in legible English characters in all business letters, billheads, letter paper, notices and other official publications; and
- (c) if the liability of its members is limited, state that fact:
  - (i) in every prospectus it issues, and in all business letters, billheads, letter paper, notices, advertisements and other official publications, in legible English characters; and
  - (ii) conspicuously on the outside of every office or place where it carries on business in India, in legible English characters and in the characters of the language, or one of the languages, in general use in that locality.

### Section 383. Service on foreign company
Any process, notice or other document required to be served on a foreign company is sufficiently served if:
- it is addressed to any person whose name and address have been delivered to the Registrar under section 380; and
- it is left at, or sent by post to, the address so delivered, or sent by electronic mode.

### Section 384. Debentures, annual return, registration of charges, books of account and their inspection
(1) Section 71 (debentures) applies, with necessary changes, to a foreign company.

(2) Section 92 (annual return) applies to a foreign company as it applies to a company incorporated in India, subject to any exceptions, modifications and adaptations made by rules.

(3) Section 128 applies to a foreign company to the extent of requiring it to keep books of account at its principal place of business in India. The books must cover money received and spent, sales and purchases, and assets and liabilities, in the course of or in relation to its business in India.

(4) Chapter VI (registration of charges) applies, with necessary changes, to charges on properties created or acquired by a foreign company.

(5) Chapter XIV (inspection, inquiry and investigation) applies to the Indian business of a foreign company as it applies to a company incorporated in India.

### Section 385. Fee for registration of documents
The prescribed fee must be paid to the Registrar for registering any document that this Chapter requires to be registered.

### Section 386. Interpretation
For this Chapter:
- (a) "certified" means certified in the prescribed manner to be a true copy or a correct translation;
- (b) "director", in relation to a foreign company, includes any person in accordance with whose directions or instructions the company's Board of Directors is accustomed to act; and
- (c) "place of business" includes a share transfer or registration office.

### Section 387. Dating of prospectus and particulars to be contained therein
(1) No person may issue, circulate or distribute in India any prospectus offering securities of a company incorporated or to be incorporated outside India unless the conditions below are met. This applies whether or not the company has established, or will establish, a place of business in India. The prospectus must be dated and signed, and must:
- (a) contain particulars of:
  - (i) the instrument constituting or defining the company's constitution;
  - (ii) the enactments, or provisions having the force of an enactment, under which the company was incorporated;
  - (iii) an address in India where the instrument, enactments or provisions (or copies of them, with a certified English translation if not in English) can be inspected;
  - (iv) the date on which, and the country in which, the company was or would be incorporated; and
  - (v) whether the company has established a place of business in India and, if so, the address of its principal office in India; and
- (b) state the matters specified under section 26.

Sub-clauses (i), (ii) and (iii) of clause (a) do not apply to a prospectus issued more than two years after the date on which the company became entitled to commence business.

(2) The following conditions are void:
- any condition requiring or binding an applicant for securities to waive compliance with a requirement under sub-section (1); and
- any condition purporting to fix him with notice of any contract, document or matter not specifically referred to in the prospectus.

(3) No person may issue to anyone in India a form of application for securities of such a company unless:
- the form is issued with a prospectus that complies with this Chapter; and
- the issue does not contravene section 388.

This does not apply if the form was issued in connection with a bona fide invitation to a person to enter into an underwriting agreement for the securities.

(4) This section:
- (a) does not apply to the issue of a prospectus or application form to existing members or debenture holders of the company relating to its securities, whether or not the applicant has a right to renounce in favour of others; and
- (b) except for the requirement that a prospectus be dated, does not apply to a prospectus relating to securities that are, or are to be, in all respects uniform with securities previously issued and currently dealt in or quoted on a recognised stock exchange.

Subject to this, the section applies to a prospectus or application form whether issued on or with reference to the formation of a company or later.

(5) This section does not limit or reduce any liability a person may incur under the general law or under this Act apart from this section.

### Section 388. Provisions as to expert's consent and allotment
(1) No person may issue, circulate or distribute in India any prospectus offering securities of a company incorporated or to be incorporated outside India (whether or not it has or will establish a place of business in India) in either of these cases:
- (a) the prospectus includes a statement purporting to be made by an expert, and:
  - the expert has not given his written consent to the issue of the prospectus with the statement included in its form and context, or has withdrawn that consent before the prospectus is delivered for registration; or
  - the prospectus does not state that he has given, and has not withdrawn, that consent; or
- (b) the prospectus does not have the effect, where an application is made under it, of making all persons concerned bound by sections 33 and 40, so far as applicable.

(2) For this section, a statement is treated as included in a prospectus if it is contained in any report or memorandum appearing on its face, incorporated in it by reference, or issued with it.

### Section 389. Registration of prospectus
No person may issue, circulate or distribute in India any prospectus offering securities of a company incorporated or to be incorporated outside India (whether or not it has or will establish a place of business in India) unless all of the following conditions are met before the prospectus is issued, circulated or distributed in India:
- a copy of it has been delivered to the Registrar for registration;
- the copy is certified by the chairperson and two other directors as having been approved by a resolution of the managing body;
- the prospectus states on its face that a copy has been so delivered; and
- the copy delivered has endorsed on or attached to it any consent required by section 388 and the prescribed documents.

### Section 390. Offer of Indian Depository Receipts
Notwithstanding any other law, the Central Government may make rules for the following, in respect of Indian Depository Receipts issued by a company incorporated or to be incorporated outside India (whether or not it has or will establish a place of business in India):
- (a) the offer of Indian Depository Receipts;
- (b) the disclosures required in the prospectus or letter of offer issued in connection with them;
- (c) the manner in which they are dealt with in a depository mode, and by custodians and underwriters; and
- (d) the manner of their sale, transfer or transmission.

### Section 391. Application of sections 34 to 36 and Chapter XX
(1) Sections 34 to 36 (both inclusive) apply to:
- (i) the issue of a prospectus by a company incorporated outside India under section 389, as they apply to a prospectus issued by an Indian company; and
- (ii) the issue of Indian Depository Receipts by a foreign company.

(2) Subject to section 376, Chapter XX applies, with necessary changes, to the closure of a foreign company's place of business in India, as if it were a company incorporated in India.

### Section 392. Punishment for contravention
(As substituted by the Companies (Amendment) Act, 2020 [verify].) If a foreign company contravenes this Chapter, the following penalties apply, without prejudice to section 391 [verify all amounts]:
