(3) Rules under this section may provide that a contravention of them is punishable with a fine up to five thousand rupees, and for a continuing contravention, a further fine up to five hundred rupees for every day after the first that the contravention continues [verify: possibly converted to a "penalty" by the Companies (Amendment) Act, 2020].

(4) Every rule made under this Act, and every regulation made by the Securities and Exchange Board under this Act, must be laid before each House of Parliament, while it is in session, for a total of thirty days. The thirty days may fall in one session or in two or more successive sessions. If, before the end of the session immediately following, both Houses agree to modify the rule or regulation, or agree that it should not be made, it thereafter has effect only in the modified form, or has no effect, as the case may be. Any such modification or annulment does not affect the validity of anything previously done under it.

### Section 470. Power to remove difficulties
(1) If any difficulty arises in giving effect to this Act, the Central Government may, by order published in the Official Gazette, make provisions that are not inconsistent with this Act and that appear to it necessary or expedient to remove the difficulty. No such order may be made after five years from the commencement of this Act.

(2) Every order under this section must be laid before each House of Parliament as soon as may be after it is made.

## SCHEDULE I
(See sections 4 and 5.) Model forms of memorandum and articles of association.

### Tables (memorandum: A to E; articles: F to J) [verify ordering]
- Table A — Memorandum of association of a company limited by shares.
- Table B — Memorandum of association of a company limited by guarantee and not having a share capital.
- Table C — Memorandum and articles of association of a company limited by guarantee and having a share capital [verify].
- Table D — Memorandum and articles of association of an unlimited company not having share capital [verify].
- Table E — Memorandum and articles of association of an unlimited company having share capital [verify].
- Table F — Articles of association of a company limited by shares.
- Table G — Articles of association of a company limited by guarantee and having a share capital.
- Table H — Articles of association of a company limited by guarantee and not having share capital.
- Table I — Articles of association of an unlimited company having share capital.
- Table J — Articles of association of an unlimited company not having share capital.

### Memorandum tables (A to E) — standard clauses
Each memorandum table contains:
- the name clause (ending with "Limited" or "Private Limited", as applicable);
- the registered office clause (the State);
- the objects clause (the objects to be pursued on incorporation, and matters considered necessary in furtherance of them);
- the liability clause (limited by shares, limited by guarantee, or unlimited);
- the capital clause, where applicable (amount and division into shares);
- for guarantee companies, the amount each member undertakes to contribute on winding up, while a member or within one year after ceasing to be a member;
- the association/subscription clause, with the subscribers' names, addresses, descriptions, occupations and number of shares taken; and
- for a One Person Company, the nominee clause.

Each is signed by the subscribers and witnessed.

### Table F — Articles of association of a company limited by shares (summary of headings and key regulations)
Interpretation:
- "the Act" means the Companies Act, 2013;
- "the seal" means the common seal of the company, if any; and
- unless the context requires otherwise, words and expressions in the regulations have the same meaning as in the Act.

Share capital and variation of rights:
- Shares are under the control of the Board, which may allot them on such terms as it thinks fit.
- Every member is entitled to a share certificate:
  - within two months after incorporation, for subscriber shares; or
  - within one month after an application for registration of transfer or transmission, or within such other period as the conditions of issue provide [verify].
- Certificates are issued under seal (if any), and signed by two directors, or by a director and the company secretary, where one has been appointed [verify].
- Certificates may be renewed on surrender if defaced or worn out, or replaced on proof of loss and indemnity.
- The company may pay commission on subscription subject to section 40(6) and the rules.
- The rights attached to a class of shares may be varied with the written consent of the holders of three-fourths of the issued shares of that class, or by a special resolution passed at a separate class meeting.
- Preference shares may be issued, including redeemable preference shares, on terms decided by special resolution.

Lien:
- The company has a first and paramount lien on every share (not fully paid) for all money called or payable at a fixed time in respect of that share, and on dividends.
- The company may sell shares under lien after fourteen days' notice demanding payment of the amount presently payable [verify].

Calls on shares:
- The Board may make calls. No call may exceed one-fourth of the nominal value of the share, or be payable less than one month from the date fixed for payment of the previous call [verify].
- Each member must receive at least fourteen days' notice of a call.
- Interest is payable on unpaid calls at the rate fixed by the Board.
- The Board may receive advance payment of calls.

Transfer of shares:
- The instrument of transfer is executed by or on behalf of both transferor and transferee.
- The Board may decline to register the transfer of a share that is not fully paid to a person of whom it does not approve, or of shares on which the company has a lien.
- Registration of transfers may be suspended for up to thirty days at a time, and forty-five days in a year.

Transmission of shares:
- On the death of a member, the survivor(s) (for joint holders) or the nominee or legal representatives are the only persons recognised as having title to his shares.
- A person entitled by transmission may elect to be registered himself or to transfer the shares.

Forfeiture of shares:
- If a call or instalment is unpaid, the Board may serve a notice requiring payment within at least fourteen days, and may forfeit the shares if the notice is not complied with.
- A person whose shares are forfeited ceases to be a member, but remains liable for the money payable at the date of forfeiture.

Alteration of capital:
- By ordinary resolution, the company may:
  - increase its share capital;
  - consolidate shares;
  - convert shares into stock and back;
  - sub-divide shares; and
  - cancel unsubscribed shares.
- The company may reduce capital by special resolution in the manner authorised by law.

Capitalisation of profits:
- On the Board's recommendation, the company in general meeting may capitalise any sum standing to the credit of reserves or the profit and loss account, and apply it in paying up unissued shares (bonus shares) or amounts unpaid on shares.
- The securities premium account and the capital redemption reserve account may be applied only to paying up unissued shares as fully paid bonus shares.

Buy-back of shares:
- Buy-back is permitted subject to sections 68 to 70 and other applicable provisions.

General meetings:
- All general meetings other than the annual general meeting are extraordinary general meetings.
- The Board may call an extraordinary general meeting whenever it thinks fit.

Proceedings at general meetings:
- The quorum is as provided in section 103.
- The chairperson of the Board presides. Failing him, the directors present elect one of themselves; failing that, the members elect one of themselves.

Adjournment of meeting:
- The chairperson may adjourn the meeting with the consent of a meeting at which a quorum is present.
- Notice is required if a meeting is adjourned for thirty days or more.

Voting rights:
- On a show of hands, every member present has one vote.
- On a poll, voting rights are in proportion to the member's share in the paid-up equity capital.
- Members may vote by electronic means as provided in section 108.
- No member may vote while calls or other sums presently payable by him remain unpaid.

Proxy:
- The instrument appointing a proxy must be deposited at least forty-eight hours before the meeting, in the form prescribed (Form MGT-11).

Board of Directors:
- The first directors are named in the articles [verify].
- Directors' remuneration is treated as accruing from day to day.
- The Board may appoint additional directors, subject to the maximum strength fixed by the articles.

Proceedings of the Board:
- The Board may meet, adjourn and regulate its meetings as it thinks fit.
- Questions are decided by a majority of votes; the chairperson has a casting vote.
- The Board may delegate powers to committees.
- A written resolution signed by all members of the Board or committee entitled to receive notice is as valid as one passed at a meeting.

Chief Executive Officer, Manager, Company Secretary or Chief Financial Officer:
- The Board may appoint them for such term, at such remuneration and on such conditions as it thinks fit.
- A director may be appointed as any of them.

The Seal:
- The Board provides for safe custody of the seal, if any.
- The seal is affixed only by the authority of the Board or a committee, in the presence of at least two directors, or one director and the secretary or other authorised person.

Dividends and Reserve:
- The company in general meeting may declare dividends, but not exceeding the amount recommended by the Board.
- The Board may pay interim dividends.
- The Board may set aside reserves before recommending a dividend.
- Dividends are paid in proportion to the amounts paid up on shares.
- No dividend bears interest against the company.

Accounts:
- The Board decides whether, to what extent, and at what times and places the accounts and books are open to inspection by members not being directors, subject to the Act.

Winding up:
- Subject to Chapter XX of the Act, with the sanction of a special resolution, the liquidator may divide the assets among members in specie or kind, and may vest assets in trustees on trust for the benefit of contributories.

Indemnity:
- Every officer of the company is indemnified out of the company's assets against any liability incurred by him in defending any proceedings, civil or criminal, in which judgment is given in his favour or in which he is acquitted, or in which the Tribunal grants him relief.

### Tables G to J
- These adapt Table F for companies limited by guarantee (with or without share capital) and for unlimited companies (with or without share capital).
- They include provisions on:
  - the number of members (as registered, which the Board may increase);
  - membership being confined to persons admitted by the Board;
  - the guarantee amount; and
  - for unlimited companies, the absence of limit on members' liability [verify].

## SCHEDULE II
(See section 123.) Useful lives to compute depreciation.

### Part A
(1) Depreciation is the systematic allocation of the depreciable amount of an asset over its useful life:
- The depreciable amount of an asset is its cost, or another amount substituted for cost, less its residual value.
- The useful life of an asset is the period over which the asset is expected to be available for use by an entity, or the number of production or similar units the entity expects to obtain from it.

(2) For this Schedule, "depreciation" includes amortisation.

(3) Without prejudice to paragraph (1):
- (i) the useful life of an asset should not ordinarily differ from the useful life specified in Part C, and the residual value should not be more than five per cent of the original cost of the asset. Where a company uses a useful life or residual value different from these limits, it must disclose the justification in its financial statements [verify: post-2014 amendment wording];
- (ii) for intangible assets, the provisions of the applicable Accounting Standards apply. For intangible assets (toll roads) created under "Build, Operate and Transfer", "Build, Own, Operate and Transfer" or any other form of public private partnership route, amortisation may be done using a revenue-based method [verify details].

### Part B
The useful life or residual value of any specific asset notified for accounting purposes by a Regulatory Authority constituted under an Act of Parliament, or by the Central Government, is applied in calculating depreciation for that asset, irrespective of the requirements of this Schedule.

### Part C — Useful lives (years) for single-shift working [verify all figures]
1. Buildings:
   - (a) buildings (other than factory buildings) with RCC frame structure — 60;
   - (b) buildings (other than factory buildings) other than RCC frame structure — 30;
   - (c) factory buildings — 30;
   - (d) fences, wells, tube wells — 5;
   - (e) others, including temporary structures — 3.
2. Bridges, culverts, bunders, etc. — 30.
3. Roads:
   - (a) carpeted roads (RCC) — 10;
   - (b) carpeted roads (other than RCC) — 5;
   - (c) non-carpeted roads — 3.
4. Plant and machinery:
   - (i) general rate, for plant and machinery not covered by specific industries — 15;
   - (ii) continuous process plant for which no special rate is prescribed — 25 [verify];
   - (iii) plant and machinery of specific industries — as listed. The list covers, among others: cinematograph films, electrical machinery, glass, mines and quarries, nuclear power, petroleum refineries, power generation, steel, telecommunications, textiles, sugar, and medical and surgical equipment (13 years) [verify].
5. Furniture and fittings:
   - (i) general furniture and fittings — 10;
   - (ii) furniture and fittings used in hotels, restaurants and boarding houses, schools, colleges and other educational institutions, libraries, welfare centres, meeting halls, cinema houses, theatres and circuses, and furniture let out on hire for use on the occasion of marriages and similar functions — 8.
6. Motor vehicles:
   - (i) motor cycles, scooters and other mopeds — 10;
   - (ii) motor buses, motor lorries, motor cars and motor taxis used in a business of running them on hire — 6;
   - (iii) motor buses, motor lorries and motor cars other than those used in a business of running them on hire — 8;
   - (iv) motor tractors, harvesting combines and heavy vehicles — 8;
   - (v) electrically operated vehicles, including battery-powered or fuel cell-powered vehicles — 8.
7. Ships:
   - ocean-going ships, including bulk carriers, liner vessels and tankers — 20 (other categories 25 or 13, as listed);
   - vessels ordinarily operating on inland waters — 28 for speed boats, 13 for other vessels [verify].
8. Aircraft or helicopters — 20.
9. Railway sidings, locomotives, rolling stocks, tramways and railways used by concerns, excluding railway concerns — 15.
10. Ropeway structures — 15.
11. Office equipment — 5.
12. Computers and data processing units:
    - (i) servers and networks — 6;
    - (ii) end user devices, such as desktops, laptops, etc. — 3.
13. Laboratory equipment:
    - (i) general laboratory equipment — 10;
    - (ii) laboratory equipment used in educational institutions — 5.
14. Electrical installations and equipment — 10.
15. Hydraulic works, pipelines and sluices — 15.

### Notes [verify]
1. "Factory buildings" does not include offices, godowns, staff quarters.
2. Where an asset is added, sold, discarded, demolished or destroyed during a financial year, depreciation on it is calculated on a pro rata basis:
   - from the date of addition; or
   - up to the date of sale, discard, demolition or destruction.
3. The following must be disclosed in the accounts:
   - (i) the depreciation methods used; and
   - (ii) the useful lives of assets used for computing depreciation, if they differ from those specified in this Schedule.
4. The useful lives are for the whole of an asset. Where the cost of a part of an asset is significant to its total cost, and the useful life of that part differs from the useful life of the remaining asset, the useful life of that significant part is determined separately.
5. The useful life is specified for single-shift working. For any period during which an asset is used for double shift, depreciation increases by 50%; for triple shift, by 100%. This does not apply to assets marked "NESD" (no extra shift depreciation).
6. From the date this Schedule comes into effect, the carrying amount of an asset as on that date:
   - (a) is depreciated over the remaining useful life of the asset as per this Schedule; and
   - (b) after retaining the residual value, is recognised in the opening balance of retained earnings where the remaining useful life of the asset is nil.
7. "Continuous process plant" means a plant required and designed to operate twenty-four hours a day.

## SCHEDULE III
(See section 129.) General instructions for preparation of balance sheet and statement of profit and loss of a company.

### Structure
- Division I applies to companies whose financial statements must comply with the Companies (Accounting Standards) Rules, 2021 (earlier, the 2006 Rules).
- Division II applies to companies whose financial statements must comply with the Companies (Indian Accounting Standards) Rules, 2015 (Ind AS), other than NBFCs.
- Division III applies to Non-Banking Financial Companies complying with Ind AS. (Inserted by notification of 11-10-2018 [verify].)

### General instructions (summary)
- Every company must prepare its balance sheet, statement of profit and loss and notes in the relevant Division's form, as near thereto as circumstances admit. Where compliance with the Accounting Standards requires a change in treatment or disclosure, the Accounting Standards prevail.
- Figures may be rounded off depending on total income:
  - less than one hundred crore rupees — to the nearest hundreds, thousands, lakhs or millions, or decimals thereof;
  - one hundred crore rupees or more — to the nearest lakhs, millions or crores, or decimals thereof.

  The unit of measurement must be used uniformly.
- Corresponding amounts for the immediately preceding reporting period must be given for all items, including notes.
- The disclosure requirements are in addition to those under the Accounting Standards and the Act.

### Division I — Balance sheet format (headings)
Equity and liabilities:
- (1) Shareholders' funds:
  - (a) share capital;
  - (b) reserves and surplus;
  - (c) money received against share warrants.
- (2) Share application money pending allotment.
- (3) Non-current liabilities:
  - (a) long-term borrowings;
  - (b) deferred tax liabilities (net);
  - (c) other long-term liabilities;
  - (d) long-term provisions.
- (4) Current liabilities:
  - (a) short-term borrowings;
  - (b) trade payables — (A) total outstanding dues of micro enterprises and small enterprises; (B) total outstanding dues of creditors other than micro enterprises and small enterprises;
  - (c) other current liabilities;
  - (d) short-term provisions.

Assets:
- (1) Non-current assets:
  - (a) property, plant and equipment and intangible assets — (i) property, plant and equipment; (ii) intangible assets; (iii) capital work-in-progress; (iv) intangible assets under development;
  - (b) non-current investments;
  - (c) deferred tax assets (net);
  - (d) long-term loans and advances;
  - (e) other non-current assets.
- (2) Current assets:
  - (a) current investments;
  - (b) inventories;
  - (c) trade receivables;
  - (d) cash and cash equivalents;
  - (e) short-term loans and advances;
  - (f) other current assets.

### Division I — Statement of profit and loss format (headings)
- I. Revenue from operations
- II. Other income
- III. Total income (I + II)
- IV. Expenses:
  - cost of materials consumed;
  - purchases of stock-in-trade;
  - changes in inventories of finished goods, work-in-progress and stock-in-trade;
  - employee benefits expense;
  - finance costs;
  - depreciation and amortisation expense;
  - other expenses.
- V. Profit before exceptional and extraordinary items and tax
- VI. Exceptional items
- VII. Profit before extraordinary items and tax
- VIII. Extraordinary items
- IX. Profit before tax
- X. Tax expense (current tax; deferred tax)
- XI. Profit (loss) for the period from continuing operations
- XII–XIV. Discontinuing operations (profit or loss, tax expense, and profit or loss after tax)
- XV. Profit (loss) for the period
- XVI. Earnings per equity share (basic; diluted)

### Division II (Ind AS) — key differences
- The balance sheet is presented as assets, then equity and liabilities.
- "Equity" consists of equity share capital and other equity.
- Financial assets and financial liabilities are shown separately.
- The statement of profit and loss includes "Other comprehensive income" (items that will not be reclassified, and items that will be reclassified, to profit or loss) and "Total comprehensive income".
- A statement of changes in equity is required.

### Additional regulatory information (inserted by notification dated 24-03-2021, effective for financial years commencing on or after 1-04-2021 [verify])
- Shareholding of promoters at the end of the year, and the percentage change during the year.
- Ageing schedules for trade payables, trade receivables, capital work-in-progress and intangible assets under development.
- Title deeds of immovable property not held in the name of the company.
- Whether property, plant and equipment or intangible assets have been revalued, and whether by a registered valuer.
- Loans or advances to promoters, directors, key managerial personnel and related parties that are repayable on demand or without specifying terms or period of repayment.
- Details of any benami property held, and proceedings under the Benami Transactions (Prohibition) Act, 1988.
- Borrowings from banks or financial institutions on the security of current assets: whether quarterly returns or statements agree with the books of account.
- Declaration as a wilful defaulter by any bank, financial institution or other lender.
- Relationship with, and transactions with, companies struck off under section 248 of the Act or section 560 of the Companies Act, 1956.
- Registration of charges or satisfaction with the Registrar still pending beyond the statutory period.
- Compliance with the number of layers prescribed under section 2(87) of the Act.
- Analytical ratios, with explanation for any change of more than 25% compared with the preceding year:
  - current ratio;
  - debt-equity ratio;
  - debt service coverage ratio;
  - return on equity;
  - inventory turnover;
  - trade receivables turnover;
  - trade payables turnover;
  - net capital turnover;
  - net profit ratio;
  - return on capital employed;
  - return on investment.
- Compliance with approved schemes of arrangement under sections 230 to 237.
- Utilisation of borrowed funds and share premium: advances, loans or investments through intermediaries for ultimate beneficiaries, with disclosures.
- Statement of profit and loss — undisclosed income surrendered or disclosed in tax assessments.
- Statement of profit and loss — Corporate Social Responsibility details, where section 135 applies:
  - the amount required to be spent;
  - the amount spent;
  - any shortfall and the reason for it;
  - the total of previous years' shortfall;
  - the nature of CSR activities;
  - related party transactions; and
  - movements in provisions.
- Statement of profit and loss — details of crypto currency or virtual currency traded or invested in, profit or loss on such transactions, amount held, and deposits or advances received for trading or investing in it.

## SCHEDULE IV
(See section 149(8).) Code for Independent Directors.

The Code is a guide to professional conduct for independent directors. Faithful adherence to these standards by independent directors fulfils their responsibilities in a professional and faithful manner, and promotes the confidence of the investment community, particularly minority shareholders, regulators and companies, in the institution of independent directors.

### I. Guidelines of professional conduct
An independent director must:
- (1) uphold ethical standards of integrity and probity;
- (2) act objectively and constructively while exercising his duties;
- (3) exercise his responsibilities in a bona fide manner in the interest of the company;
- (4) devote sufficient time and attention to his professional obligations, for informed and balanced decision making;
- (5) not allow any extraneous considerations to vitiate his objective, independent judgment in the paramount interest of the company as a whole, whether he concurs in or dissents from the collective judgment of the Board;
- (6) not abuse his position to the detriment of the company or its shareholders, or to gain any direct or indirect personal advantage, or advantage for any associated person;
- (7) refrain from any action that would lead to loss of his independence;
- (8) where circumstances arise that make him lose his independence, immediately inform the Board accordingly;
- (9) assist the company in implementing the best corporate governance practices.

### II. Role and functions
Independent directors must:
- (1) help bring an independent judgment to bear on the Board's deliberations, especially on issues of strategy, performance, risk management, resources, key appointments and standards of conduct;
- (2) bring an objective view to the evaluation of the performance of the Board and management;
- (3) scrutinise the performance of management in meeting agreed goals and objectives, and monitor the reporting of performance;
- (4) satisfy themselves on the integrity of financial information, and that financial controls and risk management systems are robust and defensible;
- (5) safeguard the interests of all stakeholders, particularly minority shareholders;
- (6) balance the conflicting interests of stakeholders;
- (7) determine appropriate levels of remuneration for executive directors, key managerial personnel and senior management, and have a prime role in appointing them and, where necessary, recommending their removal;
- (8) moderate and arbitrate in the interest of the company as a whole, in situations of conflict between management and shareholders' interests.

### III. Duties
Independent directors must:
- (1) undertake appropriate induction, and regularly update and refresh their skills, knowledge and familiarity with the company;
- (2) seek appropriate clarification or amplification of information and, where necessary, take and follow appropriate professional advice and the opinion of outside experts at the company's expense;
- (3) strive to attend all meetings of the Board and of the Board committees of which they are members;
- (4) participate constructively and actively in the Board committees they chair or are members of;
- (5) strive to attend the company's general meetings;
- (6) where they have concerns about the running of the company or a proposed action, ensure that these are addressed by the Board. To the extent they are not resolved, insist that their concerns are recorded in the minutes of the Board meeting;
- (7) keep themselves well informed about the company and the external environment in which it operates;
- (8) not unfairly obstruct the functioning of an otherwise proper Board or committee;
- (9) pay sufficient attention to related party transactions, and ensure adequate deliberations are held before approving them, and assure themselves that they are in the interest of the company;
- (10) ascertain and ensure that the company has an adequate and functional vigil mechanism, and that the interests of a person who uses it are not prejudicially affected by such use;
- (11) report concerns about unethical behaviour, actual or suspected fraud, or violation of the company's code of conduct or ethics policy;
- (12) acting within their authority, assist in protecting the legitimate interests of the company, its shareholders and its employees;
- (13) not disclose confidential information, including commercial secrets, technologies, advertising and sales promotion plans, and unpublished price sensitive information, unless the disclosure is expressly approved by the Board or required by law.

### IV. Manner of appointment
- (1) The appointment process for independent directors must be independent of the company management. When selecting independent directors, the Board must ensure an appropriate balance of skills, experience and knowledge on the Board, to enable it to discharge its functions and duties effectively.
- (2) The appointment must be approved at a meeting of the shareholders.
- (3) The explanatory statement attached to the notice of the meeting approving the appointment must:
  - include a statement that, in the Board's opinion, the proposed independent director fulfils the conditions specified in the Act and the rules, and is independent of the management; and
  - [verify: as amended, also state the justification for choosing the appointee, in terms of skills and capabilities].
- (4) The appointment must be formalised through a letter of appointment, which sets out:
  - (a) the term of appointment;
  - (b) the Board's expectations of the appointed director, the Board-level committee(s) in which he is expected to serve, and its tasks;
  - (c) the fiduciary duties that come with the appointment, along with accompanying liabilities;
  - (d) provision for Directors' and Officers' (D and O) insurance, if any;
  - (e) the Code of Business Ethics that the company expects its directors and employees to follow;
  - (f) the list of actions a director should not do while functioning as such in the company; and
  - (g) the remuneration, including sitting fees, reimbursement of expenses for participation in Board and other meetings, and profit-related commission, if any.
- (5) The terms and conditions of appointment must be open for inspection by any member at the registered office during normal business hours.
- (6) The terms and conditions of appointment must also be posted on the company's website.

### V. Re-appointment
Re-appointment of independent directors is on the basis of the report of performance evaluation.

### VI. Resignation or removal
- (1) Resignation or removal of an independent director is in the same manner as provided in sections 168 and 169 of the Act.
- (2) An independent director who resigns or is removed must be replaced by a new independent director within three months from the date of resignation or removal [verify: the period may be one hundred and eighty days].
- (3) Where the company fulfils the requirement of independent directors on its Board even without filling the vacancy, the requirement of replacement does not apply.

### VII. Separate meetings
- (1) The independent directors of the company must hold at least one meeting in a financial year, without the attendance of non-independent directors and members of management.
- (2) All the independent directors should strive to be present at this meeting.
- (3) The meeting must:
  - (a) review the performance of non-independent directors and of the Board as a whole;
  - (b) review the performance of the Chairperson of the company, taking into account the views of executive and non-executive directors;
  - (c) assess the quality, quantity and timeliness of the flow of information between the company management and the Board that is necessary for the Board to perform its duties effectively and reasonably.

### VIII. Evaluation mechanism
- (1) The performance evaluation of independent directors is done by the entire Board of Directors, excluding the director being evaluated.
- (2) On the basis of the report of performance evaluation, it is determined whether to extend or continue the term of appointment of the independent director.

(Certain paragraphs have been exempted or modified for Government companies and for specified private and unlisted companies by exemption notifications under section 462 [verify].)

## SCHEDULE V
(See sections 196 and 197.) Conditions to be fulfilled for appointment of a managing or whole-time director or manager without the approval of the Central Government, and remuneration.

### PART I — Appointments
A person is eligible for appointment as a managing or whole-time director or manager (managerial person) only if he meets the following conditions:
- (a) he has not been sentenced to imprisonment for any period, or to a fine exceeding one thousand rupees, for an offence under any of the following Acts:
  - (i) the Indian Stamp Act, 1899;
  - (ii) the Central Excise Act, 1944;
  - (iii) the Industries (Development and Regulation) Act, 1951;
  - (iv) the Prevention of Food Adulteration Act, 1954;
  - (v) the Essential Commodities Act, 1955;
  - (vi) the Companies Act, 2013;
  - (vii) the Securities Contracts (Regulation) Act, 1956;
  - (viii) the Wealth-tax Act, 1957;
  - (ix) the Income-tax Act, 1961;
  - (x) the Customs Act, 1962;
  - (xi) the Competition Act, 2002;
  - (xii) the Foreign Exchange Management Act, 1999;
  - (xiii) the Sick Industrial Companies (Special Provisions) Act, 1985;
  - (xiv) the Securities and Exchange Board of India Act, 1992;
  - (xv) the Foreign Trade (Development and Regulation) Act, 1992;
  - (xvi) the Prevention of Money-Laundering Act, 2002 [verify list];
- (b) he has not been detained for any period under the Conservation of Foreign Exchange and Prevention of Smuggling Activities Act, 1974. Where the Central Government has approved the appointment of a person convicted or detained under (a) or (b), no further approval is needed for his subsequent appointment, unless he has been so convicted or detained after that approval;
- (c) he has completed twenty-one years of age and has not attained seventy years of age. A person who has attained seventy years may be appointed if the appointment is approved by a special resolution, under section 196(3) [verify];
- (d) where he is a managerial person in more than one company, he draws remuneration from one or more companies subject to the ceiling in Section V of Part II;
- (e) he is resident in India.

Explanation I: For this Schedule, "resident in India" includes a person who has been staying in India for a continuous period of not less than twelve months immediately before the date of his appointment as a managerial person, and who has come to stay in India:
- (i) for taking up employment in India; or
- (ii) for carrying on a business or vocation in India.

(The period was reduced from twelve months; the Companies Amendment notified in 2019 changed it to "not less than twelve months" [verify]; an earlier version referred to "one hundred and eighty-two days" [verify].)

Explanation II: The residence condition does not apply to companies in Special Economic Zones, as notified by the Department of Commerce from time to time. A non-resident person may enter India only after obtaining a proper Employment Visa from the Indian mission concerned abroad. For this, he must furnish, with the visa application form, the profile of the company, the principal employer, and the terms and conditions of his appointment.

### PART II — Remuneration

#### Section I — Remuneration payable by companies having profits
Subject to section 197, a company having profits in a financial year may pay remuneration to a managerial person or persons not exceeding the limits specified in that section.

#### Section II — Remuneration payable by companies having no profit or inadequate profit without Central Government approval
(As amended by notification of 12-09-2016 and 18-03-2021 [verify].) Where in any financial year during the tenure of a managerial person or other director, a company has no profits or its profits are inadequate, it may pay remuneration without Central Government approval, not exceeding the higher of the limits under (A) and (B):

(A) Effective capital-based limits (yearly remuneration):

| Effective capital | Managerial person | Other director (non-executive/independent) |
|---|---|---|
| (i) Negative or less than 5 crores | Rs. 60 lakhs | Rs. 12 lakhs |
| (ii) 5 crores and above but less than 100 crores | Rs. 84 lakhs | Rs. 17 lakhs |
| (iii) 100 crores and above but less than 250 crores | Rs. 120 lakhs | Rs. 24 lakhs |
| (iv) 250 crores and above | Rs. 120 lakhs plus 0.01% of effective capital in excess of Rs. 250 crores | Rs. 24 lakhs plus 0.01% of effective capital in excess of Rs. 250 crores |

[verify all figures]

- These limits are doubled if the members pass a special resolution.
- For a period of less than one year, the limits are pro-rated.

(B) Professional managerial persons: the limit in (A) may be exceeded for a managerial person functioning in a professional capacity who meets all of these conditions:
- he has no interest in the capital of the company, its holding company or any of its subsidiaries, directly, indirectly, or through any other statutory structure;
- he has no direct or indirect interest in, and is not related to, the directors or promoters of the company, its holding company or any of its subsidiaries, at any time during the last two years before, on, or after the date of appointment; and
- he holds a graduate-level qualification, with expertise and specialised knowledge in the field in which the company operates.

An employee holding shares of the company not exceeding 0.5% of its paid-up share capital, under any scheme for allotment of shares to employees (including an Employees' Stock Option Plan) or by purchase from the market, is treated as having no interest in the company's capital.

Conditions for payment under (A) or (B):
- (i) the payment of remuneration is approved by a resolution of the Board and, for a company covered by section 178(1), also by the Nomination and Remuneration Committee;
- (ii) the company has not defaulted in repaying any of its debts (including public deposits), debentures, or interest on them, for a continuous period of thirty days in the preceding financial year before the date of appointment. If there has been such a default, the company must obtain the prior approval of its secured creditors for the proposed remuneration. The fact of that approval must be stated in the explanatory statement to the notice of the general meeting [verify: post-2017 wording referring to lenders];
- (iii) an ordinary or special resolution, as applicable, has been passed at the general meeting for payment of remuneration for a period not exceeding three years;
- (iv) a statement with the following information is sent with the notice calling the general meeting:
  - I. General information:
    - (1) nature of industry;
    - (2) date or expected date of commencement of commercial production;
    - (3) for new companies, the expected date of commencement of activities as per the project approved by financial institutions appearing in the prospectus;
    - (4) financial performance based on given indicators;
    - (5) foreign investments or collaborations, if any.
  - II. Information about the appointee:
    - (1) background details;
    - (2) past remuneration;
    - (3) recognition or awards;
    - (4) job profile and his suitability;
    - (5) remuneration proposed;
    - (6) comparative remuneration profile with respect to industry, size of the company, and profile of the position and person (for expatriates, with reference to the country of his origin);
    - (7) pecuniary relationship, directly or indirectly, with the company, or relationship with the managerial personnel or other director, if any.
  - III. Other information:
    - (1) reasons for loss or inadequate profits;
    - (2) steps taken or proposed to be taken for improvement;
    - (3) expected increase in productivity and profits in measurable terms.
  - IV. Disclosures, to be included in the Board's report under the heading "Corporate Governance", if any, attached to the financial statement:
    - (i) all elements of the remuneration package of all directors, such as salary, benefits, bonuses, stock options and pension;
    - (ii) details of the fixed component and performance-linked incentives, with the performance criteria;
    - (iii) service contracts, notice period and severance fees;
    - (iv) stock option details, if any, including whether issued at a discount, and the period over which accrued and exercisable.

#### Section III — Remuneration payable by companies having no profit or inadequate profit without Central Government approval in certain special circumstances
In the following circumstances, a company may pay remuneration to a managerial person in excess of the amounts in Section II, without Central Government approval:
- (a) the remuneration in excess of those limits is paid by another company, and that other company:
  - is a foreign company, or has obtained its shareholders' approval in a general meeting for the payment;
  - treats the amount as managerial remuneration for the purposes of section 197; and
  - keeps its total managerial remuneration, including such amount, within the permissible limits under section 197;
- (b) where the company is:
  - (i) a newly incorporated company, for seven years from the date of its incorporation; or
  - (ii) a sick company for which a scheme of revival or rehabilitation has been ordered by the Board for Industrial and Financial Reconstruction or the National Company Law Tribunal, for five years from the date of sanction of the scheme,

  it may pay remuneration up to two times the amount permissible under Section II;
- (c) the remuneration of a managerial person exceeds the limits in Section II, but has been fixed by the Board for Industrial and Financial Reconstruction or the National Company Law Tribunal.

Proviso: The limits under this Section apply only if all the conditions in Section II are met, together with these additional conditions:
- (i) except as provided in paragraph (a), the managerial person is not receiving remuneration from any other company;
- (ii) the auditor or Company Secretary of the company certifies that all secured creditors and term lenders have stated in writing that they have no objection to the appointment of the managerial person and to the quantum of remuneration. Where the company has not appointed a Secretary, a Secretary in whole-time practice gives the certificate. The certificate is filed with the return under section 196(4);
- (iii) the auditor or Company Secretary (or, where no Secretary is appointed, a Secretary in whole-time practice) certifies that there is no default on payments to any creditors, and that all dues to deposit holders are being settled on time.

- (d) A company in a Special Economic Zone, as notified by the Department of Commerce from time to time, may pay remuneration up to two crore forty lakh rupees per annum, if:
  - it has not raised any money by public issue of shares or debentures in India; and
  - it has not defaulted in India in repaying any of its debts (including public deposits), debentures, or interest on them, for a continuous period of thirty days in any financial year.

#### Section IV — Perquisites not included in managerial remuneration
(1) A managerial person is eligible for the following perquisites, which are not included in computing the ceiling on remuneration under Sections II and III:
- (a) contribution to provident fund, superannuation fund or annuity fund, to the extent these, singly or together, are not taxable under the Income-tax Act, 1961;
- (b) gratuity payable at a rate not exceeding half a month's salary for each completed year of service; and
- (c) encashment of leave at the end of the tenure.

(2) In addition, an expatriate managerial person (including a non-resident Indian) is eligible for the following perquisites, which are not included in computing the ceiling on remuneration under Section II or III:
- (a) children's education allowance: for children studying in or outside India, an allowance limited to a maximum of Rs. 12,000 per month per child, or the actual expenses incurred, whichever is less. The allowance is admissible for a maximum of two children;
- (b) holiday passage for children studying outside India or family staying abroad: a return holiday passage once a year by economy class, or once in two years by first class, for children and family members from their place of study or stay abroad to India, if they are not residing in India with the managerial person;
- (c) leave travel concession: a return passage for self and family, in accordance with the company's rules, where the leave is to be spent in the home country instead of anywhere in India.

#### Section V — Remuneration payable to a managerial person in two companies
Subject to Sections I to IV, a managerial person may draw remuneration from one or both companies. The total remuneration drawn from the companies must not exceed the higher maximum limit admissible from any one of the companies of which he is a managerial person.

### PART III — Provisions applicable to Parts I and II
- (1) The appointments and remuneration referred to in Parts I and II are subject to approval by a resolution of the shareholders in general meeting.
- (2) The auditor or Secretary of the company must certify that the requirements of this Schedule have been complied with. Where the company is not required to appoint a Secretary, a Secretary in whole-time practice gives the certificate. The certificate is incorporated in the return filed with the Registrar under section 196(4).

### PART IV
The Central Government may, by notification, exempt any class or classes of companies from any of the requirements of this Schedule.

### Explanations [verify]
- Explanation I — "Effective capital" means the total of:
  - the paid-up share capital (excluding share application money or advances against shares);
  - the amount standing to the credit of the share premium account;
  - reserves and surplus (excluding revaluation reserve); and
  - long-term loans and deposits repayable after one year (excluding working capital loans, overdrafts, interest due on loans unless funded, bank guarantees, etc., and other short-term arrangements),

  as reduced by the total of:
  - any investments (except in the case of an investment company whose principal business is acquiring shares, stock, debentures or other securities);
  - accumulated losses; and
  - preliminary expenses not written off.
- Explanation II — Effective capital is calculated:
  - (a) where the managerial person is appointed in the year the company was incorporated, as on the date of appointment; and
  - (b) in any other case, as on the last date of the financial year preceding the financial year in which the managerial person is appointed.
- Explanation III — "Family" means the spouse, dependent children and dependent parents of the managerial person.
- Explanation IV — "Negative effective capital" means effective capital calculated under Explanation I that is less than zero.
- Explanation V — "Remuneration" means remuneration as defined in section 2(78), and includes reimbursement of any direct taxes to the managerial person.
- Explanation VI — Defines "current relevant profit", "crisis", "sick company" and "Special Economic Zone" for the purposes of this Schedule [verify].

## SCHEDULE VI
(See sections 55 and 186.) Infrastructural projects / infrastructural facilities [verify the complete list as amended].

"Infrastructural projects" or "infrastructural facilities" include the following projects or activities:
- (1) Transportation, including:
  - (a) roads, national highways, State highways, major district roads, other district roads and village roads, including toll roads, bridges, highways, road transport providers and other road-related services;
  - (b) rail systems, including rapid rail transport systems, light rail transit systems, monorail systems and other railway-related services;
  - (c) ports (including minor ports and harbours), inland waterways, coastal shipping (including shipyards, ships, tugs, etc.) and other port-related services;
  - (d) aviation, including airports, heliports, airlines, cargo terminals and other aviation-related services;
  - (e) logistics services, including cold chains, warehousing and container depots.
- (2) Agriculture, including:
  - (a) cold storage;
  - (b) construction of facilities on harvest processing of agricultural produce, including dairy, fisheries, etc.;
  - (c) construction of facilities for agricultural inputs, including fertilisers, pesticides, seeds, etc.
- (3) Water management, including:
  - (a) water supply and distribution;
  - (b) irrigation;
  - (c) water treatment.
- (4) Telecommunication, including:
  - basic or cellular service, including radio paging;
  - domestic satellite service (a satellite owned and operated by an Indian company for providing telecommunication service);
  - network of trunking, broadband network and internet services.
- (5) Industrial, commercial and social development and maintenance, including:
  - (a) real estate development, including an industrial park or special economic zone;
  - (b) tourism, including hotels, convention centres and entertainment centres;
  - (c) public markets and buildings, trade fair, convention, exhibition, cultural centres, sports and recreation infrastructure, public gardens and parks;
  - (d) construction of educational institutions and hospitals;
  - (e) landscaping, waste management (solid and liquid) and other urban development or infrastructure facilities.
- (6) Power, including:
  - (a) generation of power through thermal, hydro, nuclear, fossil fuels, wind and renewable energy;
  - (b) transmission, distribution or trading of power by laying a network of new transmission or distribution lines.
- (7) Petroleum and natural gas, including:
  - exploration and production;
  - import terminals;
  - liquefaction and re-gasification;
  - storage terminals; and
  - transmission networks and distribution networks, including laying of pipelines.
- (8) Housing, including urban and rural housing, including public housing, slum rehabilitation, etc.
- (9) Other infrastructural facilities of similar nature.

## SCHEDULE VII
(See section 135.) Activities which may be included by companies in their Corporate Social Responsibility Policies.

Activities relating to:
- (i) eradicating hunger, poverty and malnutrition, promoting health care (including preventive health care) and sanitation, and making available safe drinking water. This includes contribution to the Swach Bharat Kosh set up by the Central Government for the promotion of sanitation;
- (ii) promoting education, including special education and employment-enhancing vocational skills, especially among children, women, the elderly and the differently abled, and livelihood enhancement projects;
- (iii) promoting gender equality, empowering women, setting up homes and hostels for women and orphans, setting up old age homes, day care centres and other facilities for senior citizens, and measures for reducing inequalities faced by socially and economically backward groups;
- (iv) ensuring environmental sustainability, ecological balance, protection of flora and fauna, animal welfare, agroforestry, conservation of natural resources, and maintaining the quality of soil, air and water. This includes contribution to the Clean Ganga Fund set up by the Central Government for rejuvenation of the river Ganga;
- (v) protection of national heritage, art and culture, including restoration of buildings and sites of historical importance and works of art, setting up public libraries, and promotion and development of traditional art and handicrafts;
- (vi) measures for the benefit of:
  - armed forces veterans, war widows and their dependents; and
  - Central Armed Police Forces (CAPF) and Central Para Military Forces (CPMF) veterans and their dependents, including widows;
- (vii) training to promote rural sports, nationally recognised sports, paralympic sports and olympic sports;
- (viii) contribution to:
  - the Prime Minister's National Relief Fund;
  - the Prime Minister's Citizen Assistance and Relief in Emergency Situations Fund (PM CARES Fund) [verify: inserted 2020]; or
  - any other fund set up by the Central Government for socio-economic development and relief and welfare of the Scheduled Castes, the Scheduled Tribes, other backward classes, minorities and women;
- (ix) research and development contributions:
  - (a) contribution to incubators or research and development projects in the field of science, technology, engineering and medicine, funded by the Central Government, a State Government, a Public Sector Undertaking, or any agency of the Central Government or a State Government; and
  - (b) contributions to bodies engaged in conducting research in science, technology, engineering and medicine aimed at promoting Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs). These bodies are:
    - public funded Universities;
    - Indian Institutes of Technology (IITs);
    - National Laboratories and autonomous bodies established under the Department of Atomic Energy (DAE), the Department of Biotechnology (DBT), the Department of Science and Technology (DST), the Department of Pharmaceuticals, the Ministry of Ayurveda, Yoga and Naturopathy, Unani, Siddha and Homoeopathy (AYUSH), and the Ministry of Electronics and Information Technology; and
    - other bodies, namely the Defence Research and Development Organisation (DRDO), the Indian Council of Agricultural Research (ICAR), the Indian Council of Medical Research (ICMR) and the Council of Scientific and Industrial Research (CSIR);

  (Item (ix) as substituted in 2019 and amended in 2020 [verify].)
- (x) rural development projects;
- (xi) slum area development. Explanation: for this item, "slum area" means any area declared as such by the Central Government, any State Government or any other competent authority under any law for the time being in force;
- (xii) disaster management, including relief, rehabilitation and reconstruction activities. (Inserted 2019 [verify].)

[verify: whether any further item has been added to Schedule VII after 2022.]
